# The Sixth and Seventh Books of Moses

*or Moses’ Magical Spirit-Art, known as the wonderful arts of the old wise Hebrews, taken from the Mosaic books of the Kabbalah and the Talmud, for the good of mankind.*

*Translated from the German, word for word, according to old writings. With numerous engravings.*

**French edition** — translated from the English edition of 1880 (New York), itself derived from the German compilation published by Johann Scheible (Stuttgart, 1849). Public-domain text, digitized by Internet Archive.

> **Translator's note.**
> This text is a pseudepigraphic grimoire of the 19th century: it is neither of Mosaic nor of biblical origin. The present translation is provided for documentary and historical purposes.
> — The “Seals” and “Tables” are, in the original, **engraved plates** (fig. 1 to 23): circular seals mingling Hebrew characters and divine names. They cannot be rendered in text; only the figure numbers are indicated.
> — The 1880 OCR scan contains illegible passages; they are marked by **[…]**. The names of spirits and divine names (Adonaï, Agla, Tetragrammaton, etc.) are left as they stand, the spelling of 1880 being itself unsettled. The passages in Latin (often corrupt in the original itself) are kept in italics.
> — “N. N.” means *nomen nescio*: the operator inserts his own name there. “F. f. f.” abbreviates *Fiat, fiat, fiat* (“so be it,” three times).

---

## Preface (New York edition, 1880)

The first edition of this volume was both praised and censured by the public. It was admitted that it constituted a valuable collection of literary curiosities in general, and particularly of those touching upon magic; but it was also said that it was apt to feed superstition and, thereby, to promote evil — a repetition of the reproach made to the honorable Horst, the editor of a magical library.

In our enlightened age, the unprejudiced mind will see in the publication of such a work only what the author claims for it: a contribution of no small merit to the said literature and to its cultivation. As for the believer, a cheap edition will render him greater service than the formerly costly productions on sorcery, which circulated only in the form of extracts, sold at exorbitant prices. What other practical value the present edition may possess is not the question here. Let us not, then, underrate this branch of popular literature: its authors wrote according to a system which was — or at least seemed to them — clear; and illustrious personages, in all times and under all climes, did not consider the labor required to fathom the mysteries of magic to be labor lost; and although they condemned its form, they could not deny the possibility, nor even the fact, that gifted men, of a worth of their own, might accomplish such wonderful things.

Of the present edition only this can be said: the books called the Sixth and Seventh Books of Moses, which for several centuries have held the popular faith, are given here according to an old manuscript (the most legible among many), word for word, cleared only of the errors of spelling which the best interest of literature required to be corrected — with scrupulous fidelity. The publisher guarantees that not a single syllable has been added to it.

— New York, 1880.

---

# THE SIXTH BOOK OF MOSES

## Magia Alba et Nigra Universalis seu Necromantia

That is to say: that which embraces the whole of the White and Black Art (black magic), or the Necromancy of all the ministering Angels and Spirits; how to cite and to require the **nine Choirs** of the good angels and spirits — Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun, Venus, Mercury and Moon.

The most helpful angels are the following:

**SALATHEEL, MICHAEL, RAPHAEL, URIEL,**

with the Necromancy of the black magic of the best ministering Spirits in the *Chymia et Alchymia* of Moses and Aaron.

That which was hidden from David, the father of Solomon, by the high priest **SADOCK**, as the highest of mysteries, but which was at last found again in the year CCCXXX, among others, by the first Christian emperor, Constantine the Great, and sent to Pope Sylvester at Rome; then, after its translation under Julius II, *Pontifice maximo*, sent to the Emperor Charles V and highly recommended in the year MDXX. *[There follows in the original a corrupt Latin formula forbidding its printing under pain of excommunication: “Typis manabilis sub poena excommunicationis de numquam publica imprimendis… Fides.”]*

### Instruction

These two Books were revealed by God, the Almighty, to his faithful servant Moses, on Mount Sinai, *intervale lucis* [in an interval of light], and thus they came also into the hands of Aaron, of Caleb, of Joshua, and at last of David, of his son Solomon and of their high priest Sadock. Wherefore they are *Bibliis arcanum arcanorum*, which means: **the Mystery of all Mysteries**.

### The Conversation of God

Adonai, Sother, Emanuel, Ehie, Tetragrammaton, Ayscher, Jehova, Zebaoth, the Lord of Hosts, of Heaven and of Earth; that which belongs to the Sixth and Seventh Books of Moses, as follows:

“Adonai, EEl, Zebaoth, Jebaouha, Jehovah, EEl, Chad, Tetragrammaton Chaddai, Channaniah, al Elyon, Chaye, Ayscher, Adoyah Zawah, Tetragrammaton, Awiel, Adoyah, Chay, Yechal, Kanus, Emmet” — thus spake to me, Moses, the Lord of Hosts:

“Eheye, Ayscher, Jehel, Yazliah, Elion. *Sum qui sum ab aeterno in aeternum* [I am that I am, from eternity to eternity]. Thou, my servant Moses, open thine ears, hear the voice of thy God. Through me, Jehovah, Aglai, the God of heaven and of earth, thy race shall be multiplied and shall shine as the stars of heaven. Moreover I will also give thee might, power and wisdom to rule over the spirits of heaven and of hell — over the ministering angels and the spirits of the fourth element as well as of the seven planets. Hear yet again the voice of thy God, by which I give thee the seven seals and the twelve tables, Schem, Schel, Hamforasch, so that the angels and spirits may always render thee obedient service, when thou callest and citest them by these seven seals and these twelve tables of my omnipotence; and therewith thou shalt also have the knowledge of the highest mysteries.

Wherefore, thou, my faithful friend, dear Moses, take the might and the high authority of thy God.

Aclon, Ysheye, Channanyah, Yeschayah, EEl, Elijon, Rachmiel, Ariel, Eheye, Ayscher, Eheye, Elyon. Through my Seals and my Tables.”

---

## The First Mystery

*Sigillum Chori Servilium Archangelorum* — Seal of the Choir of the ministering Archangels.

### Conjuration

“I, N. N., a servant of God, require, call upon and conjure thee, OCH, by water, fire, air and earth, and by all that lives and moves therein, and by the most holy names of God: Agios, Tehirios, Perailitus, Alpha and Omega, Beginning and End, God and Man, Sabaoth, Adonai, Agla, Tetragrammaton, Emanuel, Abua, Ceus, Elion, Torna, Deus Salvator, Aramma, Messias, Clerob, Michael, Abreil, Achleof, Gachenas et Peraim Dei Patris, et Peraim Dei Filii, et Peraim Dei Spiritus Teti; and by the words by which Solomon and Manasseh, Cyprian and Agrippa conjured the spirits, and by all that may overcome thee: that thou render obedience to me, N. N., as Isaac did to Abraham, and appear before me, N. N., this instant, in the beautiful and mild human form of a young man, and bring me what I desire.” *(Which the conjurer must name.)*

### The Seal *(see fig. 2)*

The most useful ministering archangels of this seal are the following, with their Hebrew citation *verbis revelatis divinitus coactivis*: Uriel, Arael, Zacharael, Gabriel, Raphael, Theoska, Zywolech, Hemohon, Yhahel, Tuwahel, Donahau, Sywaro, Samohayl, Zowanus, Ruweno, Ymoeioh, Hahowel, Tywael.

**The Mystery of all Mysteries.** The great particular secret and the special use of this seal, likewise *ex Bibliis arcanorum Thora I*: if this Seal is buried where treasures lie, they will rise of themselves to the surface, without any presence being necessary, *in plena lunio* [at the full moon].

---

## The Second Mystery of the Seal

*The Name is True.* — Seal of the Choir of Hosts, *seu Dominatorium*, of the ministering Angels.

### Conjuration

“I, N. N., a servant of God, require, call upon and conjure thee, Spirit Phuel, by the holy Messengers and all the Disciples of the Lord, by the four holy Evangelists and the three holy Men of God, and by the most terrible and most holy words: Abriel, Fibriel, Zada, Zaday, Zarabo, Laragola, Lavaterium, Laroyol, Zay, Zagin, Labir, Lya, Adeo, Deus, Alon, Abay, Alos, Pieus, Ehos, Mibi, Uini, Mora, Zorad — and by these holy words: that thou come and appear before me, N. N., in a beautiful human form, and bring me what I desire.” *(Which the conjurer must name.)*

### The Seal *(see fig. 3)*

Of this Seal of the Choir of Dominations, or Hosts, the most useful are the following: Aha, Roah, Habu, Aromicha, Lemar, Patteny, Hamaya, Azoth, Hayozer, Karohel, Wezynna, Patecha, Tehom.

The special secret of this Seal is the following, *ex Thora Bibliis arcanorum, Sacra Scriptura*: if a man carries this Seal upon him, it will bring him great fortune and blessing; wherefore it is called the truest and highest **Seal of Fortune**.

---

## The Mystery of the Third Seal

Seal of the ministering Angels of the Throne, *ex Thora III Bibliis Arcanorum*.

### Conjuration

“I, N. N., a servant of God, require thee, call upon thee and conjure thee, Tehor, by all the holy Angels and Archangels, by the holy Michael, the holy Gabriel, Raphael, Uriel, the Thrones, Dominations, Principalities, Virtues, Cherubim and Seraphim; and with unceasing voice I cry: Holy, Holy, Holy is the Lord God of Sabaoth! — and by the most terrible words: Soab, Sother, Emmanuel, Hdon, Amathon, Mathay, Adonai, Eel, Eli, Eloy, Zoag, Dios, Anath, Tafa, Uabo, Tetragrammaton, Aglay, Josua, Jonas, Calpie, Calphas. Appear before me, N. N., in a mild and human form, and do what I desire.” *(Which the conjurer must name.)*

### The Seal *(see fig. 4)*

The ministering Angels of the Throne of this Seal are the following: Tehom, Haseha, Amarzyora, Schawayt, Chuscha, Zawar, Yahel, Labebor, Adoyahel, Schimuel, Achusaton, Schaddyl, Chamyel, Parymel, Chayo.

The special secret of this Throne is likewise *ex Thora III Bibliis arcanorum script.*: to carry this Seal upon oneself will make you very agreeable and much beloved, and will moreover undo all your enemies.

---

## The Fourth Seal — of the ministering Cherubim and Seraphim

With their characters.

### Conjuration

“I, N. N., a servant of God, call upon thee, require and conjure thee, O Spirit Anoch, by the wisdom of Solomon, by the obedience of Isaac, by the blessing of Abraham, by the piety of Jacob and of Noah, who sinned not before God, by the serpents of Moses, and by the twelve tribes, and by the most terrible words: Dallia, Dollia, Dollion, Corfuselas, Jazy, Agzy, Ahub, Tilli, Stago, Adoth, Sima, Eotath, Aloe, Jaoth, Dilu — and by all the words by which thou mayest be compelled: that thou appear before me in a beautiful human form, and give me what I desire.” *(Which the conjurer must name.)*

### The Seal *(see fig. 5)*

The most obliging ministering Cherubim and Seraphim of this Seal are the following, with their Hebrew call: Anoch, Sewachar, Chaylon, Esor, Yaron, Oseny, Yagelor, Ehym, Maakyel, Echad, Yalyon, Yagar, Ragat, Ymmat, Chabalym, Schadym.

The special secret of this Seal is the following, *Thora IVta Bibliis arcanorum*: to carry this Seal upon the body will preserve from all misery, and will give the greatest fortune and a long life.

---

## The Fifth Seal

Seal of the Angels of Power.

### Conjuration

“I, N. N., a servant of God, call upon thee, require and conjure thee, Spirit Scheol, by the most holy appearance in the flesh of Jesus Christ, by his most holy birth and circumcision, by his sweat of blood in the Garden, by the lashes of the scourge which he bore, by his bitter sufferings and death, by his Resurrection, his Ascension and the sending of the Holy Ghost the Comforter, and by the most dreadful words: Dai, Deorum, Ellas, genio Sophiel, Zophiel, Canoel, Elmiach, Richol, Hoamiach, Jerazol, Vohal, Daniel, Hasios, Tomaiach, Sannul, Damamiach, Sanul, Damabiath — and by these words by which thou mayest be overcome: that thou appear before me in a beautiful human form, and accomplish what I desire.” *(Which the conjurer must name.)*

### The Seal *(see fig. 6)*

The most helpful Angels of Power, with their *verbis hebraicis citationes divinae ex Thora Vta*, are the following: Schoel, Hael, Sephiroth, Thamy, Schamayl, Yeschah, Holyl, Yomelo, Hadiam, Mazbaz, Elohaym.

The special secret of this Seal is the following, *ex Thora V. D. B. A.*: if this Seal is laid upon a sick person, in full and true faith, it will restore him — if, *nota bene*, he has not lived out the full number of his days. Wherefore it is called the **Seal of Power**.

---

## The Sixth Mystery

The Seal of the Angels of Might, *seu Potestatum, ex Thora VI Bibliis arcanorum*, over the Angels and Spirits of all the Elements.

### Conjuration

“I, N. N., a servant of God, require, call upon and conjure thee, Spirit Alymon, by the most dreadful words: Sather, Ehomo, Geno, Poro, Jehovah, Elohim, Volnah, Deoach, Aloulam, Ophiel, Zophiel, Sophiel, Habriel, Eloha, Alesimus, Dileth, Melohim — and by all the most holy words by which thou mayest be overcome: that thou appear before me in a mild and beautiful human form, and accomplish what I command thee, as surely as God shall come to judge the living and the dead. Fiat, Fiat, Fiat.”

### The Seal *(see fig. 7)*

The most obedient Angels of Might, *seu Potestates*, with their divine citations in Hebrew words, are, for the four elements, the following: Schummyel, Alymon, Mupiel, Symnay, Semanglaf, Taftyah, Melech, Seolam, Waed, Sezah, Safyn, Kyptip, Taftyarohel, Aeburatiel, Anyam, Bymnam. Such is the mystery, or Seal, of the Angels of Strength.

The particular Arcanum of this Seal of the Mighty is the following, *ex Thora VIta Arcanorum sacra scriptura*: if a man carries this Seal to bed, he will learn what he desires to know through dreams and visions.

---

## The Seventh Secret Seal

Of the most obedient Angels, *Coeli Coelorum Legionum*, over the Angels of the Seven Planets and the Spirits.

### Conjuration

“I, N. N., a servant of God, call upon thee, require and conjure thee, Ahael, Banech, by the most holy words: Agios (Tetragrammaton), Eschiros, Adonai, Alpha and Omega, Raphael, Michael, Uriel, Schmaradiel, Zaday — and by all the known names of Almighty God, by whatsoever may compel thee, thou Ahael: that thou appear before me in a human form, and accomplish what I desire. Fiat, Fiat, Fiat.” *(Which the conjurer must name.)*

### The Seal *(see fig. 8)*

The most obedient Angels and Spirits of this Seal of the Seven Planets are the following: Ahaeb, Baneh, Yeschnath, Hoschiah, Betodah, Leykof, Yamdus, Zarenar, Sahon.

This Seal, laid upon the earth over treasure, or placed in the workings of a mine, will reveal all the precious contents of the mine. *Ex Thora VII Arcanorum.*

---

**End of the Sixth Book of Moses.**

# THE SEVENTH BOOK OF MOSES

## The First Table — the Spirits of the Air

*(see fig. 10)*

Text inscribed in the seal: *Jehovah — Father — Deus — … — Adonay — I conjure thee — Eloha, I cite thee — by Adonay — by Jehovah.*

To carry upon oneself the First Table of the Spirits of the Air — spirits as swift to help as thought — delivers the bearer from all necessity.

## The Second Table — the Spirits of Fire

*(see fig. 11)*

“Aha, I conjure thee, Aha, by Eheye; by Ihros, Eheye; by Agla, Aysch, Jehovah, I conjure thee: that thou appear unto me.”

Around the seal: *Eheyeh — Aha — … — Schvwym — Asmlh — Chartye — Jehovah — Veloheij — … — Schadey — […]*

## The Third Table — the Spirits of Water

*(see fig. 12)*

“I call upon and command thee, Chananya; I conjure thee, Yeschayah; by God Tetragrammaton, by Alpha and Omega, Eloh — and thou art thereby compelled by Adonaï.”

The Third Table brings great fortune by water, and its spirits furnish in abundance the treasures of the deep.

## The Fourth Table — the Spirits of the Earth

*(see fig. 13)*

“I, N. N., command thee, Aziel; I, N. N., a servant of God, conjure thee by Otheos, Athanatos, Agios, and by the names by which Aaron was cited and called […] Agios. Zebaoth.”

This Fourth Table will give the treasures of the earth if it is laid in the earth. Its spirits deliver the treasures of the earth at all times.

## The Fifth Table — Saturn

*(see fig. 14)*

“I, N. N., order, command and conjure thee, Sadol, by Hother; Vednij, by Sabaoth; […] by Helioren; Jaho, by the Truth Jehovah; Rawa, Alha: that ye appear before me in human form, as truly as Daniel overcame and confounded Baal. F. f. f.”

[…] of the Fifth Table of Saturn will serve in all things.

## The Sixth Table — Jupiter

*(see fig. 15)*

“I conjure thee […] by the celestial Alphabet […] and Tetragrammaton; Zeno, by the Paraclete; Obel, by Orlenius; Lima, by Agla: that ye obey, that ye appear before me and accomplish my desire — thus, in and through the name Elion […]. F. f. f.”

The Sixth Table of Jupiter helps to win lawsuits and to overcome quarrels and disputes; its spirits are at all times ready to lend assistance.

## The Seventh Table — the Spirits of Mars

*(see fig. 16)*

“N. N., I cite thee, Spirit Emol, by Deus Sachnaton; Luil, by Acumea; Ltriji, by Ambriel; Tijlaij, by Ehos; by Jeha, by Zora; Ajreh, by Awoth: that ye appear before me in a beautiful human form and accomplish my desire — thus, in truth, in and through the name which Aaron heard and which was prepared for him. F. f. f.”

The Seventh Seal of Mars brings good fortune in case of quarrels. The Spirits of Mars will come to thy aid.

## The Eighth Table — the Spirits of the Sun

*(see fig. 17)*

“I, N. N., conjure thee, Wijch, by Dalia; Jka, by Doluth; Auet, by Dilu; Veal, by Anub; Meho, by Igfa; Ymij, by Elon: that ye appear before me — as truly as Zebaoth, whom Moses named, and all the rivers of Egypt were turned into blood.”

The Eighth Table of the Spirits of the Sun helps to attain places of honor and wealth; its spirits also give gold and treasures.

## The Ninth Table — the Spirits of Venus

*(see fig. 18)*

“Beta, Kijmah, Yamb, Yheloruvesopijhael — I call upon thee, Spirit Awel, by God Tetragrammaton; Uhal, by Pomamiach: that ye obey my orders and accomplish my desires — thus, in truth, in and through the name Esercheije, which Moses named, and whereupon there fell such a hail as had not been known since the beginning of the world. F. f. f.”

The Ninth Table of the Spirits of Venus makes one beloved in every respect and makes secrets known through dreams. Its spirits also lend ample assistance in all kinds of affairs.

## The Tenth Table — the Spirits of Mercury

*(see fig. 19)*

“Petasa. Abor. Havaashar. N. N. cites thee, Spirit Yloij, by God, God Adonaij; Ymah, by God Tetragrammaton; Rawa, by God Emmanuel; Ahaij, by Athanatos: that thou appear before me — as truly as in and through the name of Adonaï, which Moses pronounced, and the locusts appeared. Fiat, fiat, fiat.”

The Tenth Table of the Spirits of Mercury gives wealth in chemistry [alchemy]. These spirits procure the treasures of the mine.

## The Eleventh Table — of the Spirits

*(see fig. 20)*

“I, N. N., cite thee, Spirit Yhaij, by El; Yvaij, by Elohim; Ileh, by Elho; Kijlij, by Zebaoth; Taijn, Iseij, by Tetragrammaton; Jeha, by Zadaij; Ahel, by Agla: that ye obey my orders — as truly as in and through the name Schemesumatie, which Joshua invoked, and the sun stood still in its course. Fiat, f. f. f.”

The Eleventh Table gives luck and fortune; its spirits give the treasures of the sea.

## The Twelfth Table — of the Schemhamforasch

*Over all the Spirits of the Magia Alba et Nigra.* *(see fig. 21)*

“I, N. N., cite and conjure thee, Spirit of the Schemhamforasch, by the seventy-two holy names of God: that thou appear before me and accomplish my desire — as truly as in and through the name Emmanuel, which the three young men, Schadrach, Meschach and Abed-Nego, sang in the fiery furnace from which they were delivered. F. f. f.”

This Twelfth Table, laid upon the Table or the Seal of the Spirits, compels them to appear forthwith and to serve in all things.

---

## The General Citation

*Nigromantia, seu Magia Alba et Nigra — translated ex Thora XXta Biol. Arcan.* *(see fig. 22)*

“Aba, Jehovah, Agla, Aschaij, Chad, Yah, Saddaij, Yedreh, Aschre, Noosedu, Zawa, Agla.” — *Here pronounce the names of the Angels of the Seal or of the Table, and their proper names.*

“Eheije, Ajscher, Eheije, Weatta, Eloheij, Harenij, Yechuateche, Hagedola, Merof, Zaroteij, Agla. Pedenij, Zije, Kotecha, Barach, Amijm, Gedolijm, Yerachena, Aleij, Weijazijloti, Mijkol, Zara, Umikol, Ra, Schadaij, Jehovah, Adonai, Zebaoth, Zah, Elohim, Yeasch, Jephila, Yaij, Bearechet, Vaij, Yomar, Ahaha, Elohim, Ascher, Hithalleij, Chuabotheij, Lepha, Yaij, Yehuel.”

Here make a short pause of prayer to God. Commit thyself to the will of Almighty God: He will guide thy undertaking to thy best interest. Then take up again in hand the Seal or the Table written upon parchment, and begin the above citation anew. If thy desire remains unfulfilled, continue thus:

“Hamneijs, Hakha, Elohim, Horro, Heotij, Meo, Dij, Adhaijijon, Hazze, Hamalach, Haggo, Elohij, Mijcol, Rhab, Yeba, Rech, Elhaneah, Tijmneik, Ea, Rebe, Hem, Schemne, Schembotaij, Yeischak, Yegid, Gulaooc, Kered, Haarez, Jeha.”

As the effects and apparitions will then follow, thy wishes are accomplished; if not, repeat the Citation *toties quoties* [as many times as necessary].

---

## The Ministering Formulas, or Mysteries

They are the following:

- **Astarte**, *Salamonis familiarum, III Regum* — Spirit of Water; Spirit of the Air; Spirit of the Earth.
- **Astoreth**, *in Palestina familiari* — Schaddaij, Driffon, Agrippa, Magaripp.
- **Aaijhtam, Sinna**, *familiarus, IV Buch Regum* — Schijwin, Aimeh, Chanije, Cijbor.
- **Baal-Berith**, *Judicum IX, XIII* — Adola, Eloheij, Umijchob, Channanijah.
- **Adramelech** of Sepharvaim, *familiaris* — Yhaij, Vvaij, Yles, Kijgij.
- **Nisroch**, familiar spirit of King Sennacherib of Assyria — Jehuel, Sarwiel, Urikon, Thoijil.
- **Asima**, *vironim Emath familiaris* — Barechel, Jomar, Ascher, Uwula.

These must be cited by means of the Twelfth Table, at the time of an eclipse of the Sun or of the Moon.

To the ministering Spirits of **Moloch** (*familiarum Ammonitarum*) corresponds the following Seal of generation, for all services, *caracteristico obedientiale*. *(see fig. 23)*

This Seal, at the time of the Citation, must be written upon parchment and held in the right hand. But it must not be read.

---

**End of the Seventh Book of Moses.**

---

# PART I — THE MAGIC OF THE ISRAELITES

The complete and trustworthy history of the human and the divine — the divine revelations and the influence of the men of God or of pious men — is to be found in the scriptural monuments of the ancient Hebrews, in the Holy Scriptures.

It is rightly that the Bible is called the Holy Scripture, for it contains the knowledge of the saints, while at the same time it unites and harmonizes word and deed, doctrine and action. It indicates the true relation of man to the Almighty; it offers the most direct reference to the great truths of the spiritual and the intellectual; it treats of the origin of the universe and of its laws, by which all things must be brought to light — of the earlier and later history of mankind, of its future destiny and of the means of attaining it; of the living and visible agents whom God employs in the great work of redemption, and finally of the most exalted of all beings — of the Savior of the world, who was in his own person a universal expression, and who manifested in a single person all divine power and all divine action, whereas all his precursors were endowed only with isolated powers and perfections; he who revealed to fallen man the highest and purest ends of his life, as well as the means of his purification and of his restoration.

We find among the oriental nations all the degrees of Magic — the stages necessary to resolve the mysteries of somnambulism and of second sight, and the infinitely multiplied operations by which unaccustomed events are produced; we find these things likewise among the Israelites, but of a totally different character. In the first case, it concerned the individual and his present; in the second, magic rested not upon the individual, but upon mankind in general and upon the great future. There, the light of man was made to shine by skillful actions, produced by the lowest arts; here shone forth a pure, cloudless, peaceful life, quickened by the warm breath of the Almighty — a light radiating toward the future, and upon this light depended all life and all action. To the Israelite seer was revealed not only the fate of isolated individuals, but the fate of nations, yea, of mankind, which at the end must be reconciled with God through the unfolding of the magical art, as often happened under the old covenant, through instinctive somnambulistic influences. If we examine the history of the old covenant, we shall find that this remarkable people stood solitary and alone, like a pillar of fire in the midst of heathen darkness.

Although we find among other nations worthy men, who seek the divine light, surrounded by darkness and uncertainty, here there are men of God, bearing the stamp of the true faith, who give indubitable proofs of higher powers by visible acts and signs which everywhere separate life from death and truth from error; and while the ancient remains of other nations show only theory without application, we find here a continuous chain of acts and events — in fact, a divine drama full of life. Of all these things the various books of the Holy Scriptures speak with assurance, so that the history of no other people, interwoven with fables, can be compared with them. Accordingly, the Bible contains the light which illumines every obscure phase of life; it is the foundation of all human actions, the star which guides from the earthly to the eternal — from the intellectual to the divine —, the aim and end of all knowledge. It is the first of the three great lights which guide and govern our faith, and it is beyond all comparison with the two other great lights: the square, which makes these actions lawful, or the circle, which makes perceptible the fixed limits of the condition of mankind. The Bible is also more instructive and richer, as regards our subject, than all other books put together. We shall therefore cite some principal points, both of the phenomena and of the mode of action and the theory relating to magnetism, and then we shall draw special attention to the healing of the sick according to the scriptural teachings. We shall give an account of dreams, a great number of which are recorded in the Bible.

## Dreams and Visions in Scripture

The dreams related in the Bible are numerous and remarkable. The voice by which God spoke to the prophets and to the men consecrated to him was generally made heard in dreams.

The visions of the ancients, according to the testimony of Moses, were almost always dreams. Numbers 12:6: “And he said, Hear now my words: If there be a prophet among you, I the LORD will make myself known unto him in a vision, and will speak unto him in a dream.” Job 33:15-16: “In a dream, in a vision of the night, when deep sleep falleth upon men, in slumberings upon the bed; then he openeth the ears of men, and sealeth their instruction.” 1 Kings 3:5: “In Gibeon the LORD appeared to Solomon in a dream by night: and God said, Ask what I shall give thee. And Solomon said, Thou hast shewed unto thy servant David my father great mercy, according as he walked before thee in truth, and in righteousness, and in uprightness of heart with thee; and thou hast kept for him this great kindness, etc.; give therefore thy servant an understanding heart to judge thy people, that I may discern between good and bad: for who is able to judge this thy so great a people?” Genesis 20:3: “But God came to Abimelech in a dream by night, and said to him, Behold, thou art but a dead man, for the woman which thou hast taken; for she is a man’s wife; and God said unto him in a dream, Yea, I know that thou didst this in the integrity of thy heart; for I also withheld thee from sinning against me.” Genesis 31:24: “And God came to Laban the Syrian in a dream by night, and said unto him, Take heed that thou speak not to Jacob either good or bad.” The dreams of Joseph concerning his brethren are likewise remarkable. Genesis 37:6: “And Joseph dreamed a dream, and he told it his brethren: and they hated him yet the more; and he said unto them, Hear, I pray you, this dream which I have dreamed: For, behold, we were binding sheaves in the field, and, lo, my sheaf arose, and also stood upright: and, behold, your sheaves stood round about, and made obeisance to my sheaf. And his brethren said to him, Shalt thou indeed reign over us? or shalt thou indeed have dominion over us?” And he dreamed yet another dream, and told it to his own and said: “Behold, I have dreamed a dream more; and, behold, the sun and the moon and the stars made obeisance to me. And his father rebuked him, and said unto him, What is this dream that thou hast dreamed? Shall I and thy mother and thy brethren indeed come to bow down ourselves to thee to the earth?”

Sacred history proves that after Joseph had been sold by his brethren to the Egyptian merchants, he did indeed attain […] to the court of Pharaoh. Joseph’s power of interpreting dreams was first manifested by the interpretation of the dreams of the butler and the baker while he was in prison, Genesis 40; likewise also by that of the dreams of King Pharaoh, of the seven fat and lean kine which came up out of the water, and of the seven full and good ears and the seven thin and withered ears, Genesis 41:1. In the New Testament other dreams are mentioned by which God willed to speak to his faithful. It was in this manner that Joseph, the husband of Mary, was warned by an angel (Matthew 1:20) that she would conceive of the Holy Ghost and bring forth a son who would become the Savior of the world; then again, that he should take the child and flee into Egypt to escape the murderous design of Herod; and after the death of Herod, that he should return to Nazareth (Matthew 2:12-19). In the same manner, the three wise men from the East were warned in a dream not to return to Herod, but to go back to their own country by another way. The Apostles frequently had visions in the night. Thus, for example, Paul was commanded to go into Macedonia (Acts 16:9). And in the same book of the New Testament (18:9), we read: “Then spake the Lord to Paul in the night by a vision, Be not afraid, but speak, and hold not thy peace.” Many similar passages could be cited, for example 23:11; 27:23, etc., etc.
## The Account of the Creation

Let us begin with the history of the creation, as Moses recorded it. “In the beginning God created the heaven and the earth.”

Therein lies the great first cause. God is an uncreated being — heaven and earth are the first created things; the world is the first thing that was made of God. As to a second antithesis, Moses speaks of light and darkness: “And darkness was upon the face of the earth, and God said, Let there be light: and there was light.” Here again it is a question of light as a creation, which nevertheless had darkness for its opposite; for this reason the ancient Egyptians regarded darkness as the beginning of all things. Even according to the writings of Moses, such was the Egyptian doctrine, for he says: “Darkness was upon the face of the deep.” But if the Egyptian doctrine, in its first conception, may be regarded as good authority, their error arose in this, that they admitted darkness before light; likewise the similar error of the Persians, in that they admitted that light was created before darkness, the one before the other. […], as Moses clearly says: “And God divided the light from the darkness; and God called the light Day, and the darkness he called Night.”

The Bible points out also another antithesis in the first germs and figures of the earth, namely water and spirit — water as the matter and germ of the figurative, and the spirit, the Elohim, as the active and fructifying principle: “And the Spirit of God moved upon the face of the waters.” One-sided views led the first philosophers into many errors. Thus Thales made everything proceed from water and failed to recognize the spiritual and active whole, in which he was imitated by the later champions of materialism. The other one-sided view consists in holding everything to be spiritual and in regarding matter only as a vain abstraction (*caput mortuum* [dead head]), which was the case, from the most ancient period of time, among the spiritualists and the defenders of spiritualism. Moses therefore shows his superiority over all the disciples of the ancient teachings […], as well as over the more modern sects, in that he was enlightened by divine wisdom and represented each subject not in a partial manner, but in its true scope and its true significance: he assigned to matter its just value and placed the spiritual beside it. Moreover, Moses gave the account of the creation in beautiful and captivating language; as for example concerning the waters: the difference between the moist and the dry, and how the dry land came forth from the water; how the grass and the herb yielding seed, and the fruit trees, grew upon the earth; how the immense deep was filled with living and moving creatures, together with the fowl that fly in the firmament of heaven; how the earth at last brought forth living creatures, each after its kind, and lastly, how God made man “in his own image,” to whom he gave “dominion over the fish of the sea, over the fowl of the air, over the cattle, and over every creeping thing that creepeth upon the earth.”

## The Celestial Adam and the Fall — Extract from the MAGIKON

The Mosaic Eden was the habitation of the beings originally created in purity, within whose bounds grew the tree of the knowledge of good and evil. The figure of the serpent shows the nature of the fall of man. We have spoken, in another part of this work, of the original purity and wisdom of the men of God. This is the place to record other principles of this kind. To this end we make use of a mystical and interesting manuscript from which we extract the following: (MAGIKON, or the Secret System of a Society of Unknown Philosophers, etc., 2 vols., Frankfort and Leipzig, 1784. A rare book, whose teachings agree, in many respects, with the doctrine of the Brahmins of India.)

By reason of his divine origin, as an unconditioned emanation of God, Adam was not only the noblest being who, as an expression of the divine power, had the preference over all others, for he never owed his existence to a mother; he was a celestial Adam, brought into being by God himself, and he was not born into the world in the ordinary manner. He also enjoyed, in consequence of his nature, all the prerogatives of a pure spirit, surrounded by an invulnerable veil. But this was not the sensual body of the present time, which is but a proof of his degradation — a coarse mantle — by which he sought to protect himself against the unbridled elements. His garments were holy, simple, indestructible and of an indissoluble character. In this condition of perfect glory, in which he enjoyed the purest happiness, he was destined to reveal the power of the Almighty and to rule over the visible and the invisible. Being in possession of all the prerogatives and all the insignia of a king, he could also use every means to accomplish his high destiny. Champion of unity, he was safe from the attacks of all enemies within and without, because the veil with which he was covered (and the germs of which are still in us) rendered him invulnerable. One of the advantages possessed by the original pure man was that no natural poison, nor all the powers of the elements, could harm him. Christ promised invulnerability to his apostles and to all his disciples, through the regeneration of man. In this condition man also bears a fiery sword, two-edged, piercing all things — a living word which unites in itself all power, and by which “all things are possible to him.” Of this sword Moses says, Genesis 3, 24: “So he drove out the man; and he placed at the east of the garden of Eden Cherubims, and a flaming sword which turned every way, to keep the way of the tree of life.” In Revelation 1, 16, we read: “And out of his mouth went a sharp twoedged sword,” etc. By this sword we are to understand the living word, which was originally inherent in man, and which can be restored to him only by his return to a pure state, and when he shall have been washed from the defilement of sensualism. It is the word of which we read in Hebrews 4, 12: “For the word of God is quick, and powerful, and sharper than any twoedged sword, piercing even to the dividing asunder of soul and spirit.”

Altogether extraordinary is the mighty Honover (word of power) of Zoroaster, which conquers all and corresponds fully with the foregoing, and by which Ormuzd vanquished Ahriman and all evil. “In this condition of royal honor and power,” we read further, “man, the most living image of his father (whose vice-regent he was upon the earth), might have enjoyed the purest happiness had he properly kept his Eden; but he committed a breach of trust. Instead of ruling over the things of sense and striving toward a higher spiritual state, to which he was ordained, he became imbued with the unhappy idea of exchanging the great cardinal points of light with truth, that is to say, he confounded light with truth, and in this confusion he lost both the one and the other and hid himself from himself. Because he lost sight of the boundary of the kingdom over which he was placed as sentinel, and confined himself to a single one of its portions, namely the sensual, whose brilliance so dazzled him that it made him forget all the rest; because he flattered himself that he could find light in another place than the first great source, he fixed his voluptuous eye upon a false existence, became enamored of sensualism and became sensual himself. By this adultery he sank into darkness and confusion, the result of which was that he was transplanted from the light of day into the night of innumerable little twinkling stars, and he then experienced a sensual nakedness of which he was ashamed. The abuse of the knowledge of the bond between the spiritual and the corporeal, according to which man strives to render the spiritual sensual and the sensual spiritual, is the true adultery, of which that which is moved by the female sex is merely a consequence and an imitator. Through sin man not only lost his original habitation and became an exile in the religious state of fathers and mothers, compelled to go by the way of the flesh, but he also lost the fiery sword, and with it all that had made him seeing in all things and invincible. His holy garments then became like the skin of animals, and this mortal and perishable covering afforded him no protection against the elements. With the devastated half of his body, the spiritual also added to the confusion, and discordant sounds were heard in the dark places of his spiritual domain.”

Although man had sunk deeply into sin, the hope of a full restoration was given him, on the condition of a perfect reconciliation. Without such a reconciliation, however, he sinks ever deeper, and his return becomes still more uncertain. To be reconciled, he must abase himself and resist the false seductions which serve only to plunge him into the mire of the elements, and he must seek, by prayer, to obtain the higher blessings of benevolent influences, without which he cannot breathe a pure breath. In this reconciliation he must gradually overcome everything and put away from himself all that would darken his inner nature and separate him from the great source of his being; for he can never enjoy peace within himself and with the nature that surrounds him until he has thus conquered all that is opposed to his own nature, and won the victory over all his enemies. But this can be done only if he retraces the same path by which he went astray. He must wean himself by degrees from the sensual by a heroic life and, like a weary traveler with bruised feet, who has many steep mountains to climb, continue to ascend until he reaches the goal, which is lost in the clouds. Overcoming one obstacle after another, he must dispel the dark vapors which interpose themselves between him and the true sun, so that at last the pure rays of light may reach him without interruption. What follows is an authentic scriptural doctrine according to the Indian creed, differing however in character:

“The Almighty has provided means to aid man in the work of reconciliation. God has established higher agents to bring him back to Himself from the error of his ways. But he can be fully restored only by the Savior of the world, who completed and perfected all that these agents had accomplished only in part, at different periods. Through Him all power was animated and exalted; through Him he approaches the first and only true light, the knowledge of all things, and above all the knowledge of himself. If man is disposed to accept this offered help, he will surely reach the desired goal, and he will be so firmly established in the faith that no future doubt can ever make him waver. If he raises his will so as to bring it into unison with the divine will, he can spiritualize his being even in this world, so that the higher spiritual kingdom becomes visible to his eyes, and he feels God nearer to him than he had ever believed possible; that all things become possible to him, because he adds all powers to his own, and in this union and this harmony, with the fullness of a higher vitality, the divine agents, Moses, Elijah, yea, Christ himself, may become visible to him, when, living in the midst of thought, he no longer has need of books. In a word, man may attain such a degree of perfection, even in this life, that death will have nothing else to do than to strip him of his coarse covering to reveal his spiritual temple, because he then lives and moves in the eternal. It is only when he arrives at the end of this valley of darkness that he will receive, at each stage of his journey, a more extended life, a greater inner power, a purer air and a wider horizon. His spiritual being will taste nobler fruits, and at the end of his course nothing will be able to separate him from the sublime harmonies of those spheres, of which mortal sense can trace only a faint image. Without distinction of sex, he will begin to live the life of the angels and will possess all their powers, of which he had here below only a faint token; he will then enjoy anew the incense of the eternal temple, the source of all power, from which he was exiled, and Christ will be his great High Priest (Hebrews 7, 17, 24, 25). Man will not only enjoy his own gifts, but he will share in the gifts of the elect, who constitute the council of the wise; that holy sovereign will there be more exalted than he could be here below; there will be there neither rising nor setting of the light of the stars, nor alternation of day and night, nor multiplicity of tongues; every being will at that moment be rendered capable of reading the name of that holy book from which life flows for every creature (Hebrews 12, 22, 23).” And here again the views of Zoroaster agree with the foregoing, for he too speaks of a celestial assembly, and of the participation of every faithful follower of Ormuzd in the sacrifices and prayers of all, etc.

In placing this eminent treatise so frankly before the eyes of the reader, I have felt no hesitation, because it was so clear and so true, because it seemed so well suited to this work, and in order to show why only pure and truly Christian men can perform great wonders and see visions of which worldly minds have not even the conception. I shall now relate some examples of apparitions and magnetic events, many of which are recorded in the Bible.

## Examples of Magnetic Events in the Bible

The first and most striking we find in connection with Adam. Moses writes (Genesis 2, 21) as follows: “And the Lord God caused a deep sleep to fall upon Adam, and he slept.” And now the question arises: what kind of sleep was this? The answer is: it was a deep sleep. Was it the sleep of death, or a state of lethargy, or an ecstasy (*raptus divinus* [divine rapture]), or was it merely an ordinary sleep? The first hypothesis appears improbable; but even had it been the case, we know that in the loosening of the body from its earthly bonds, and shortly before death, the clearest examples of second sight have occurred; but there is no question here of a mortal illness, but of a deep sleep. If it was an ecstasy, then this inner second sight may be regarded as all the more probable. The seventy-two translators of the Bible do indeed regard this sleep as an ecstasy, and Tertullian says, referring directly to it: “The operative power of the prophecies of the Holy Spirit fell upon him.” (*Accidit super illum spiritus sancti vis operatrix prophetiae.*)

Another remarkable vision is that which Noah had of the ark, long before the flood came. Likewise the calling of Abraham, in which he was commanded to leave his native land and to journey toward Haran, in Canaan. Abraham had many visions — or was the conversation of the Lord with him, recorded in the Bible, really nothing more than a figurative expression of intuition? By these visions or conversations, as one pleases, he was taught that he would be greatly blessed and that he would be the father of a great nation, etc. As he entered the sacred grove of Moriah, the Lord appeared again to Abraham and said: “Unto thy seed will I give this land.”

The innocent life of the shepherds, and their frequent sojourn in the sacred groves, quite naturally carried this intuition to the highest point of perfection, and this was especially the case when their minds were occupied with God and divine things. And this shows itself particularly in the history of the pastoral life of the pious Israelites, not only among the ancient patriarchs, but also later, in the time of the kings and judges. Isaac and Jacob had visions similar to those of Abraham. We notice especially the vision of Jacob during his journey to Mesopotamia, in which he saw a ladder reaching from the earth to heaven. It is written (Genesis 28, 10): “And Jacob went out from Beersheba, and went toward Haran. And he lighted upon a certain place, and tarried there all night, because the sun was set; and he took of the stones of that place, and put them for his pillows, and lay down in that place to sleep. And he dreamed, and behold a ladder set up on the earth, and the top of it reached to heaven: and behold the angels of God ascending and descending on it. And, behold, the Lord stood above it, and said, I am the Lord, etc. The land whereon thou liest, to thee will I give it, and to thy seed, etc. And in thee and in thy seed shall all the families of the earth be blessed. And Jacob awaked out of his sleep, and he said, Surely the Lord is in this place; and I knew it not! This is none other but the house of God, and this is the gate of heaven.” How truly has the dream of Jacob been fulfilled! The promised land became the possession of the Jews; through his seed, that is to say, through Christ the Savior, who is the heavenly ladder by which the angels of God ascended and descended, all the nations of the earth have been or shall be blessed.

We find another remarkable example of magnetic influence modifying the nature and appearance of living objects, in the history of Jacob. It is this: Jacob agreed with Laban that he would continue to keep his sheep, on condition that Laban should give him, as a reward for his service, all the spotted lambs and kids that should in future be added to his flocks. Laban consented to this proposal, and Jacob became immensely rich. It is worth while to insert the passage relating to this transaction, as an application of the mysterious doctrine of magnetism.

When Jacob would no longer watch over the sheep and desired to depart with his wives and his children, Laban said to him, Genesis 30, 27-43: “I pray thee, if I have found favour in thine eyes, tarry: for I have learned by experience that the Lord hath blessed me for thy sake. And he said, Appoint me thy wages, and I will give it. And he said unto him, Thou knowest how I have served thee, and how thy cattle was with me. For it was little which thou hadst before I came, and it is now increased unto a multitude; and the Lord hath blessed thee since my coming: and now when shall I provide for mine own house also? And he said, What shall I give thee? And Jacob said, Thou shalt not give me any thing: if thou wilt do this thing for me, I will again feed and keep thy flock: I will pass through all thy flock to day, removing from thence all the speckled and spotted cattle, and all the brown cattle among the sheep, and the spotted and speckled among the goats: and of such shall be my hire. So shall my righteousness answer for me in time to come, when it shall come for my hire before thy face: every one that is not speckled and spotted among the goats, and brown among the sheep, that shall be counted stolen with me. And Laban said, Behold, I would it might be according to thy word. And he removed that day the he goats that were ringstraked and spotted, and all the she goats that were speckled and spotted, and every one that had some white in it, and all the brown among the sheep, and gave them into the hand of his sons. And he set three days’ journey betwixt himself and Jacob: and Jacob fed the rest of Laban’s flocks. And Jacob took him rods of green poplar, and of the hazel and chesnut tree; and pilled white strakes in them, and made the white appear which was in the rods. And he set the rods which he had pilled before the flocks in the gutters in the watering troughs when the flocks came to drink, that they should conceive when they came to drink. And the flocks conceived before the rods, and brought forth cattle ringstraked, speckled, and spotted. And Jacob did separate the lambs, and set the faces of the flocks toward the ringstraked, and all the brown in the flock of Laban; and he put his own flocks by themselves, and put them not unto Laban’s cattle. And it came to pass, whensoever the stronger cattle did conceive, that Jacob laid the rods before the eyes of the cattle in the gutters, that they might conceive among the rods. But when the cattle were feeble, he put them not in: so the feebler were Laban’s, and the stronger Jacob’s. And the man increased exceedingly, and had much cattle, and maidservants, and menservants, and camels, and asses.”

This clearly proves that the sheep and goats could be made to bring forth young changed in color and appearance, in correspondence with the pilled rods which Jacob placed before them while they drank the water. In our day, the theory that the features of the child of a human mother can be affected by an object upon which the mother fixes her gaze is declared absurd; and yet this theory, in the very nature of things, is as firmly established as the fact that the mental qualities of many children differ entirely from those of their parents. The fact that the sheep and goats, at the sight of the objects which Jacob so skillfully placed before them, brought forth young differing in appearance from themselves, has a very profound significance. Either Jacob knew by experience what the result of this stratagem would be, or it was revealed to him in a dream, for we read, Genesis 31, 10: “And it came to pass at the time that the cattle conceived, that I lifted up mine eyes, and saw in a dream, and, behold, the rams which leaped upon the cattle were ringstraked, speckled, and grisled.” With the water they drank, and in which they saw at the same time their own reflection, they transmitted to their young the image of the spotted rods.

We have not space here to enter into a more extended argument to prove the truth of this phenomenon; but the fact that the mother, both human and animal, is capable, during the period of gestation, of transmitting to her offspring the image and likeness of surrounding objects, has a surer foundation than is commonly believed possible. The great army of materialists, who represent the spirit of the Scriptures and of life as ordinary earthly matter, in order to make it believed that nothing is hidden in the sanctuary which their intellect cannot comprehend, will never be converted; and those who rely upon the benign influences of a higher light in the temple, which will endure beyond the life of this world, will never need conversion.

## Moses, the Man of God

Moses himself, the great man of God, had many remarkable visions. These visions consisted partly of dreams and partly of ecstasies, and it is for this reason that he was instructed in all the mysteries of the Egyptians and in all their magical arts, in which he surpassed all others. On account of his extraordinary piety and wisdom, he was made the savior of his people, delivering them from the bondage of Pharaoh. His visions were of a varied character. His ability to lead and govern the people was the direct result of a profound intuition. If we regard this ability as a mere inner sight, then we must admit that it was a purely magical gift; if we regard it as the result of the direct command of the voice of God (for, according to the Scriptures, God often spoke in person with Moses), we find therein a confirmation of the truth that a pious mind, open to divine influences, can also perform divine acts.

The first important vision of Moses occurred at Mount Horeb, while he was still occupied in keeping the flocks of Jethro, his father-in-law (Exodus 3, 2): “And the angel of the Lord appeared unto him in a flame of fire out of the midst of a bush: and Moses said, I will now turn aside, and see this great sight, why the bush is not burnt. And when the Lord saw that he turned aside to see, God called unto him out of the midst of the bush, and said, Draw not nigh hither: put off thy shoes from off thy feet, for the place whereon thou standest is holy ground.”

Moses, the prophetic seer, knowing the weakness of his brethren, full of religious zeal and endowed with an ardent *phantasia* [imagination], came to the dwelling of his father-in-law in Midian, where he had time and opportunity, as a shepherd, to nourish his mind with religious contemplations, so that, in a state of ecstatic second sight, he could pass in review the ways and means by which he might become the leader and shepherd of his people. The *centrum* of his mind was open to the higher influences of God, who appeared to him as a light in the burning bush which was not consumed, and with whom, his face veiled, he conversed familiarly. We find in Moses the emotions of an inner psychological struggle, made up of hopes and fears, of extreme weakness and supernatural strength of will; of submission, reverence and obedience; of confidence, and finally of an enthusiasm such that, regarding all earthly obstacles as nothing, he triumphed over all things. While he was thus armed with well-nigh divine powers, he subdued the elements of nature and compelled them to bear witness to the greatness and glory of God by the marvelous wonders he performed. In such ecstasies Moses could remain long upon the mountains and separate himself from the people during the journey in the wilderness, while nevertheless being venerated as a man of wonders. The visions of Moses embraced the present and the future. Not only did he transmit the commandments of God from the top of the mountain, but he also foresaw the offerings that were brought to the golden calf; he foresaw that he could prepare the children of Israel for the pure worship of God, and keep them from error and idolatry, only by isolating them in the wilderness from the heathen nations that surrounded them. Besides the above indications, it suffices for us to call attention to the particular visions of Moses, to his gifts, to his ability to transfer to others the power of divination, and to rank them among magnetic events; we may omit the different kinds of sacrifices, the consecration and blessing by water, oil and blood, the laying on of hands, etc., as well as the rigorous prohibitions against taking any part in witchcraft, false divination, conjurations and the interrogation of the dead.

Among some examples resembling magnetism, we mention particularly the rod with which Moses performed his wonders before Pharaoh, and the stretching forth of his hands by which he divided the waters of the sea (Exodus 14, 16): “But lift thou up thy rod, and stretch out thine hand over the sea, and divide it; and as Moses stretched out his hand over the sea, the waters were divided. And when the Egyptians pursued them, Moses stretched forth his hand over the sea, and the sea returned to his strength when the morning appeared; and the Egyptians fled against it, and the Lord overthrew the Egyptians in the midst of the sea, so that there remained not so much as one of them.” The stretching forth of the hand of Moses and the wonders he performed with his rod are of great significance. With his rod he smote the rock at Rephidim, and water gushed forth to quench the thirst of the murmuring people (Exodus 17, 5): “And the Lord said unto Moses, Go on before the people, and take with thee of the elders of Israel; and thy rod, wherewith thou smotest the river, take in thine hand, and go; and thou shalt smite the rock, and there shall come water out of it, that the people may drink.” And when Amalek came and fought against Israel, Moses said unto Joshua (Exodus 17, 9-11): “Choose us out men, and go out, fight with Amalek: to morrow I will stand on the top of the hill with the rod of God in mine hand. And it came to pass, when Moses held up his hand, that Israel prevailed; and when he let down his hand, Amalek prevailed.”

The gift of prophecy seems also to have been given to the pious elders of Israel through their intercourse with Moses, for it is written (Numbers 11, 23-29): “And the Lord said unto Moses, Is the Lord’s hand waxed short? thou shalt see now whether my word shall come to pass unto thee or not. And Moses went out, and told the people the words of the Lord, and gathered the seventy men of the elders of the people, and set them round about the tabernacle. And the Lord came down in a cloud, and spake unto him, and took of the spirit that was upon him, and gave it unto the seventy elders: and it came to pass, that, when the spirit rested upon them, they prophesied, and did not cease. But there remained two of the men in the camp, the name of the one was Eldad, and the name of the other Medad: and the spirit rested upon them; and they were of them that were written, but went not out unto the tabernacle: and they prophesied in the camp. And Joshua the son of Nun, the servant of Moses, one of his young men, answered and said, My lord Moses, forbid them. And Moses said unto him, Enviest thou for my sake? would God that all the Lord’s people were prophets, and that the Lord would put his spirit upon them!”

The various conditions of clairvoyance are clearly described by Moses. Miriam and Aaron spoke against Moses because of the Ethiopian woman whom he had married, and they said (Numbers 12, 2-8): “Hath the Lord indeed spoken only by Moses? hath he not spoken also by us? And the Lord heard it. And the Lord came down in the pillar of the cloud, and called Aaron and Miriam: and they both came forth. And he said, Hear now my words: If there be a prophet among you, I the Lord will make myself known unto him in a vision, and will speak unto him in a dream. My servant Moses is not so, who is faithful in all mine house. With him will I speak mouth to mouth, even apparently, and not in dark speeches; and the similitude of the Lord shall he behold.” And so it was among the Israelites and the other nations, and so it is still in our magnetic apparitions and our revelations by visions, and especially in dreams and in dark speeches and figures, which is frequently the case in the lower condition of somnolence; but in the highest degrees of clairvoyance, when the mind is pure as in the case of Moses, it is beholding in the true form.

The personal conversations of God with Moses, and his power of beholding the Almighty in his true likeness, are figurative expressions and are not to be taken in the literal sense. For the Lord speaks by revelation and by means of light, and not mouth to mouth; and God cannot be seen by mortal eyes, for he says in another place: “There shall no man see me, and live.” This language is the expression or the impression of the divine word, and a light issuing from the purest source; it is the spiritual gift and the revelation of the Deity to man, which must be received according to the various degrees of intelligence of beings — as in nature, according to the kind of light produced by different actions, according as the effect is produced upon objects near or distant, thick or thin, hard or soft, etc.

This language was understood by the prophets and the consecrated men of all ages, and these could communicate the light they had received in no other language than those spoken in their time, although what came upon them was much simpler, more comprehensive and more spiritual than any spoken communication could have been. The language of God is the influence of a higher light by which the mind it penetrates becomes as if electrified. God, as *centrum*, acts only upon the *centrum* of things, that is to say, upon the inner or the spiritual, and the outward manifestations follow *ex post* [after the fact]. It is not less significant that the bite of the fiery serpents was healed by looking upon the brazen serpent: “And the soul of the people was much discouraged because of the way, and the people spake against God, and against Moses. And the Lord sent fiery serpents among the people, and they bit the people; and much people of Israel died. Therefore the people came to Moses, and said, We have sinned, for we have spoken against the Lord, and against thee; pray unto the Lord, that he take away the serpents from us. And Moses prayed for the people. And the Lord said unto Moses, Make thee a fiery serpent, and set it upon a pole: and it shall come to pass, that every one that is bitten, when he looketh upon it, shall live.” (Numbers 21, 4-9.)

## Balaam, the Heathen Soothsayer

The visions and prophecies of Balaam, the son of Beor, to whom Balak sent messengers that he might curse Israel, are also of a remarkable character. (Numbers 22.) “If Balak would give me his house full of silver and gold, I cannot go beyond the word of the Lord my God, to do less or more.” Thus spoke Balaam to Balak, who sought to bribe him to do evil.

In Numbers 24, 4, 15, 16, 17, 19, we have the account of the visions of the heathen seer, in which the coming of Christ was announced: “And the spirit of the Lord came upon him, and he took up his parable and said: Thus saith the man whose eyes are open: thus saith he who heard the words of God, and knew the knowledge of the Most High, who saw the vision of the Almighty, falling into a trance, but having his eyes open: I shall see him, but not now; I shall behold him, but not nigh; there shall come a Star out of Jacob, and a Sceptre shall rise out of Israel. Out of Jacob shall come he that shall have dominion.” The history of Balaam proves that the power of working wonders was not possessed by the holy seers alone. Balak, king of the Moabites, being afraid of the Israelites, desired to form a league with the Midianites. But as neither the Moabites nor the Midianites felt disposed to enter into hostilities with the Israelites, they had recourse to magic, and as they had no magician among them, they sent for Balaam, who was famed for his powers as a charmer and soothsayer. The messengers came to Balaam with precious gifts in their hands (for he took payment for his services as a soothsayer), and demanded that he should curse this strange people. Balaam invited them to tarry the night; in the morning he rose and made known to the messengers that God permitted him neither to curse the Israelites nor to accompany them into their country, for “this people was favored of God.” Balak, thinking that he had not offered enough, sent gifts still more precious by the hands of his nobles, in order to induce Balaam to visit him and to curse Israel. Balaam, a mixture of faith and inconstancy, of truth and avarice, of true prophecy and magic, said to the servants of Balak: “If Balak would give me his house full of silver and gold, I cannot go beyond the word of the Lord my God, to do less or more.” And yet, after having spoken with the Lord during the night, he rose in the morning, saddled his ass and prepared to depart with the Moabite princes; and he afterwards taught the enemies of Israel how they might draw it into idolatry.

Here follows the history of Balaam’s perfect somnambulism. Being a visionary, he was divided within himself, because he tried to serve God and Mammon. His conscience reproached him. “And God’s anger was kindled because he went: and the angel of the Lord stood in the way for an adversary against him.” Then he turned his inner perception away from the angel and transferred it to the ass, which now likewise perceived the angel standing in the way, and thereupon entered into a reasonable conversation with her rider. The ass, with the obstinacy peculiar to her kind, preferred the fields to the uneven paths of the vineyards, and when force was used to bring her back into the way, she thrust herself against the wall and crushed Balaam’s foot against the wall, for which he smote her with his staff; and as there was no path to turn aside either to the right or to the left, the ass fell down under Balaam, and he smote her again. At last the ass spoke to Balaam and showed him his unreasonable conduct, and when he came to himself, he saw the angel again instead of the ass; but his conscience smote him; he confessed his sin and promised to return. But the angel permitted him to go on, on condition that he should say only what the Lord had commanded him to say, a condition which he fulfilled in spite of all the temptations that Balak could offer him; “and he went not, as at other times, to seek for enchantments, but he set his face toward the wilderness.” Instead of cursing the Israelites, he blessed them, and then actually prophesied concerning the Star of Jacob. (Numbers 24.)

This spurious prophet had no truly divine inspirations, but he prophesied in the same manner as our mesmeric clairvoyants do. For, in the first place, he always withdrew apart when he was about to prophesy, in order to avoid outward disturbances, which no true prophet ever did. Secondly, his inner perceptions were opened by the closing of his outer senses. “Thus saith he who beareth the word, whose eyes shall be opened when he falleth down.” It is evident that the angel with the drawn sword was a vision, and the fact that the speaking ass did not appear strange to him proves clearly that he could not have been awake. According to the Arabic, Balaam was called “the man with the closed eye,” and this leads Tholuck to compare his condition to a state of magnetic ecstasy. Thirdly, Balaam was so incapable of distinguishing between the real object and the apparent subject, that the ass endowed with speech made no impression upon him, so that, after having recovered his senses, he saw the angel standing before him and fell down before him. Fourthly, Balaam made use of certain outward means to throw himself into an ecstatic state, which the true prophets never did. He was led from place to place in order to obtain visions of different natures, so as to make them accord with the good pleasure of Balak. He even employed magic, for it is written: “And when Balaam saw that it pleased the Lord to bless Israel, he went not, as at other times, to seek for enchantments, but he set his face toward the wilderness.” Fifthly and lastly, Balaam’s ecstasies were unstable and uncertain, and his figures and expressions were symbolical, for we read, for example: “He couched, he lay down as a lion, and as a great lion.” This false prophet then returned to his dwelling, but he reappears later in the camp of the Midianites, where he finally perished by the sword, at the hand of those whom Moses had sent to fight.

## In the Days of the Judges and Kings

In the days of the judges and kings, dreams and prophetic visions signified the same thing. In 1 Samuel 28, 6 *[sic; the quotation is found in 1 Samuel 9, 9]*, we read: “Beforetime in Israel, when men went to inquire of the Lord, thus they spake: Come, and let us go to the seer; for he that is now called a prophet was beforetime called a seer.”

In Numbers 27, 18-21, when Moses asked the Lord to give him a worthy successor, it is written: “And the Lord said unto Moses: Take thee Joshua, a man in whom is the spirit, and lay thine hand upon him, etc., and thou shalt put some of thine honor upon him, etc. And he shall stand before Eleazar the priest, who shall ask counsel for him after the judgment of Urim before the Lord,” etc. I have already quoted passages from the Bible to show that dreams and prophetic visions were regarded as one and the same thing; and dreams were so important, indeed, that a dreamer was placed in the same category as a prophet. “And when Saul saw the host of the Philistines, he was afraid, and his heart greatly trembled. And when Saul inquired of the Lord, the Lord answered him not, neither by dreams, nor by Urim, nor by prophets.” (1 Samuel 28, 6.) We read in Deuteronomy 13, 1-4: “If there arise among you a prophet, or a dreamer of dreams, and giveth thee a sign or a wonder, and the sign or the wonder come to pass, whereof he spake unto thee, saying: Let us go after other gods, which thou hast not known, and let us serve them: thou shalt not hearken unto the words of that prophet, or that dreamer of dreams: for the Lord your God proveth you, to know whether ye love the Lord your God with all your heart and with all your soul.” From this we learn that persons who were not prophets, and who were not of a pure heart, also had prophetic visions.

It would be as tedious as it is superfluous to relate all the visions of the prophets. We shall not, however, pass over in silence the most remarkable of them. In 1 Samuel 16 we find the history of Saul, who, after the spirit of God had departed from him, became gloomy and sick, and whose condition could be relieved only by the sweet sounds of music. “But the spirit of the Lord departed from Saul, and an evil spirit from the Lord troubled him. And Saul’s servants said unto him: Behold now, an evil spirit from God troubleth thee. Let our lord now command thy servants, which are before thee, to seek out a man who is a cunning player on an harp: and it shall come to pass, when the evil spirit from God is upon thee, that he shall play with his hand, and thou shalt be well. And Saul sent unto Jesse, saying: Let David, I pray thee, stand before me, etc. And it came to pass, when the evil spirit from God was upon Saul, that David took an harp, and played with his hand: so Saul was refreshed, and was well, and the evil spirit departed from him.” When Saul saw the host of the Philistines, his heart failed him, and he inquired of the Lord, but the Lord answered him not, “neither by dreams, nor by Urim, nor by prophets.” “If there arise among you prophets, or a dreamer of dreams, and giveth a sign, etc.” Saul was a seeker after signs and wonders, for once he inquired of Samuel concerning his lost asses; at another time he consulted the witch of Endor, and at other times again he trusted to deceitful dreams. The witch said to Saul: “Wherefore then dost thou ask of me, seeing the Lord is departed from thee, and thy kingdom is gone out of thine hand?”

In the books of Samuel, who was a clairvoyant from his youth, we find recorded many prophetic visions. The most remarkable of these visions were those of Samuel and of David. Even Saul attempted to prophesy, until the spirit of the Lord had departed from him. We have already related the history of the aged King David, who could obtain no more bodily warmth, though they covered him with clothes. A young virgin was procured for him, who slept in the king’s arms and ministered to him, and thus he regained warmth. (1 Kings 1, 1.)

## Elijah and Elisha

Among all the prophets of the old dispensation there was none more exalted than Elijah, whose very name was synonymous with a higher degree of being. We find in him an example of great significance in magnetic operations. He transmitted the most important doctrines of life, and he restored life to those who seemed dead, a history which we insert here *verbatim* [word for word]: “And it came to pass after these things, that the son of the woman, the mistress of the house, fell sick; and his sickness was so sore that there was no breath left in him. And she said unto Elijah: What have I to do with thee, O thou man of God? Art thou come unto me to call my sin to remembrance, and to slay my son? And he said unto her: Give me thy son. And he took him out of her bosom, and carried him up into the loft, where he abode, and laid him upon his own bed. And he cried unto the Lord, and said: O Lord my God, hast thou also brought evil upon the widow with whom I sojourn, by slaying her son? And he stretched himself upon the child three times, and cried unto the Lord, and said: O Lord my God, I pray thee, let this child’s soul come into him again. And the Lord heard the voice of Elijah; and the soul of the child came into him again, and he revived. And Elijah took the child, and brought him down out of the chamber into the house, and delivered him unto his mother.” (1 Kings 17, 17-24.)

Of a similar kind, a still more remarkable example of the striking and powerful magnetic influence is given in the history of the son of the Shunammite, who was restored to life by the prophet Elisha (2 Kings 4, 18-37). “And when the child was grown, it fell on a day, that he went out to his father to the reapers. And he said unto his father: My head, my head! And he said to a lad: Carry him to his mother. And when he had taken him, and brought him to his mother, he sat on her knees till noon, and then died. And she went up, and laid him on the bed of the man of God, and shut the door upon him, and went out. And she called unto her husband, and said: Send me, I pray thee, one of the young men, and one of the asses, that I may run to the man of God, and come again. And he said: Wherefore wilt thou go to him to-day? It is neither new moon, nor sabbath. And she said: It shall be well. Then: Drive, and go forward; slack not thy riding for me, except I bid thee. So she went and came unto the man of God to Mount Carmel. And it came to pass, when the man of God saw her afar off, that he said to Gehazi his servant: Behold, yonder is that Shunammite: run now, I pray thee, to meet her, and say unto her: Is it well with thee? Is it well with thy husband? Is it well with the child? And she answered: It is well. And when she came to the man of God to the hill, she caught him by the feet: but Gehazi came near to thrust her away. And the man of God said: Let her alone; for her soul is vexed within her: and the Lord hath hid it from me, and hath not told me. Then she said: Did I desire a son of my lord? Did I not say: Do not deceive me? Then he said to Gehazi: Gird up thy loins, and take my staff in thine hand, and go thy way: if thou meet any man, salute him not; and if any salute thee, answer him not again: and lay my staff upon the face of the child. And the mother of the child said: As the Lord liveth, I will not leave thee. And he arose, and followed her. And Gehazi passed on before them, and laid the staff upon the face of the child; but there was neither voice, nor hearing. Wherefore he went again to meet him, and told him, saying: The child is not awaked. And when Elisha was come into the house, behold, the child was dead, and laid upon his bed. He went in therefore, and shut the door upon them twain, and prayed unto the Lord. And he went up, and lay upon the child, and put his mouth upon his mouth, and his eyes upon his eyes, and his hands upon his hands: and he stretched himself upon the child, and the flesh of the child waxed warm. Then he returned, and walked in the house to and fro; and went up, and stretched himself upon him: and the child sneezed seven times, and the child opened his eyes. And he called Gehazi, and said: Call this Shunammite. So he called her. And when she was come in unto him, he said: Take up thy son.”

What can we learn from this? First, that one must be a man of God as Elisha was. Secondly, Elisha must have been well acquainted with the transmission of this power by means of a conductor, otherwise he would not have sent his servant before him with the staff, to raise the dead child by simply laying it upon his face. Thirdly, the order he gave his servant to salute no one on the way has a profound significance. He was to give his whole attention to the business of bringing the dead back to life, and not to allow himself to be diverted by any other consideration or occasion whatsoever. A proof that it is highly necessary and important that a magnetic physician should be free from all distraction, in order to concentrate all his energies upon the one object — the patient. Fourthly, the very conduct of this case is incomparable. Fifthly, it is a proof that perseverance and continuity are a first condition of a magnetic operation: a tree is not felled with a single stroke; thus Elisha, after the first effort, rose and walked to and fro in the house, and it was only at the second effort that the dead child began to breathe. From the conduct of Elisha (and of Saul) we may learn that the inner sense may also be developed by music, as, for example, when Elisha, called upon to prophesy, said: “But now bring me a minstrel.” And as the minstrel played, the hand of the Lord came upon him, and he prophesied. (2 Kings 3, 15.) That they knew the method of healing by the laying on of hands, and that they practised it, is proved by the passage (2 Kings 5, 11) in which Naaman, the Syrian captain, expected that Elisha would strike his hand over the leprous place, and thus remove his leprosy.

We often read that the remains of the saints wrought wonderful miracles and healed the sick long after their decease. This was the case with Elisha, for we read (2 Kings 13, 20): “And Elisha died, and they buried him. And the bands of the Moabites invaded the land at the coming in of the year.
