## Extract from the Magical Kabbalah of the Sixth and Seventh Books of Moses (Second Version)

**By S. T. N.**

Translated for the first time from the Cuthan-Samaritan language into English.

*Anno MDCCXXVI.*

### Translator's Preface

As the Oriental transcript of this work was imperfect in many places, its translation had to be established from the great original book, by reason of the purity of its text; it thereby gained the advantage of understanding and performing the exercises with serenity and confidence. The translator believes, however, that none of those who feel themselves honestly called to these things can ever fall a prey to misfortune, nor be deceived by the wiles and seductions of the old serpent — an inevitable fate which, under any other exorcism, must necessarily befall him — and that he may thence go forward surely and without fear, because only the angels of God will perform the service which He Himself requires.

### The Vestibule of the Entrance

The language and manuscript of this rare and eternal monument of light and of a higher wisdom are borrowed from the Cuthans, a tribe of the Samaritans, called Cuthim in the Chaldean dialect according to the Talmud; and they were so named in a spirit of derision. They were treated as sorcerers, because they taught at Cutha, their first place of abode, and afterwards in Samaria, the Kabbalah or Higher Magic (Book of Kings). Caspar, Melchior and Balthasar, the elected high priests, are shining lights among the Magi of the East. They were at once kings and doctors — the first Priest-teachers of this glorious knowledge; and from these Samaritan Cuthans — from these omnipotent priests of the fountain of light, called Nergal according to the traditions of the Talmud — are descended the Gypsies, who, through degeneracy, lost the consecration of their primordial power.

### Laws of Entrance

1. Before you can enter the temple of consecrated light, you must purify your soul and your body for thirteen days.

2. As a brother and disciple of the new covenant, or as a Christian, you must receive the holy sacrament for the glorification of the three kings — Caspar, Melchior and Balthasar.

3. Three holy masses must be read each time you make use of this Book in your priestly service, the intention fixed upon the three glorified kings.

4. You must provide yourself with a ram's horn, to summon the angels and the spirits. This horn must be included in your intentions of the holy mass.

5. You must wear a breastplate of parchment, ten inches high and ten inches wide, upon which shall be inscribed the names of the twelve apostles with the fivefold name of Schemhamforasch, in the very order in which it is placed upon the last leaf.

6. You must draw around yourself a circle upon white paper or upon sky-blue silk. Its circumference shall be thirteen feet, and, at the distance of one foot each, one of the following names must be written, namely: Moseh, Messias, Aaron, Jehova, Adoni, Jesus, Christus, Caspar, Melchior, Balthasar. Al. Al. Al.

7. Between each name you must place the holy symbol of Horet, namely: ✠ or ✠.

8. The breastplate must be included in the intention of the holy mass.

9. By consecration with the holy water of the three kings and with three lighted wax candles, you must finally pronounce a blessing upon this book, the horn, the breastplate and the circle, after reading a well-chosen mysterious ritual.

10. You may enter alone, or begin this great work with two companions, by day or by night, but always from the first to the thirteenth day of the month, and during the thirteenth day, and throughout the whole night of the new moon, and also during the full moon, when the three planets, Saturn, Mars and Jupiter, are visible in the heavens on the day of the exorcism, either separately or together.

11. You must always stand with your face turned toward Zion, or toward the rising of the sun.

12. He who refuses a copy of this book, or who suppresses it, or who steals it, shall be seized with an eternal trembling, like Cain, and the angels of God will depart from him.

### Introduction and Beginning

**Breastplate of Moses.** — *Schedusi, Weduse, Tiwisi.* — I have sinned, I shall sin.

**Prayer.** — Eternal God of our all! Our God! hear our voice, spare us and have mercy upon us. Receive our prayer with mercy and with favour. I have sinned. I have committed transgressions, I have sinned before Thee; I have done here upon earth that which displeases Thee. For the sake of Thy great name, forgive me all the sins, iniquities and transgressions which I have committed against Thee from my youth. Restore to their perfection all the holy names which I have defiled, great Champion, terrible, most high God, eternal Lord, God Sabaoth.

**Helmet of Moses and Aaron.** — *Wochnu, Tukal, Beschufa, Gutal.* — If I sin, I shall sound the great horn.

Here the horn must be sounded, three times in succession, toward the four corners of the earth, or toward the four quarters of the earth.

For the ram's horn, in the old covenant, is the symbol of omnipotence and of purification, or of beauty, of truth and of holiness.

**Breastplate of Aaron.** — *Dehutu, Euwsaitu, Bescholam.* — You have sinned. I shall sin in peace.

**Prayer.** — The Lord, King of all Kings, holy and praised is He; the Father, God, the Son of God, the Holy Spirit of God are three in one among these three. In the power of Thy strength and of Thy right, loose those who are bound, receive the prayer of Thy people, strengthen us, purify us, O terrible Hero, we who adore Thy only name. Protect them as the apple of Thine eye, bless them, purify them, reward them always in mercy and in justice. Mighty, holy Lord, recompense Thy congregation out of Thy great goodness. Thou, the only God, the exalted God, appear unto Thy people with Thy holy name; receive and remember our prayer; give ear unto our cries, Thou who knowest all secrets and who knowest our desire.

Here the horn must be sounded as before.

## Magical Laws of Moses

*Knig-Alf Lewuwat.* — We are great! Our hearts!

**Prayer.** — O Lord, arise, that my enemies may be destroyed and that they may flee; let those who hate Thee be scattered like smoke — drive them away. As wax melts before the fire, so let all the wicked vanish before God, for God has given Thee the kingdom. Pour out Thy wrath upon them. Let Thy wrath seize them. Thou shalt tread upon leopards and asps, and Thou shalt subdue the lion and the dragon. With God alone can we do great things. He will put them under our feet.

### The Chalice of Holiness

Al, Al, Al. Arise, eternal Angel!

This must be repeated three times aloud, and also by the symbol of the horn, for he is an angel of the sanctuary.

**Prayer.** — Thou who art, who wast and who shalt be in the old and the new covenant! Eternal, Jehovah, Jesus Christ, Messiah, All-Beautiful, All-True, All-Holy! All-Loving and All-Merciful in the old and in the new covenant. Thou hast said: heaven and earth shall pass away, but my words shall not pass away. Thou hast said: I am not come to destroy the old covenant, but to fulfil it. Thou hast said: he who sees me, sees the Father. Thou hast said: if you have true faith, you will be able to perform the wonders that I have performed; yea, you shall perform wonders far greater still than those which I have done. Come also unto me for the sake of my faith; come also unto me for the sake of Moses, Thy messenger of faith. Reveal to me also Thy mysterious name issuing from Jehovah, as Thou didst once to Thy prophet of fire, Moses, in the solitude; come, and tell me with love, by the heart of Moses and with the tongue of Aaron:

**Scahebual!** I will come!

### The Conjuration of Eleazar

*Duwatu, Buwatie, Bemaim.* — I come to you upon the water!

Bring me N. N.!

### Dismissal of Eleazar

*Orum, Bolectu, Ubajom.* — Cursed by night and by day!

## Citation of Quernithay, or Leviathan

These exorcisms, like those that follow, contain only the particular names of the angels who will permit the conjured spirits to appear, or who will compel them by force to appear. Here shall be called the three angels of omnipotence, to drive away the monsters of hell, namely:

**Elubatel, Ebuhuel, Atuesuel!**

Each name must be repeated three times.

### Dismissal

I beseech and conjure thee, angel Elubatel, to lead N. N. out of my presence.

The name of each angel must be called three times toward the four quarters of the earth, and the horn must be sounded three times.

### Chap. III. — Sorcery of Balaam

*Onu, Baschba, Nischoaz Hueretz.* — In the name of God, I conjure the earth.

### Chap. V. — Conjuration of the Laws of Moses

*Keisehu, Nischba, Lawemso.* — As it befits God, so hast Thou sworn it to our fathers.

**Prayer.** — Eternal of Eternals! Jehovah of Light, Adonai of Truth! Messiah of the All-Merciful! Jesus Christ, the beloved, all redemption and all love! Thou hast said: he who sees me, sees also the Father. Father, eternal Father of the old and of the new covenant; threefold Father, threefold Son, threefold Spirit, our Father. I beseech Thee and conjure Thee by the eternal words of Thine eternal truth.

And now the seventeenth chapter of John, or the prayer of Jesus, must be prayed.

**Final Prayer of the Conjuration of the Law.** — Eternal God, Jehova, Thou hast said: ask, and it shall be given you. I pray that Thou wilt hear Thy servants Caspar, Melchior and Balthasar, the high priests of Thy fountain of light! I pray that Thou wilt command Thine angels to purify me from all sin; that they may breathe upon me with love, and that they may cover me with the shadow of their wings. Send them down here below! Such is my prayer, in peace!

### Chap. VI. — Egitgim

Conjuration of the three angels: **Gebril! Meachuel! Nesanel!** By the lamp of the threefold eternal light, let N. N. appear before me.

Three calls of the voice and three of the horn.

### Chap. VII. — General Citation of Moses upon All Spirits

**Tubatlu! Bualu! Tulatu! Labusi! Ublisi!** — Let the spirit of N. N. appear and be brought before me.

Each of these five omnipotent angels must be called three times toward the four corners of the world, in a clear and mighty voice, and when the name of each has been pronounced three times, three sounds must be drawn from the horn. The name of each angel must therefore receive three calls of the voice and three of the horn.

### Dismissal of Moses

**Ubelutusi! Kadukuliti! Kebutzi!** — Remove from my presence the spirit of N. N.

Twelve calls of the voice and twelve of the horn, for each name.

### For the Left Hand

These signs were employed at the time of the burnt offering in the holy temple. *(see fig.)*

### For the Right Hand

These are also the symbols of the plagues of Pharaoh in Egypt. *(see fig.)*

*Schema Israel Adonai Elhoejno, Ekat.* — Hear, O Israel, the Lord our God is the only God.

## The Seventh Book of Moses

### Chap. I. — The Revelation of Zion

**The spirit appears in a pillar of fire, by night.**

**Talubsi! Latubusi! Kalubusi! Alusi!**

Arise and bring me the Pillar of Fire, that I may see.

The name of each angel must again receive three calls of the voice, directed toward the four quarters of the earth, and an equal number by the horn.

### Chap. II. — The Spirit Appears in a Pillar of Cloud, by Day

**Bual!** come! **Aul!** arise! **Tubo!** I come! **Wegulo!** arise!

In this place the sounding shall be repeated.

### Chap. VII. — General Citation of Moses upon All Spirits

**Adulal! Abulal! Lebusi!**

Arise and bring before me the spirit N.

Calls of the voice and of the horn as is already known.

### The Pentagon, or the Omnipotent Five-Corners

Here follows the Pentagon, or the omnipotent Five-Corners. *(see fig.)*

This mysterious figure must be drawn before the exorcism, in the open air, and upon the ground, with consecrated chalk or with the forefinger of the right hand dipped in the holy water of the three kings, just as it is drawn upon the paper, but each line must be thirteen feet in length. The conjurer then kneels in the centre of the star, with head uncovered and face turned toward Zion, and first calls in a loud voice, coming from the heart, the names of Caspar, Melchior and Balthasar, thirteen times; and after having called them thirteen times, he must also call the high and sacred name of Elohim 375 times, with equal fervour and equal faith. But only, as has already been said in the Laws of Entrance, No. 10, in the first three days or nights of the new moon, or of the full moon, or when Saturn, Mars and Jupiter appear in the heavens.

### Chap. IV. — Three New Signs: Frogs, Mice, Lice and Similar Vermin

**Adus! Baachur! Arbu! Ulu!**

Frogs, mice, lice and similar vermin, arise to our service.

### Chap. V. — Three Signs: Cattle Plague, Black Smallpox and Hail

**Abuli, Baal!**

Pestilence, black smallpox, etc., arise to our service.

### Chap. VI. — Three Signs: Locusts and Darkness

**Ardusi! Dalusi!**

Locusts, Darkness, arise to our service.

Such are the plagues which the Cuthans often employed in their exorcisms, by way of punishment.

## Revelation of the Sixth Book of Moses

### Chap. I. — The Spirit Appears to Moses in a Burning Bush

**Conjuration.**

**Kaluku! Ubesu! Lawisu!** — Arise and instruct me.

Calls of the voice and of the horn as is already known.

### Chap. II. — Moses Changes the Rod into a Serpent

**Tuwisu! Kawisu! Lawisu!** — Arise and change this rod into a serpent.

Calls of the voice and of the horn as usual.

### Chap. III. — Moses Changes the Water into Blood

**Akauatiu! Tuwalu! Labatu!** — Arise and change this water into blood.

Calls of the voice and of the horn as usual.

Here follows the Latin title of the book from which these magical fragments are extracted:

**BIBLIA Arcana Magica Alexandri (Magi)** [Bible of the Magical Arcana of Alexander (the Magus)], **according to the (revealed) Tradition of the Sixth and Seventh Books of Moses, with the Magical Laws.**

*Ex Verbis H. (humano) Intellectui Sigillatis Verbis.* [From the Words sealed to human understanding.]

*Nunc Apostolica ✠ (Auctoritate) Consecrata de Novo Confirmata ✠ ✠ (Licentia).* [Now consecrated by apostolic authority and confirmed anew, with licence.]

*Script de Eppbio.*

*Anno MCCCXXXVIII* [?].

### Description of the Image of Moses

Now Moses appears upon the right side of the silk, in simple priestly garb, holding in the right hand the tree of life, which bears four leaves, and in the left the ram's horn.

Upon his heart rests the cross; a broad white band floats over his shoulder and breast, a broad white girdle adorns his loins. He stands upright upon a kind of hat, adorned with three flowers. On the right of the hat rests a dove holding a ring in its beak; on the left side and above the head of Moses rises a threefold tripod bearing magical hieroglyphs.

1. Upon the leaves of the tree of life are read the words:

**Besulol Bedunim! Labatel!**

That is to say: if you are not pure, or if you do not become pure as a virgin through the two angels Bunedreim and Lebutal, you cannot attain the tree of life.

2. A hat adorned with three flowers, upon which Moses stands upright, and from which a dove, the ring in its beak, seems to descend; that is to say: if you preserve faith and hope with holy care, the spirit of omnipotence will emanate from you.

3. A threefold interlaced triangle, extending from the feet to the head, bearing inscribed the names of the three angels Meachuel, Labatel, Kautel; that is to say: and the three angels of the threefold God, Meachuel, Lebatel, Ketuel, will surround you and bear you upon their hands.

On the reverse of the image of Moses, or rather upon the first leaf according to the Oriental reckoning, appears the serpent raised up, sinuous and crowned, holding a ring between its teeth. Around the serpent may be seen the moon, the stars, the planets, water and many other magical hieroglyphic signs. On the left side of the tail are seen seven nails; on the right side are magical hieroglyphs forming the name of Schemhamporasch. See Jesus Christ with the cross, that is to say: Jesus Christ, by his love and by his seven wounds, and by his death upon the cross, for the sake of his love, has conquered the kingdoms of this world, and has thus taken back from the old serpent, the devil, the seal-ring of human omnipotence, or the happiness of man, for all eternal eternities, in order to fulfil the old covenant in the new covenant, to the eternal glorification of the eternal Father in the eternal Son, through the eternal Spirit. Amen.

**BIBLIA ARCANA MAGICA ALEXANDER, according to the Tradition of the Sixth and Seventh Books of Moses, with the Magical Laws.**

*Ex Verbis (H) Intellectui Sigillatis Verbis Nunc Apostolica ✠ Consecrata de Novo Confirmata ✠ ✠* [From the Words sealed to (human) understanding, now consecrated by apostolic authority and confirmed anew.]

*Script De Ulstro.*

*MCCCLXXXIII* [?].

### Breastplate of Aaron

*(plate of characters — see fig.)*

Hear, O Israel, the Lord our God is the only God. Amen.

# APPENDIX — OF THE SPIRITS AND THEIR OFFICES

## Magical Command (of the spirits), with the Black Raven

*Romae ad Arcanum Pontificatus*, under Pope Alexander VI; printed *anno (Christi) M. D. I.* [the year of Christ 1501].

### PREFATIO [Preface]

*Quoniam per ILLUSTREM et Generosum D. ARNOLDUM, comitem Bentheimi Tichelenburgi-Steinfordii, Dominum in Rhoda Wivelhoven, magica Arcana originalia celeberrimi D. Joannis FAUSTI, in Germania ex Kundling oriundi, Baroni HERMANNO in Mosa Ripa et Geldriae fines Batoburgico Episcopo fideliter admanuata, et anno quadragesimo supra sesquimillesimum ad VATICANUM APOSTOLICUM NOSTRUM ROMANUM per eum transmissa sunt, volumus : ut haec typis imprimantur, ad Arcanum Pontificatus mandentur, et sicut pupilla oculi in archivio Nostro serventur et custodiantur, atque extra Valvas Vaticanas non imprimantur neque inde transportentur ; si vero quiscunque temere contra agere ausus fuerit, DIVINAM maledictionem latae sententiae ipso facto, Nobis solis reservandis, se incursurum sciat ; ita mandamus et constituimus Virtute Apostolica Ecclesiae JESU CHRISTI, sub poena Excommunicationis et supra. Anno secundo Vicariatus Nostri. ROMAE, VERBI INCARNATI Anno M. D. I.*

*ALEXANDER VI, D. G. PONTIFEX.*
*F. Piccolomineus, Cardinalis Cancell.*

[Translation: Whereas, by the illustrious and generous lord ARNOLD, Count of Bentheim-Tecklenburg-Steinfurt, lord of Rheda and Wevelinghoven, the original magical arcana of the most celebrated D. Johannes FAUST, native of Kundling in Germany, were faithfully delivered by hand to Baron HERMANN, Bishop of Batenburg, on the Meuse, upon the borders of Guelders, and by him transmitted, in the year one thousand five hundred and forty, to OUR APOSTOLIC ROMAN VATICAN, We will: that these pieces be printed, committed to the Arcanum of the Pontificate, kept and preserved in Our archives as the apple of the eye; that they be neither printed outside the doors of the Vatican nor carried abroad; and should anyone rashly dare to act to the contrary, let him know that he would incur, by the very deed, the DIVINE curse of sentence already pronounced, absolution from which is reserved to Us alone. Thus We ordain and establish it, by virtue of the apostolic authority of the Church of JESUS CHRIST, under pain of excommunication and beyond. In the second year of Our Vicariate. At ROME, in the year of the INCARNATE WORD 1501. — ALEXANDER VI, by the grace of God PONTIFF. — F. Piccolomineus, Cardinal Chancellor.]

### APPROBATIO [Approbation]

*[…] instructio Vaticana Sapienti paucis maxima ; praeterea […] curator a sua Sanctitate desuper facta Consecratio Canonica.*

[Approximate translation, the passage being much corrupted: “… Vatican instruction — to the wise, few words suffice, yet they are of the highest import; moreover, … the canonical Consecration made thereupon by His Holiness.”]

### LECTOR [To the Reader]

*Ne spernas librum, si non intelligis esto,*
*Si contra captum est, satis est, quod vota secundet.*
*Frigidus in pratis cantando rumpitur anguis. (Ecl. 8, Virg.)*
*Quidlibet audendi semper fuit aequa potestas.*
*Hoc lege, lector, opus : sacra haec monumenta polorum ;*
*Hinc optata feres, haec te tua vota docebunt.*
*Fide, cave, sapienter age, et virtute labora,*
*Sicque beabit opus pia CONSECRATIO facta.*

[Translation: Do not despise this book, even though thou shouldst not understand it; if it surpasses thy understanding, it is enough that it seconds thy wishes. “The cold serpent, in the meadows, bursts at the chant of incantations” (Virgil, Eclogue VIII). To dare all things was ever an equal right for all. Read, reader, this work: these are the sacred monuments of the heavens; thou shalt draw from it what thou desirest, thy very wishes shall teach it thee. Have faith, be wary, act wisely and labour with virtue: thus shall the pious CONSECRATION accomplished make the work blessed.]

D. I. F.

## INSTRUCTIONS

If thou wilt cite the spirits and compel them to appear visibly before thee and to render thee obedience, observe the following instructions:

1. Keep the commandments of God as far as it is possible for thee to do so.

2. Build and rely solely upon the might and power of God; believe firmly in His almighty help in thy work, and the spirits will become thy servants and obey thee.

3. Continue thy citations and cease not, even though the spirits should not appear at once. Be constant in thy work and in thy faith, for he who doubts shall obtain nothing.

4. Take particular heed to the time, namely:

   - the night of Monday, from eleven o’clock until three;
   - the night of Tuesday, from ten o’clock until two;
   - the night of Wednesday, from midnight until three;
   - the night of Thursday, from midnight until two;
   - the night of Friday, from ten o’clock until three;
   - the night of Saturday, from ten o’clock until midnight.

   Sanctify the Sabbath unto the Lord Sabaoth, Adonai, Tetragrammaton.

5. It must at the same time be the new moon. Thou mayest perhaps say: “Why these hours and these signs — are not all days the Lord’s?” That is true; but not all hours and all signs are favourable for ruling over their spirits.

6. Draw in full the following circle, described in this work, upon parchment, written with the blood of young white doves. The size of the circle is left to thy choice.

7. If thou wilt undertake the operation, take care first to consecrate the circle.

✠ ✠ ✠

*(see this circle, fig. 24)*

*Ego N. N., consecro et benedico istum Circulum per Nomina Dei Altissimi in eo Scripta, ut sit mihi et omnibus Scutum et Protectio Dei Fortissimi Elohim Invincibilis contra omnes malignos Spiritus eorumque Potestates. In Nomine Dei Patris, Dei Filii, Dei Spiritus Sancti. Amen.*

[Translation: I, N. N., consecrate and bless this circle by the Names of the Most High God written therein, that it may be, for me and for all, the shield and protection of the most mighty God, Elohim the Invincible, against all evil spirits and their powers. In the Name of God the Father, of God the Son, of God the Holy Ghost. Amen.]

On entering this circle, speak thus: **Tetragrammaton, Theos, Ischyros, Athanatos, Messias, Imas, Kyrie Eleison. Amen.**

After having entered the circle, begin thy operation with the following prayer, taken from the ninety-first Psalm:

> He that dwelleth in the secret place of the most High shall abide under the shadow of the Almighty. I will say of the Lord, He is my refuge and my fortress: my God; in him will I trust. Surely he shall deliver thee from the snare of the fowler, and from the noisome pestilence. He shall cover thee with his feathers, and under his wings shalt thou trust: his truth shall be thy shield and buckler. Thou shalt not be afraid for the terror by night; nor for the arrow that flieth by day. Because thou hast made the Lord, which is my refuge, even the most High, thy habitation; there shall no evil befall thee, neither shall any plague come nigh thy dwelling. Because he hath set his love upon me, therefore will I deliver him: I will set him on high, because he hath known my name. He shall call upon me, and I will answer him: I will be with him in trouble; I will deliver him, and honour him. With long life will I satisfy him, and shew him my salvation. — So mayest thou come to my aid, to me and to all who seek thy holy [name], ✠ God the Father, ✠ God the Son, ✠ God the Holy Ghost. Amen.

## Citation of the Seven Great Princes

**taken from the Sixth and Seventh Books of Moses, *Biblia Magica***

### CITATIO AZIELIS [Citation of Aziel]

Agla, Cadelo, Sambit, Caclem, Awenhatocoro, Aziel, Zorwoth, Yzewoth, Xoro, Quotwe, Theosy, Meweth, Xosoy, Yachyres, Gabay, Hagay, Staworo, Wyhaty, Ruoso, Xuatho, Rum, Ruwoth, Zytos, Quaylos, Wewor, Vegath, Wysor, Wuzoy, Noses, **Aziel**.

### CITATIO ARIELIS [Citation of Ariel]

Yschyros, Theor, Zebaoth, Wyzeth, Yzathos, Xyzo, Xywethorowdy, Xantho, Wiros, Rurawey, Ymowe, Noswathosway, Wirvnethowesy, Zebaoth, Yvmo, Zvswethonowe, Yschyrioskay, Ulathos, Wytoy, Yrsawo, Xyzeth, Durobij, Chaos, Wudowelhus, Yzweoy, Zaday, Zywaye, […], Yachyros, Imas, Tetragrammaton, **Ariel**.

### CITATIO MARBUELIS [Citation of Marbuel]

Adonay, Jehova, Zebaoth, Theos, Yzhathoroswe, Wehozymathos, […]yghoroy, Vegorym, Abaij, Wogos, Gijghym, Zewoij, Ycosowe, Wothym, Kijzwe, Uijwoth, Omegros, Hehgewe, Zebaoij, Wezator, Zibuo, Sijbetho, Ythos, Zeatijm, Wovoe, Sijwoijmwethij, Pharvoij, Zewor, Wefgos, Ruben, Hvbathoroos, Stawows, Zijen, Zijwowij, Ham, Worse, Yzwet, Zebaoth, Agla, **Marbuel**.

### CITATIO MEPHISTOPHILES [Citation of Mephistophiles]

Messias, Adonaij, Weforus, Xathor, Yxewe, Soraweijs, Yxaron, Wegharb, Zijbalor, Wegbaij, Wesorou, Xoxywe, Zykohwowetho, R[…]atho, Zebaoth, Adonaij, Zijwetho, Aglaij, W[…]daij, Zijebo, Xosthoy, Athlato, Zsewey, Zyxyzet, Ysche, Sarsewu, Zyzyrn, Deworonhathbo, Xyxewe, Syzwe, Theos, Yschaos, Worsoubefgosy, Gefgowe, Hegor, Quaratho, Zywe, Messias, Abarabi, **Mephistophiles**.

### CITATIO BARBUELIS [Citation of Barbuel]

Yschiros, Imas, Zebaoth, Otheos, Kuwethosorym, Zylohym, Zaday, Yschowe, Quyos, Zenhatoroway, Yzwesor, Xywoy, Yzyryr, Zalymo, Zabaoth, Adonaii, Messios, Aglaabaij, Stoweos, Hijwetho, Ycoros, Zijwetho, Uwoim, Chamoweo, Zijzobeth, Sotho, Emnohaij, Zedije, Huwethos, Chory, Yzquoos, Lijraije, Weghoijm, Xijxor, Waijos, Gofaljme, Toroswe, Yeijros, Emanuel, Imas, **Barbuel**.

### CITATIO AZIABELIS [Citation of Aziabel]

Theos, Ygweto, Yzgowoij, Quiseo, Wijzope, Xoraoij, Nowetho, Yxose, Bagathon, Xoro, Theos, Magowo, Wijzosorwothe, Xaroshaij, Zebaoth, Emanuel, Messia, Yzijwotho, Zadaij, Xexhatosijmeij, Buwatho, Xsewet, Xijrathor, Zijbos, Malhaton, Yzos, Uzewor, Raguil, Wewot, Xawewe, Quorhijm, Zadob, Zibathor, Weget, Zijzawe, Ulijzor, Tetragrammaton, **Aziabel**.

Theos, Aba, Aaba, Aba, Agathoswaij, Yzorog, Ywetho, Quardos, Quasoat, Uschijros, Cijmoe, Qowathim, Gefoij, Zarobe, Weghat, Ohegathurewaij, Mesows, Xalose, Waghthorsowe, Wephatho, Yzebo, Storilwet, Ooaij, Quorathon, Sijbo, Mephor, Wijbose, Zaloros, Buetho, Zebaothonaijwos, Zijweth, Ycarij, Ruwethonowe, Ruiathosoraij, Zebaoth, Messias, **Aniquel**.

*[Here follow the four leaves — fig. 24, 25, 26, 27, 28.]*

## The Plates

- **Circle written upon parchment with the blood of young white doves** *(see fig. 24)* — the circle bears, among others, the inscriptions ORIENS [East] and OCCIDENS [West].
- ✠ ✠ ✠ **AZIELIS** — Seal or character for coercion and obedience *(see fig. 25)*.
- ✠ ✠ ✠ **ARIELIS** — Seal or character for coercion and obedience *(see fig. 25)*.
- ✠ ✠ ✠ **MARBUELIS** — Seal or character for coercion and obedience.
- ✠ ✠ ✠ **MEPHISTOPHILIS** — Seal or character for coercion and obedience *(see fig. 26)*.
- ✠ ✠ ✠ **BARBUELIS** — Seal or character for coercion and obedience *(see fig. 27)*.
- ✠ ✠ ✠ **A[…]ELIS** *(name corrupted in both copies — most probably one of the two last princes)* — Seal or character for coercion and obedience.

## NOTE — Of the Use of the Seals

When these great princes do not appear immediately at the foregoing citations, or if they hesitate in their obedience, then take incense and myrrh and cast them upon burning coals; and when the smoke rises, place the seal of the spirit above it, with the following mysterious words:

*Ex VI. et VII. Libro MOSIS, Bibliae Arcano MAGICAE.*
**ALTISSIMA DEI VERBA, SPIRITUUM COACTIVA, MOSIS, AARON ET SALOMONIS.**
[From the Sixth and Seventh Book of MOSES, from the Arcanum of the Bible of MAGIC. — Most high words of God, coercive of the spirits, of Moses, Aaron and Solomon.]

Zijmuorsobet, Noijm, Zavaxo, Quehaij, Abawo, Noquetonaij, Oasaij, Wuram, Thefotoson, Zijoronaifwetho, Mugelthor, Yzxe, Agiopuaij, Huzije, Surhatijm, Sowe, Oxursoij, Zijbo, Yzweth, Quaij, Salarthon, Qaij, Qeahaij, Qijrou, Sardowe, Xoro, Wugothoswerhy, Kaweko, Ykquos, Zehatho, Aba. Amen.

### Apparitio, or the Apparition

It is almost instantaneous upon these mysterious words and this procedure. As soon as they appear, however, address thyself to them and compel them to obedience by the following *coactio* [constraint]:

### BOND OF MOSES

Zebaoath, Abatho, Tetragrammaton, Adonaij, Abathoij, Xijhawe, Aglaij, Quohowe, Agla, Muijrosheweth, Phalowaij, Agla, Theos, Messias, Zijwethororijm, Feghowo, Aba, Mowewo, Choe, Adonaij, Cewoe, Christohatos, Tetragrammaton.

A ✠ M ✠ C ✠ N ✠

### INSTRUCTIONS

Since the spirits will now appear promptly, present thy desires honestly, as before thy fellow man, without fear, for nothing can harm thee; on the contrary, all must serve thee, render thee obedience and serve thee according to thy wishes. In this regard, take care not to compromise in any measure with the spirit; for all this power and this word of strength, which Moses, Aaron and Solomon used according to the revelations of God, suffice to compel the spirits to reveal to thee the treasures of the earth and of the sea, and to deliver them to thee without damage or deceit.

*Mihi Fausto crede (✠) Experto.* [Believe me, Faust, who have had experience of it.]

## REMARKS [catalogue of the spirits and their offices]

- **AZIEL** is a spirit of the treasures of the earth and of the sea, very prompt. He appears in the form of a wild ox.

- **ARIEL** is a very serviceable spirit; he appears in the form of a ferocious dog. He commands the lost treasures of the earth and of the sea.

- **MARBUEL** appears in the form of an old lion. He delivers the treasures of the water and of the earth, and helps to obtain all secret knowledge and all honours.

- **MEPHISTOPHILES** is ready to serve, and appears in the form of a youth. He helps willingly in all arts of skill, and gives the *spiritus servos* [serving spirits], otherwise called “familiars.” He brings very quickly the treasures of the earth and of the deep.

- **BARBUEL** is a master of all arts and of all secret knowledge, a great master of all treasures. He is very accommodating, and appears with eagerness in the form of a wild boar.

- **AZIABEL** is a prince of the spirits of the water and of the mountain and of their treasures. He is amiable, and appears with a great crown of pearls.

- **ANITUEL** appears in the form of a serpent of Paradise. He confers great riches and great honours, according to the wish.

The seals or characters […] of the Seven Great Princes of the spirits must be written upon virgin parchment, with the blood of butterflies, at the time of the full moon. Know moreover that the Seven Great Princes of the spirits count among their number certain of the legions of crowned spirits who were cast out of Heaven — *ita ex Revelatione Traditio VI. et VII. libri Mosis* [thus, according to the Revelation, the tradition of the Sixth and Seventh Books of Moses relates it] […].

D. I. F.

Since the spirits have now served thee according to thy wish, dismiss them and send them away as follows:

**Zebaoth, Theos, Yschyres, Messias, Imas, Weghaymnko, Quoheos, Roveym, Christoze, Abay, Xewefaraym, Agla.**

And now depart in the name of God; praise, live and thank God, until the

**E. N. D.**

# SCHEMHAMPHORAS AND THE SEVEN SEMIPHORAS

*[The original opens with a Latin epigraph, corrupted in both scans, whose sense is approximately: the divine names in the Hebrew tongue, whether they be read from the beginning or from the end, from the right as from the left, are of immense virtue.]*

## Semiphoras and Schemhamphoras of King Solomon

*Wesel, Duisburg and Frankfurt: printed and published by a licensed bookseller of the said cities.*

### Humble Prayer for Obtaining Wisdom and Understanding

“For the Lord giveth wisdom: out of his mouth cometh knowledge and understanding.” — Proverbs II, 6.

“If any of you lack wisdom, let him ask of God, that giveth to all men liberally, and upbraideth not.” — Epistle of James I, 5.

O God, my Father and Lord of all goodness, Thou who hast created all things by Thy Word, and who hast prepared man in Thy wisdom that he may rule over all the creatures made by Thee, that he may govern the world with holiness and justice and judge with an upright heart: give me that wisdom which stands constantly about Thy throne, and cast me not out from among Thy children. For I am Thy servant and the son of Thy handmaid, a feeble creature of short existence, too weak in understanding, in right and in law. Send her from the height of Thy heaven and from the throne of Thy glory, that she may abide with me and labour with me, that I may know and do the things that are pleasing unto Thee. For Thy wisdom knows and understands all things; let her guide me in my works and protect me by her glory, and my labours shall be pleasing unto Thee. When I was yet in my youth, I sought wisdom without fear in my prayer; I asked for her in the temple, and I will seek her until my end. My heart rejoices in her as when the young grapes ripen. Thou art my Father, my God and my Shepherd, Thou who dost succour me. Thy hand hath created and prepared me; teach me, that I may learn Thy commandments; open mine eyes, that I may behold the wonders of Thy law. Remember, Lord, Thy covenant, and teach me what I ought to say and think. Instruct me, and I shall live. Lord, show me Thy ways, lead me in Thy truth and teach me. I am Thy servant: teach me, that I may understand Thy testimony. Comfort me anew by Thy help, and let the spirit of joy sustain me. Thou who lovest life, Thy immortal spirit is in all things. Teach me to work in a manner that is pleasing unto Thee, for Thou art my God. Let Thy good spirit lead me by pleasant paths. With Thee is the living fountain, and in Thy light we see light. Establish my steps, and let no iniquity have dominion over me. Teach me wholesome ways and enlighten me, for I believe Thy commandments. Lead me in Thy truth and teach me, for Thou art the God who succours me, and I wait for Thee every day. Let Thy face shine upon Thy servant, and teach me to know Thy righteousness. Let me behold Thy glory, for Thou, Lord, art my light, and Thou wilt turn my darkness into day. Be pleased to unite Thyself to me for eternity, and to entrust to me justice and judgment, grace and mercy; yea, be pleased to unite me to Thee in faith, that I may know Thee, the Lord. Lord, let my complaints come before Thee; instruct me according to Thy word. Let my prayers come before Thee; deliver me according to Thy word. Show me Thy ways, O Lord, that I may walk in Thy truth. Keep my heart in simplicity, that I may fear Thy name. I will remember Thy name from childhood; therefore shall all peoples give Thee thanks for ever and ever. Amen.

### Declaration of King Solomon

In the name of the most high and almighty Creator, I, King Solomon, hold to the interpretation of the name of God **Semiphoras** — that is to say the First and the Greatest, the most ancient and the most hidden of mysteries, of great power and virtue, for obtaining all that is asked of God. For God must be worshipped in Spirit and in truth, which does not consist in many and vain words: every word and name of God exists of itself, and therefore the name and the prayer must agree, and no foreign name must be employed without necessity when one intends to accomplish something fearful or wonderful — so that the divine quality may pour into our soul and our spirit His grace and His gifts; that is to say, the consciousness of God in His name, whereby He draws near and abides with those who know His name. Wherefore this name must be held in the greatest honour and must be hidden from all frivolous and unworthy persons, since God Himself says in Exodus: “From all places I will come unto thee and I will bless thee, because thou rememberest my name.” Wherefore the Hebrews [Maccabees] have seventy-two names for God, and have named and written **Schemhamphoras**, the name of seventy-two letters.

First, it must be known that the names of God can be taught and understood in the Hebrew tongue alone, and that neither can we pronounce them in another dialect, as they have been revealed to us by the grace of God. For they are the sacrament and the emanation of the divine omnipotence — not of man, nor of the angels —, instituted and consecrated by God to instil the divine harmony in a certain manner, according to the characters of His immutable number and figure, and before which those who are set over the heavens tremble. The angels and all creatures honour them and make use of them to praise their Creator and to bless Him with the greatest reverence in His divine works; and whosoever shall apply them as is fitting, with fear and trembling and with prayer, shall be mightily enlightened by the spirit of God, shall be joined to the divine unity, and shall be powerful according to the will of God.

*[There follows a passage much corrupted in both scans, of which here is the reconstructed substance:]* The man who would operate by these names must know that nothing is accomplished without the fear of God and the love of one’s neighbour; whosoever raises and improves his understanding knows and works great things. He who pronounces the names of God with a pure soul and not out of levity […]. On the other hand, the name of God must never be employed in vain: the true name of God […] He has reserved it and wills not that it be pronounced without necessity, until His design be accomplished and perfect. After this, the angels have their own names […] which it is not necessary for us to enumerate.

In the third place, all the names of God are drawn from the Torah by three methods, by means of the kabbalistic art: *Gematria, Notariacon* and *Geometria* [the third method is usually called *Temurah*].

### Of the Parts of the World

*[Degraded passage; substance:]* Jehovah, the ineffable Creator of the world, created […] by His Providence. There are four parts of the world, of which the most subtle is the supercelestial world: there are the Choirs of the angels — Cherubim and Seraphim, *Potestates et Virtutes*, Archangels and Angels, spirits who stand before the throne of the Most High; and the four angels who stand at the four corners of heaven — Michael, Raphael, Gabriel, Uriel; four angels preside over the elements: Cherub [for the air], Tharsis [for the water], Ariel [for the earth], Seraph [for the fire]. Highly enlightened men all receive the light of God.

The second light, or part of the world, is the heaven of all the stars, with the Triplicities of the twelve signs, under which the sun accomplishes its course each year, producing the change of the seasons — spring, summer, autumn and winter —, the principle of birth and of corruption, and changing […].

In the third part of the work are the elements and all that is subordinate to them: this is the little world, man, who in his turn bears the four elements within himself. *Anima* [the soul] is in the head, by thought; *Spiritus* [the spirit] in the heart, and operates through the arteries; *Corpus* [the body] is the whole body with the veins; *Genius*, a spark of fire, is in the kidneys and engenders birth. Man has four spiritual and active faculties, or *spiritus*: *Animalis, Vitalis, Naturalis, Genitivus*. The soul has inner senses: the *sensus communis*, in which it keeps the forms (*ideae*) and the other perceptions, *intelligens* in the brain;

2ndly the *imaginatrix*, the higher reason, otherwise called *phantasia*, which draws a picture from the power and accomplishes all things;

3rdly the *aestimatio*, which recalls to the mind the species of all things […];

4thly the *ratio*, knowledge of all things […];

5thly the *memoria*, the memory, which retains all things belonging to the faculties and operations of the mind, to carry experience […] by the agitation of the senses increase the human […] by God. The living spirit of the heart embraces within itself four virtues: *Justitia, Temperantia, Prudentia, Fortitudo* [justice, temperance, prudence, fortitude], and these, through the arteries and the blood, animate the soul with the body. The appetite of the senses […] the spiritual action and power reside in the liver […] nutrition and attraction, support and sustenance; the proper spirit of strength and of sap resides in the kidneys, to multiply by the divine perfection.

Man has within him the elements: namely spirit, fruit [blood?], flesh and bone — four natures or temperaments: hot, cold, moist, dry. Attraction is produced by heat and dryness; retention by cold and dryness; digestion (*coctio*) by heat and moisture; expulsion by cold and moisture: bile, blood, phlegm and melancholy [the four humours].

In the fourth part of the world is the darkness, instituted for condemnation, for wrath and for punishment. Four princes of the demons do harm in the four elements: **Samael, Asmodeus [Asmodel], Azael, Mahazael**; four princes of the demons over the four quarters of the earth: **Oriens, Paymon, Egyn, Amaymon**.
## The Seven Semiphoras of Adam

The first Semiphoras is that of Adam, when he spoke with the Creator [in the garden of Eden].

The second Semiphoras, when he spoke with the angels and the spirits.

The third, when he spoke with [the demons or spirits].

The fourth, when he spoke with the creatures of the four elements — the birds, the fishes, the beasts and the creeping things of the earth.

The fifth, when he spoke with inanimate things, such as trees, seeds and vegetation.

The sixth, when he spoke with the winds.

The seventh, when he spoke with the sun, the moon and the stars.

By the power of the seven Semiphoras he could create and destroy […].

**The first Semiphoras** was recognized by Adam when God made him and placed him in Paradise, where he was permitted to remain only seven hours. The name is **Jove**, which must be pronounced only in the greatest necessity, and then only with the most devout feelings toward the Creator. In that case thou shalt find grace and succor.

**The second Semiphoras**, by which Adam spoke with the angels, granted him the expression **Yeseraye**, that is to say, “God without Beginning and without End”; it must be pronounced when one speaks with the angels, and then thy questions shall receive answer and thy wishes shall be fulfilled.

**The third Semiphoras**, by which Adam spoke with [the spirits whom he questioned], who gave him satisfactory answers, consists in the words **Adonay Sabaoth, cadas adonay cadas**; these words must be pronounced when thou wishest to gather together the winds, the spirits or the demons: **cadas Adonay Sabaoth, cadas**.

**The fourth Semiphoras**: **Layamen, Iava, Firin, Lavagellayn, Lavaquiri, Lavagola, Lavatasorin, Layfialafin, Lyafaran**; by this name he bound and loosed all animals and spirits.

**The fifth Semiphoras**: **Lyacham, Lyalgema, Lyafaran, Lialfarah, Lebara, Lebarosin, Layararalus**; if thou wishest to bind [growing things], such as trees and seeds, thou must pronounce the words above.

**The sixth Semiphoras** is great in power and in virtue: **Letamnin, Letaylogo, Letasynin, Lebaganaritin, Letarminim, Letagelogin, Lotafalosin**. Employ them when thou desirest that the elements or the winds should accomplish thy wishes.

**The seventh Semiphoras** is great and mighty. These are the names of the Creator, which must be pronounced at the beginning of every undertaking: **Eliaon yoena adonay cadas ebreel, eloy ela agiel, ayoni sachado, essuselas eloym, delion iau elynla, delia yazi, Zazael, paliel man, umiel, onela dilatan saday alma paneim alym, canal dens usami yaras calipix calfas sasna saffa saday aylata panteomel auriel arion phaneton secare panerionys emanuel Joth Jalaph amphia, than demisrael mu all Le Leazyns ala phonar aglacyel qyol paeriteron theferoym barimel, jael haryon ya apiolell echet.**

These holy names, pronounce them each time with reverence toward God, when thou desirest to accomplish something by the elements or by that which pertains to them, and thy wishes shall be fulfilled, and that which is to be destroyed shall be destroyed; for God will be with thee, because thou knowest his name.

## The Seven Semiphoras of Moses

What follows is another name of Semiphoras, which God gave to Moses in seven parts.

The first is that in which Moses hid himself and spoke with God, when the fire burned in the bush without consuming it.

The second, when he spoke with the Creator upon the mountain.

The third, when he divided the Red Sea and passed through it with all the people of Israel.

The fourth, when his rod was changed into a serpent, which devoured the other serpents.

The fifth, these are the names which were written upon the forehead of Aaron.

The sixth, when he made a brazen serpent and burned the golden calf, to turn the plague away from the Israelites.

The seventh, when the manna fell in the wilderness and the water gushed forth from the rock.

**In the first** are the words which Moses pronounced as he went up the mountain, when he spoke to the flames of the fire: **Maya, Affaby, Zien, Jaramye, yne Latebni damaa yrsano, noy lyloo Lhay yly yre Kylo Zya Lyelee, Loate, lideloy eyloy, mecha ramethy rybifassa fu azyry scihin rite Zelohabe vete hebe ede neyo ramy rahabe conbec** […]. If thou devoutly addressest this word to God, thine undertaking shall be accomplished without any doubt.

**In the second** are the words which God addressed to Moses when he went up the mountain: **Abtan, Abynistan, Zoratan Juran nondiesip otarte fays alapeina pognij podaij sacrificium.** By these words the prophet spoke to the angels with whom the four quarters of the earth are filled, and by whom the temple was founded […]. If thou wishest to pronounce them, thou must fast three days, be chaste and pure; then thou shalt be able to accomplish many wonders.

**In the third** are the words which Moses pronounced to divide the Red Sea: **Qua claie saijec holowomaatl; bekahu aijclo mare asnia baene hicha ijfale malieha arnija aremeholona queleij, Lineno, feijano, ijoije nfalao flaboira nethee hijcere.** If thou hast lost the favor of thy master, or if thou wishest to win the good will of someone, pronounce these words with fervor and humility.

**In the fourth** are the words which Moses pronounced when he changed his rod into a serpent: **Micrato, raepijsathon reb petanith pistan ijttn ijer hijgarin ignition temayronaycoti dunsnas castas Lacias astas yecon cijna calbera natu facas.** Pronounce these names when thou wishest to see thy desires accomplished.

**In the fifth** are the names written upon the forehead of Aaron when he spoke with the Creator: **Sadaij baijlves Lucas etacijus jaconi hasihaia yein irio, sep, actitas barne lud donecnij eija ichhu reu, vaha, vialia, eije. Vie haija hoij asaija salna hahai. Qici, ijaija. Elenehel, na vena, setna.** These names are mighty to satisfy every request.

**In the sixth** are the names which were written upon the rod of Moses, when he made the brazen serpent and broke the golden calf: **Yane mare syam, abijl ala, uano, uija actenal tijogas ijano, eloim ya nehu ijane haij ijanehu, ahijaco mea.** By this name destroy all sorcery and all evil. Thou must not pronounce it lightly in thy works.

**In the seventh** are the words which Moses employed in leading the Israelites out of Egypt, by which he caused the manna to come from heaven and made the water flow from the rock: **Sadaij amara elon pheneton eloij eneij ebeoel messias ijahe vehu hejiane, ijananel elijon.** Pronounce these words when thou desirest to do something wonderful, or when thou art in great necessity, and call upon God with fervor.

### Prayer

O thou living God, great, strong, mighty, holy and pure Creator, full of mercy, blessed Lord of all things: may thy name be praised. I implore thee: fulfill my desire. Thou canst do it. Permit us to accomplish this work; grant us thy grace and give us thy divine blessing, that we may happily complete this work. Thou, holy, merciful and gracious God, have pity upon us. May thy name **Jeseraye** be adored for ever and ever. Amen.

## Declaration of the Divine Names

In the name of the almighty Creator, I, Solomon, hold to the declaration of the divine names:

**Agla** — “Thou art a mighty God for all eternity.” He who carries this name upon him, written on a plate of gold, shall never die a sudden death. **Ardita** — the beginning of all unity. **Ahen** — “thou, solid rock, united to the Son. Amen.” “Thou, Lord, true king, accomplish it,” etc.

These names are composed from the beginning of the chapters of **Adonay**, which the Hebrews used in place of the unpronounceable name, **Asser Eserie**.

The seven mighty names, which may be obtained at a favorable hour and in a favorable place: **Comitejon, sedeaij, throtomas, sasmagata bilil ijcos.**

The four names of the Creator: **Jva, Jona, Eloij, Jena**. He who […] *mepherias phaton*: he who carries this name upon him is invincible.

The name which God communicated to Moses on Mount Sinai, **El Cedjon**, drives away all causes of sorrow.

The name which Joshua prayed when the sun stood still: *haahaads a helmijr, deahd atr* — it brings down vengeance upon enemies.

The ten names of **Sephiroth** which Solomon pronounced — “I prayed toward them, and he gave me wisdom”: **Ether [Kether], Hochmah, Binah, Hesed, Geburah, Tiphereth, Nezah, Hod, Jesod, Malchut.**

Now follow the ten names of God: **Eheie, Jod Tetragrammaton, Tetragrammaton Elohim, El, Elohim Gibor, Eloha, Tetragrammaton Sabaoth, Elohim Sabaoth, Sadai, Adonay Melech** — [Elohim Sabaoth] has ten letters; Tetragrammaton Vedaath has eight letters; **Eheje**, the self-existence of God; *Aser He Aser, eheie*, names of God of seven letters.

**Eseh**, employed by Moses as the fire of God […]; these are all Hebrew characters.

**Emeth**, the true God — this is the seal of God. The explanation of the ten names of God and of the ten Sephiroth is given in Cornelius Agrippa, *De occulta philosophia*, book 3, chap. 10.

**Hacaba**, the holy and adored God. — **Hu**, himself: the power of the Deity. — **Hod**, the Eternal [?], a divine being. — **Jah**, a just God, who compares himself with man. — **Jesuba**, the Messiah, who shall come in the golden age. — **Jaht**, he who created the light. — **Isala**, which with the name **El** corresponds to the transposed name (each making 31).

**Mettatron** [Metatron] […] and **Masfrag**: the two names derive from a transposition […]. **Meudib**, derived from the transposition of the letters […] the name of God, must be employed in tribulation and oppression. — **Oromasim, Mitrim, Araminon** signify God and the Spirit: these are three princes of the world. — **Pele**, he who works wonders.

These names must be chosen [letter by letter] according to the work undertaken, for the accomplishment of which the help of God must be implored — in like manner to a certain text of Exodus, consisting of three verses: *[one is read] in one line, from left to right; [the second] from right to left, in reverse order; [the third again from left to right,] which constitutes the seventy-two letters of which the **Schemhamphoras** is composed*. Thus are the divine names created […]: the proper name of God, of which each of the [seventy-two] names […].

*[Here follows in the original the list of the seventy-two angels of the Schemhamphoras. This list is almost entirely destroyed in both scans — only a few fragments are legible (…Bariel, …Michael, …Jeliel, …Sitael, Elemiah…, …Behael, …Induel, …Rochel, …Jabamiah…); out of fidelity to the rule of inventing nothing, it is not reconstructed here. It may be found in the printed editions of the text.]*

*[Degraded passage; substance:]* He who prays these names with an upright and fervent soul […]: these are the praises of the sacred tongue, and whoever offers himself to God with these names […] such is our method of forming the divine names […]. The other method of making the Schemhamphoras consists in arranging the three verses with their letters, written one beneath the other, the first from right to left, [the second from left to right, the third from right to left] […].

One reads in the fifth book […] that God created the world by him, and that he penetrates into the twelve signs and through them into all the servants and instruments of God […]. He who fears God and keeps his commandments […] shall receive the blessing […].

*[Pages 63–64, degraded and then legible:]* […] Man is created in the image and likeness of God […]; under the favor of God, he shall obtain his desire through faith and wisdom: first, from the stars and the heavens, through the rational reflections of his mind; second, from the animal kingdom, through his senses; third, from the elements, through his fourfold body.

Therefore man binds all creatures by comparison, by invoking the higher power, through the name and the power which govern a thing, and then through the inferior things themselves.

And now, he who desires to become master of the operations of the soul must acquaint himself with the order of all things, as they are established by God in their proper state, from the highest to the lowest, by natural connections, so as to descend as by a ladder. It is on this point that the heathen committed the error of worshipping the planets and the fixed stars — not because they understood them, but because they were moved by the powers which governed them, and driven to it by the kinship of their founder and creator. In the same manner, the Christian nations have committed the error of paying homage to the departed saints and of giving to the creature the honor which belongs to the Creator alone; now God is a jealous God and does not permit the worship of idols. The prayer of faith, in proper language and for lawful objects, is therefore intimately bound to the name of God, from which we descend by words, from one to the other, following one another according to a natural relation, in order to accomplish something.

The son thus obtains from his father that he should support him — even though the father would not do it willingly, since he is his offspring, he must provide for his maintenance. How much greater must be the care which our heavenly Father feels for us, if we serve him as is fitting!

He who desires the influence of the sun must not merely direct his eyes toward it: he must raise the power of his soul toward the soul-power of the sun, which is God himself, having first made himself like unto God by fasting, purification and good works; he must also pray in the name of the Mediator, with fervent love toward God and his neighbor, in order to attain to the solar spirit, to be filled with its light and its brightness, which he may draw to himself from heaven — so as to be endowed with heavenly gifts and to obtain all the desires of his heart; and as soon as he grasps the higher light and attains to the state of perfection, being endowed with a supernatural intelligence, he shall also obtain supernatural strength and power. For this reason, without piety, man will deny his faith in Christ and become unacceptable to God, often falling a prey to the evil spirits, against which there is no better protection than the fear of the Lord and fervent love toward God and men.

Most of those who are skilled in divine works, and who possess the right to command the spirits, must be worthy by nature, or become so through the education and discipline proper to their vocation; they must keep all their works secret, but cannot conceal them from a true and pious person. The dignity of birth comes from condition; but it is to Saturn, the Sun, Mercury or Mars that he owes being made prosperous, being learned in physics, metaphysics and theology.

If a man has the knowledge of God as the first great cause, he must also recognize the other causes, or cooperating spirits, and determine what rank of dignity and honor to accord them — a knowledge without which their presence and their help cannot be obtained. Such honor must not be rendered for the love of the spirits, but for the love of their Lord, whose servants they are. In this manner the angels of God shall encamp round about those who fear and love the Lord; and, as Augustine says: “Every thing possesses a predestined angelic power.” It is for this reason that the Hebrew theologians, Mecubales and Kabbalists, have named ten principal divine names as members of God, and ten *Numerationes* or **Sephiroth** as garments and instruments of the Creator, by which he infuses himself into all his creatures, according to the order of the ten angelic choirs and the ten princely choirs of the spirits, from which all things derive their power and their quality.

### The Ten Divine Names and the Ten Sephiroth

**1. The name EHEIE** (*Eheie Asser Eheie*), its *numeratio* **Kether Elion**, “one Lord”: this is the most simple Deity, which no eye has seen; it is attributed to God the Father and gives its influence through the order of the Seraphim, in Hebrew *Haioth ha-Kadosch* — the gate of holiness or of life, which transmits life to every thing. Thence it flows through the *primum mobile*, so that all things must exist and the heavens accomplish their revolution every twenty-four hours. This wonderful being is called the intelligence **Metatron**, that is to say, “prince of faces”; his office is to lead the others into the presence of the Sovereign, and it is through him that God spoke to Moses.

**2. JEHOVAH, Jod**, or **Jah**; its *numeratio* **Chochma** — wisdom: the Deity full of spirit, the first-born Son by whom the Father has redeemed man from his curse; it infuses itself through the order of the Cherubim, in Hebrew *Ophanim*, “of forms or of Counsel.” Thence it flows into the starry heavens and there produces many figures — the chaos of creatures — through the particular intelligence **Raziel**, who was the representative [the guardian angel] of Adam.

**3. TETRAGRAMMATON ELOHIM**; its *numeratio* is called **Binah**, that is to say, prudence or sense, and signifies pardon and rest, gladness, repentance and conversion — the great trumpet, the redemption of the world and the life of the age to come. It befits the Holy Spirit and flows in its power through the order of the Thrones, called in Hebrew *Aralim* — the great, strong and mighty angels —, thence through the sphere of Saturn; it gives to liquid matter its form. Its intelligence **Zaphkiel** was the representative of Noah, and another intelligence, **Jophiel**, the representative of Shem. These are the three supreme and sovereign *Numerationes*, as a throne of the divine Persons, by whose orders all things come to pass, and which the seven others complete, called for this reason *Numerationes fabricae*.

**4. EL**; its *numeratio* **Hesed**, that is to say, grace or goodness: it is called mercy, piety, great power, scepter and right hand, and flows through the order of the Dominations, in Hebrew *Hasmalim*; it confers a peaceful justice through the sphere of Jupiter and dispenses in a general manner a special intelligence: **Zadkiel**, representative of Abraham.

**5. ELOHIM GIBOR**, the strong God, who punishes the fault of the wicked. Its *numeratio* is **Geburah**: power, gravity, strength, security, judgment. It inflicts chastisement by the sword and by war. To it are attached the judgment seat of God, the girdle of the Lord, a sword and a left arm; it is also called *Pachad*, that is to say, the fear before God. It flows through the order of the Powers, in Hebrew *Seraphim*, and thence through the sphere of Mars, which has great powers of war and affliction; and finally through the element of fire. Its particular intelligence is **Camael**, representative of Samson.

**6. ELOHA**, [God] of alchemy [?]; its *numeratio* is **Tiphereth**: grace, beauty, ornament, happiness and pleasure; it signifies the tree of life and flows through the order of the Virtues, in Hebrew *Malachim*; this angel, through the sphere of the Sun, gives prosperity and life, and produces the metals. Its particular intelligence, **Raphael**, was the representative of Isaac and of Tobias the younger, and **Peliel** the representative of Jacob.

**7. TETRAGRAMMATON SABAOTH**, or **Adonay Sabaoth**, the God of hosts; its *numeratio* is **Nezah**: triumph and victory. To it is attached the right-hand pillar; it signifies eternity, the justice of the avenging God; it flows through the order of the Principalities, in Hebrew *Elohim*, that is to say, the gods, into the sphere of Venus — love and justice; it produces all vegetable growth. Its particular intelligence is **Haniel**, and the angel **Cerviel**, representative of David.

**8. ELOHIM SABAOTH**, God of hosts — not of war nor of wrath, but of piety: it is from the bosom of mercy and goes before its armies. Its *numeratio* is called **Hod**: praise, confession, ornament and renown. To it is accorded the left-hand pillar; it flows through the order of the Archangels, [called in Hebrew] *Bene Elohim*, into the sphere of Mercury — ornament, safety and firmness — and produces the [inferior] metals. Its particular intelligence **Michael** was the representative of Solomon.

**9. SADAI**, the Almighty, who does all things in abundance, and **El-Hay**, that is to say, the living God. Its *numeratio* is called **Jesod**, that is to say, the foundation, and is termed good sense, redemption and rest. It flows through the order of the Angels, in Hebrew *Cherubim*, into the sphere of the Moon — which makes all things wax and wane —, sustains and contributes to the genius of men. Its intelligence **Gabriel** was the representative of Joseph, of Joshua and of Daniel.

**10. ADONAY MELECH**, that is to say, Lord and King. Its *numeratio* is called **Malchut**, that is to say, the kingdom and dominion; it is named the Church, the temple of God, and the gate. It flows through the order *Animasticum*, [the order] of the blessed souls, in Hebrew *Issim* — the life of the princes [?] —, inferior to the higher hierarchies. They give knowledge to the children of men of the wonderful things of learning and keep them by prophecies. Their *Anima Messiae* [soul of the Messiah], or according to others the intelligence **Metatron** — called the first creature, the work of the world —, is the representative of Moses, the fountain of all life.

These are, in summary, the [ten] names of God and the ten Sephiroth, embraced in the *Archetypum*.

### The Nine Choirs of the Angels

We must now consider the nine choirs of the angels, with the ten [orders]:

1. Seraphim; 2. Cherubim; 3. Thrones; 4. Dominations; 5. Powers; 6. Virtues; 7. Principalities; 8. Archangels; 9. Angels; [10. Blessed Souls].

The Hebrews therefore name them: *Haioth [ha-Kadosch]*; *Ophanim*; *Aralim*; *Hasmalim*; *Seraphim*; *Malachim*; *Elohim*; *Bene Elohim*; *Cherubim*; *[Issim]*.

Their representative angels are: **Metatron, Jophiel, Zaphkiel, Zadkiel, Camael [?], Raphael, Haniel, Michael, Gabriel, [the Messiah / the soul of the Messiah]**.

The nine choirs of the angels divide theology into three hierarchies.

In the **first hierarchy** are the Seraphim, Cherubim and Thrones. These more than celestial spirits are called gods, or sons of the gods, because they continually contemplate the order of divine providence. The first, [immersed] in the goodness of God, praise Him without ceasing and pray for us. The second [contemplate] the being of God according to form; the third [consider] how the divine decrees stand before God.

In the **middle hierarchy** are the Dominations, Powers and Virtues, as spirits of intelligence [which govern] the whole world. The first command and [distribute] what the others execute; the second [remove obstacles and repel] the enemies of God; the third work wonders and constantly accomplish great prodigies. [It is from these three] orders of spirits [that miracles descend].

In the **lower hierarchy** are the Principalities, Archangels and Angels, who are ministering spirits to watch over earthly affairs. The first, in general, watch over princes and magistrates and take care of kingdoms and countries, each in its own sphere, as Moses declares in his song: “When the Most High divided the world and its inheritance, when he separated the sons of Adam, he set the bounds of the people according to the number of the children of Israel.” And Daniel says (chap. 10): “But the prince of the kingdom of Persia withstood me one and twenty days.” And Jesus Sirach testifies that every nation has its appointed director; therefore the Israelites called upon the angel of their country. These are established to institute and regulate the worship of God among men, and take care of the prayers, the offerings and the piety of mortals. The [second and third] orders take charge of lesser aids, and each has its assigned office […].

It is for this reason that the fourth hierarchy [of souls] is added to the preceding ones, as souls of the celestial bodies: first [the souls] which receive the light from the higher degrees [heroes, virtuous men and martyrs?]. The first control the light and the influence of the stars, so that their power proceeds from God toward the lower regions. The second are the holy elect of the Redeemer. The third are the souls of the innocents and of the martyrs, and of the men of God who gave up the spirit amid pains and sufferings, for the love of God.

Now God the Father has given to the Son, our Mediator, Savior and Redeemer, all power [in heaven and on earth]; thus the angels of the great name of God and of Jesus — who is the first power in God — [receive his influx]: it flows into the twelve angels and the twelve signs, through which it spreads into the seven planets and, by a natural consequence, into all the other servants and instruments of God, until it penetrates into the lower regions — so that even an insignificant herb may develop a particular power, though it be withered; and thus the angel of man appears before God at all times, bearing his prayers into his presence.

Without the name of Jesus, the old Hebrew kabbalists can in our days accomplish nothing with the ancient arts as the Fathers employed them. Therefore all creatures fear and honor him. All men who believe in him are enlightened by his brightness; our souls are united to him, and the divine power which emanates from him is communicated to us.

## Of the Motion of the Celestial Powers

The first course in the *Mundo coelesti* watches day and night — *Primum Mobile*, [in Hebrew] *Reschit ha-Galgalim*; it continues from morning until evening. From this the heathen divided the angels into thirty-three orders. The first great light communicates light, life and condition from the first course, and opposes others [to it] in the sphere of the Zodiac; it causes summer and winter, the spring of all things of the elements — in Hebrew *Masloth*; it goes from evening to morning according to the twelve signs of the heavens.

But even though all things hold their existence from God, the great First Cause, we must not despise the other causes, according to the changes of the time, of the year, of the month, of the day and of the hour; nor yet consider these causes exclusively and forget God — for it was in this manner that heathen idolatry was instituted. […] The heathen found that the celestial spirits were not united to their bodies as our souls are united to ours, but that they could rejoice in the presence of God and prepare their bodies without great labor to work with the lower creatures of God. They regarded the celestial spirits as gods and conferred divine honors upon them. Very often the Jews turned away from God and worshipped the hosts of heaven, and the wrath of God was kindled against them. But by reason of the order of all things, God has placed them before us as his instruments, which we must regard, on account of their honorable office, as the noblest creation of God, and honor, after God, according to their rank — not as gods, but as creatures which he has established as twelve princes over the twelve gates of heaven, so that they may admit what they have received from the divine name, transposed twelve times.

Ezekiel writes: “The laws of the twelve tribes of Israel were thus written, and God Tetragrammaton reigned over them.” Likewise it is written in the Apocalypse that the stones of our heavenly city are planted in the ground, or that the Church of Christ is represented by the twelve names of the Apostles, including twelve angels, and among them the name of Jesus, who has received all power from the Father — so that the heavens shall receive what the angels give them, according to the will of God. If, therefore, an *Intelligentia* is attributed to each heaven, each star and each department of the heaven must have a distinct and separate power and influence, and consequently a distinct *Intelligentia*. There are therefore twelve princes of the angels, who represent the twelve signs of the Zodiac; thirty-six, who represent as many *Decuriae*; and seventy-two angels, who represent as many *Quinariae* of the heaven, of the seventy-two nations and languages of men. Likewise seven angels of the hosts for the seven heavens of the seven planets, to govern the world; and four angels who represent the *Triplicitates* of the twelve signs of the Zodiac and the four elements.

All have their names and their signs, which the philosophers used in their works — signs, images, garments, mirrors, rings, cards, figures of wax — as though they had before them a work of the sun; and they called them by the names of the sun and of its angels, and likewise of the others.

In the third place, they designated the lowest angels as servants. They distributed them over the world and named them after the seven planets; these have their special course according to the four elements and the four parts of the air and of the earth — several *Diurni* for the day, several *Nocturni*, several *Meridiani* — not that they are subordinate to the influence of the stars like the body which they represent, but because they are more akin than others to the nature of that astral body; otherwise they might be everywhere.

Every human being has three angels. For God has ordained that every human being should have his good angel as protector, who also strengthens the spirit and urges and exhorts us to that which is good and praiseworthy, so that we may flee from that which is evil. And likewise every man has also an evil spirit, which commands the desires of the flesh and awakens the lusts of the heart; between these two angels there is a constant struggle for supremacy, and the one to which man gives the preference shall carry off the victory: if the evil angel triumphs, man becomes his servant; if the good angel shows himself the stronger, he will purify the soul and save man from destruction. The angel and his impulses come from the stars. In the third place come the *Genii* of man, which govern the birth and are joined to every perfection in man. They are recognized by the star which is the Lord of the birth. The Chaldeans seek this *Genium* in the sun and the moon; the astronomers wish to draw the good *Genium* from the eleventh house, which they call for this reason *bonum Genium*, and the evil one from the sixth house. But each shall learn to know it by the natural inclination toward which he was borne from his youth. Therefore it is called the angel of birth, sent into the world by God. The Psalmist says of it: “Thou hast made the spirit of man a flame of fire.” For experience teaches us that the flame of fire and the spirit of birth may be separated without harm to man, and that we may learn hidden things from it, if it is good and true. […]

Moreover, God has endowed man with a divine character by the number **Pachad** — the sword of the left hand of God, by which man becomes a curse to all creatures; and on the other hand he has another character in the number of God **Hesed** — the right hand and the scepter of God, by which he finds favor in the eyes of God and of all his creatures. An evil conscience is man's judge, but a good conscience is his happiness. Thus, by the other divine numbers, and by the angels and the stars, man finds himself marked with signs and characters of conscience, which make him happy at one moment and unhappy at another.

For this reason, if a man has committed a murder, a theft, or any other act which his conscience condemns, he may be brought to confess his fault by the persistent invocation of the name of God, for his conscience will leave him no rest until he restores what he has stolen, or has suffered the punishment due to his crime. To this end: in the name of the Father, of the Son and of the Holy Ghost, take three small pieces of wood from the threshold of the door through which the thief passed in leaving the place where he committed the theft; place them in a wagon wheel, and say through the hub of the wheel the following words: “I pray thee, holy Trinity, to cause that he who has robbed me […] may have neither rest nor peace until he has restored to me what he has stolen.” Turn the wheel three times and replace it on the wagon. — Nevertheless, all pious Christians who have any concern for their future happiness must carefully avoid all superstitious matters, and refrain from employing unworthily the holy name of God, holding it in the greatest reverence, lest they draw down upon themselves eternal punishment.

If a man knows himself and understands that he is created in the image and likeness of God, he will recognize God the Creator before all things, and then the world and all its creatures. From the higher spirits, the angels and the heavens he holds his portion; and from the elements, the animals, the vegetation and the stones, he possesses within himself all that he desires to obtain.

If a man knows how to appropriate the particular place, the time, the order, the mass, the proportion and the mental organization of someone, he may attract and draw them to himself as the magnet attracts iron; but he must first be prepared, as the magnet must be shaped and charged. To this end, the soul must first be purified and consecrated to God by faith; a pure heart and a constant joy in the spirit are required. He must possess the love of God and of his neighbor; then he may attain to the perfect state and become like unto the Son of God; he shall be united to God and become again like unto him. It is given neither to the angels nor to any creature to be united to God, but to man alone; and he may become his son. When this is accomplished, so that he overcomes himself, he also overcomes and may draw to himself all other creatures and command their obedience.

But our spirit, our word and our act have no power in magic and knowledge, if they are not everywhere strengthened by the word of God, which we must hear often. We must pray to God without ceasing, lead a sober, temperate and spotless life; we must live in a continual state of repentance, give alms and succor the poor, for Christ did not say in vain: “Make to yourselves friends of the unrighteous riches, that they may receive you into the everlasting tabernacles” — that is to say: employ your wealth and your abundance in the support of the poor, so that they may receive from you their daily bread and be satisfied. Christ says: “What you have done unto the least of mine, you have done unto me.” These are the friends who shall lead us to the divine dwelling in heaven, where we shall receive a hundredfold and eternal life. Others, on the contrary, shall be cast out, for Christ also says: “I was hungry and thirsty, and you gave me neither to eat nor to drink; depart from me, workers of iniquity, into the outer darkness.”

Thus, by fasting, prayer, almsgiving, by preparing the hearts of believers for the temple, we may become co-heirs of the heavenly gifts, which the Most High shall confer upon us even in this life if we know how to use them as is fitting.

Since all things hold their life and their being from God, the proper name of each thing has been drawn from the being of that thing, and all things receive an influence from the Creator if they have been named in a fitting manner; for just as God produces all things through the influence of heaven and the operation of the planets, so the names of all things have been given according to some quality of the thing named, by him who counts the stars. Thus it was that God brought all creatures to Adam that he might name them, and their name indicated some particular quality or part which each possessed. Every name which has a meaning therefore shows, by comparison with the celestial influence, an inherent qualification of the object, although it is frequently changed. When, however, the two meanings of the name harmonize, the power of the will and the natural power become identical. Moreover, the celestial office to which man is ordained by God endows him with the power to confer life, and tells him what he must encourage, raise up or suppress in his own sphere, and to accomplish wonderful works with full devotion toward God.

## What Man Receives from the Order of Angels

Man is strengthened with a wondrous power by the order of angels, so that he declares the divine will.

From the **Seraphim**: that we cleave [to God] with fervent love.

From the **Cherubim**: the illumination of the mind, power and wisdom over the exalted figures and images, by which we may contemplate divine things.

From the **Thrones**: the knowledge of how we are made and constituted, so that we may direct our thoughts toward eternal things.

From the **Dominions**: assistance in subduing our daily enemies, whom we carry constantly with us, and in attaining salvation.

From the **Powers**: protection against the human enemies of life.

From the **Virtues**: God infuses into us the strength to combat the enemies of truth and of reward, so that we may complete the course of our natural life.

From the **Principalities**: that all things become subject to man, that he may seize all power and draw to himself all secret and supernatural knowledge.

From the **Archangels**: that he may rule over all that God has subjected to him — over the beasts of the field, over the fishes of the sea and over the birds of the air.

From the **Angels**, he receives the power to be the messenger of the divine will.

## What Man May Obtain from the Twelve Signs

As every creature receives from God its spirit, its number and its measure, so every creature has also its time.

In **Aries**, the plants of the earth receive new vigor, the trees their sap, and females become more fit to propagate the human race. In this sign, the fruitfulness of all creatures is bounded and regulated. It has Sunday for its particular time and end.
