In **Taurus**, all transactions and undertakings prosper and are favored, so that they advance according to the will of God; but to this end constant prayers are necessary, particularly on Sunday.

In **Gemini**, the angels have power over bodily changes and removals from one place to another, through the heavens and through the course of the stars; they have power over the movement of the waters in the rivers and in the sea, cause love between brothers, friends and neighbors, and give warnings against dangers, persons and objects.

In **Cancer** [the scan prints “Scorpio” by mistake], the angels rule over inheritances and riches, over treasures and treasure-seekers; they are fitted by nature to confer power, the art of speech, and to enlighten the mind in holy things, after the manner of the apostles in their unceasing prayers to God, at Pentecost.

In **Leo**, the angels have the power to move every living being, to multiply the species, to watch, and in certain ways to judge. And by the gift of God they confer *Physica*, *Medicina* and *Alchymia*.

In **Virgo**, the spirits have the power to overthrow kingdoms, to regulate all conditions, to discern between master and servant, to command evil spirits, to confer perpetual health, and to give to man *Musica*, *Logica* and *Ethica*.

In **Libra**, the angels hold from God a great power, inasmuch as the sun and the moon stand under this sign. Their power controls the friendship and enmity of all creatures; they have power over danger, war, quarrels and calumny; they lead armies into all quarters of the earth, cause rain, and give to man *Arithmetica*, *Astronomia*, *Geometria*.

In **Scorpio**, the angels have power over suffering and terror, over what man undertakes against God, over common privileges. They compel the conscience to obedience, and also force the demons to keep their engagements toward men, and reciprocally. They govern the life and death of all creatures, have power over departed souls, and give to man *Theologia*, *Metaphysica* and *Geomantia*.

In **Sagittarius**, they have power over the four elements, lead peoples from one distant country to another, regulate the changes of the elements and the propagation of animals.

In **Capricorn**, the angels give the high dignities of the world, honor and virtue, such as Adam enjoyed in Paradise in his innocence. They also enlighten the understanding and confer human reason.

In **Aquarius**, the angels keep man in good health and teach him what is harmful to him; they make him content, and teach him, by the command of God, the mysteries of heaven and of nature.

In **Pisces**, the angels compel the evil spirits to submit to man; they protect the pious, so that the great enemy may not harm them.

## The Twelve Signs Are Divided into Four Triplicities

The twelve angels who represent the twelve signs are called [in the Apocalypse]: **Malchidael, Asmodel, Ambriel, Muriel, Verchiel, Hamaliel, Zuriel, Barbiel, Adnachiel, Hanael, Gambiel, Barchiel**. Moreover, the angels also received names from the stars over which they rule, like the twelve signs: **Teletial, Zariel, Tomimil, Sartimel, Ariel, Batuliel, Masniel, Acrabiel, Chesetiel, Gediel, Doliel, Dagymel** — which comes to the same thing, expressed in Latin: *Ariel, Tauriel, Geminiel, Cancriel, Leoniel, Virginiel, Libriel, Scorpiel, Sagitariel, Capriel, Aquariel, Pisciel.*

This method of obtaining all manner of things by a particular power, in the twelve signs, is described in many kinds of books. The seal of *Hermes* teaches how the powers of celestial influence may be obtained under each sign in a crystal or a gem, when they are constellated; and then, at each period of the twelve signs, the appropriate character of each divides into four parts, each represented by an angel. Thus it was that each of the twelve stones of the breastplate of office of Aaron (of Solomon) was constellated; and the Amorites possessed a constellated stone for each idol, and to this end they consecrated the book.

Moreover, King Solomon teaches a hidden *Almadel*, or geometrical figure bearing upon the twelve signs of heaven, which he calls “altitudes,” and he gives to each altitude seven or eight names of princes. There are also many other methods of seeking the powers of heaven in the twelve signs, which for good reasons must not be divulged, because they are not mentioned in the Holy Scriptures and were kept secret.

## The Planets Have Seven Altitudes and Seven Angels

The altitudes are named as follows:

1. **Samaym.** 2. **Raaquin.** 3. **Saaquin.** 4. **Machonon.** 5. **Mathey.** 6. **Sebul.** 7. **Arabat.**

Of the operations of these and of their angels — office, order, number and measure —, an account is found in a work of **Raziel**, which constitutes the Sixth Book *Physicum Salomonis*, and in the *Elementa Magica* of Peter of Abano (page 674). From this book the book of the angel *Tractatus* draws its source. (Cf. Cornelius Agrippa, book 3, *Philosophia Occulta*, pages 377, 375.)

There are seven exalted Angels of the Throne, who execute the commands of the *Potestates*, namely:

1. **Ophaniel.** 2. **Tychagara.** 3. **Barael.** 4. **Quelamia.** 5. **Asazimur.** 6. **Paschar.** 7. **Boel.**

These are named after the name of God by which they were created, and belong to the first heaven — **Schamaym**, [whose angel is] **Gabriel**.

The second heaven, **Raaquin**, has twelve lords, or twelve altitudes of angels, placed above all: **Zachariel, Raphael**.

The third heaven, **Saaquin**, has three princes: **Jabniel, Rabacyel, Dalquiel**; they rule over fire, and each has his subordinate angel. The chief prince of the angels in this altitude is called **Anahel** [Avahel].

The fourth heaven, **Machon**, by its angels conducts the sun by day, and by other angels by night. The chief angel is called **Michael**.

The fifth heaven, **Mathey** (otherwise Machon), has for its prince **Samael**, served by two million angels. These are distributed among the four quarters of the world, three in each quarter, who control the twelve months; and above them are twelve chief angels.

The sixth heaven, **Zebul**, has for its prince **Zachiel**, with two million angels. The angel **Zebul** is placed above them during the day, and another angel, **Sabath**, during the night. They rule over kings, create fear, and give protection against enemies.

**Arabath**, the seventh heaven, has for its prince the angel **Cassiel**.

The names of the angels of the seven planets are the following: **Zaphiel** (Saturn), **Zadkiel** (Jupiter), **Camael** (Mars), **Raphael** (Sun), **Haniel** (Venus), **Michael** (Mercury), **Gabriel** (Moon).

There are seven princes who stand continually before God, to whom are given the spirit-names of the planets: **Sabathiel, Zedekiel, Madimiel, Semeliel** (or Semishia), **Nogahel, Cochabiath** (or Cochabiel), **Jareahel** (or Jevanael). For the planets are called for themselves:

**Sabachay** [Saturn], by which God sends famine and tribulation upon the earth.

**Zedeck** [Jupiter], through him come honor and favor, the right and the holiness of man.

**Madym** [Mars], through him anger, hatred, lies and war.

**Hamma** [Sun], from him come light and the power to distinguish between time and life.

**Noga** [Venus], from him food and drink, love and consolation.

**Cochab** [Mercury], from him proceed all trade and commerce.

**Lavanah** [Moon], causes all things to wax and wane.

I, Solomon, acknowledge that in the hours of Sabachay and Madym it is toilsome to labor, but that in the hours of Zedeck and of Noga labor is light; during the other hours, labor is middling — sometimes good, occasionally bad.

Some authors, such as Cornelius Agrippa (*Occult Philosophy*, book 3, chap. 16), call the seven regents of the world by other names, distributed according to the powers of other stars: **Orphiel, Zachariel, Samael, Michael, Anael, Raphael, Gabriel**; and each of them governs the world three hundred and fifty-four years and four months. Some give to the angelic year three hundred and sixty-five years — as many years as there are days in our year; others, one hundred and forty-five years. *Apac: viginti unum Spiritus, Septem in Conspectu Dei Throni sunt quos reperi etiam praesidere Planetis.*

The names of the seven angels of the seven heavens must be pronounced first, and afterward the names of those who preside over the seven planets, the seven days of the week, the seven metals, the seven colors; and these must be pronounced in the morning of each day of the week.

### Invocation of the Angels

O ye angels aforesaid, ye who execute the commands of the Creator: be pleased to be present with me in the work which I have undertaken at this time, and help me to complete it; be my attentive hearers and my assistants, so that the honor of God and my own good may be promoted.

Above, there are twenty-eight angels who rule over the twenty-eight mansions of the moon, namely: **Asariel, Cabiel, Dirachiel, Scheliel, Amnediel, Amixiel, Ardesiel, Neriel, Abdizriel, Jazeriel, Cogediel, Ataliel, Azerniel, Adriel, Amutiel, Iciriel, Bethnael, Odiel, Mequiel, Abrinael, Ariel, Tagriel, Abheiel, Amnixiel.** And each moon has its own guardian and regent, described in book 2 of the *Raziel*.

A man must also know how to divide the months, the days and the hours into four parts, for God has ordained that all things are best accomplished on suitable days and at proper hours.

The angels placed over the four parts of heaven are: **Scamym, Gabriel, Cabrael, Adrael, Madiel, Boamiel**;

**Alscius, Loquel, Zaniel, Hubaiel, Baccanael, Janael, Carpatiel**;

**Elael, Unael, Wallum, Vasans, Hiaijel, Usera, Staijel**;

**Ducaniel, Baabiel, Barquiel, Hanun, Anael, Nahijmel.**

In the second heaven, Raaquin, the following angels serve: **Nathan, Catroije, Betabbat**; **Yeseraije, Vuacon**; **Thiel, Jareael, Yanael, Venetal, Vebol, Abuionij, Vetameil**; **Milliel, Nelepa, Baliel, Calliel, Holij, Batij, Jeli.**

There are also, over the four quarters of the globe, four great angels:

Over the winds of the morning rules **Michael**.

Over the winds of the evening rules **Raphael**.

Over the winds of midnight rules **Gabriel**.

Over the winds of noon rules **Nariel**, or **Uriel**.

**The angels of the elements are:** of the air, **Cherub**; of the water, **Tharsis**; of the earth, **Ariel**; of the fire, **Seraph** (or **Nathaniel**).

These are all great princes, and each has under him many legions of angels; they have great power in the government of their planets, times, signs of the year, of the month, of the day and of the hour, and in their part of the world and of the wind.

In the third heaven, Saaquin, the angels are: **Sarquiel, Quadissu, Caraniel, Turiescorat, Amael, Husael**; **Turiel, Coniel, Babiel, Kadie, Maltiel, Hufaltiel**; **Faniel, Peneal, Penac, Raphael, Carniel, Deramiel**; **Porna, Sadiel, Kyniel, Samuel, Vascaniel, Famiel.**

In the fourth heaven, Machon, the angels of the divisions serve: **Carpiel, Beatiel, Baciel, Ragoel, Altel, Fabriel, Vionatraba**; **Anahel, Pabliel, Ustael, Burcat, Suceratos, Cababili.**

In the fifth heaven, Machijn, the following angels serve in four divisions: **Friagne, Cnael, Damael, Calzas, Arragon**; **Lacana, Astagna, Lobquin, Soncas, Jael, Jasiael, Nael**; **Rahumiel, Jahyniel, Rayel, Seraphiel, Mathiel, Serael**; **Sacriel, Maianiel, Gadiel, Hosael, Vianiel, Erastiel.**

In the sixth heaven, Zebul, and the seventh, Arabat, [the angels preside] above the fifth heaven.

If no *Spiritus Aeris* nor any division is to be found, then pronounce, toward the four quarters of the world, the following words:

“O great God, exalted and adored from all eternity!

O wise God, day and night I pray to thee, O most merciful God: that I may complete my work today, and understand it perfectly, through our Lord Jesus Christ, thou who livest and reignest, true God from eternity to eternity!

O strong God, mighty and without end!

O mighty and merciful God!”

On Saturday, invoke God by the words which he gave in Paradise, in which is the name of God:

“O holy and merciful God of Israel, the highest terror and fear of Paradise, Creator of heaven and earth” (as above).

✠ *Quaere hoc signum.* [Seek this sign.]

---

**End of the First Division.**

---

# OF THE USE OF THE PSALMS (SCHIMMUSCH TEHILLIM)

## Introduction

**Of the use of the Psalms for the bodily welfare of man.** A fragment drawn from the practical Kabbalah, accompanied by an extract from some other kabbalistic writings. With five illustrations on four plates.

Translated by Godfrey Selig, Lect. Publ. Acad. Lips.: 1788.

This eminent editor and translator rigorously insists upon this point: only persons of a moral character can hope for success in the use of the method set forth hereafter.

### From the Translator’s Preface

It cannot be denied that true kabbalists, wise and enlightened, lived in former times, and that some still live. But these do not go wandering from place to place, offering their art for sale in order to amass riches; they are content to dwell peacefully in the pillared palace of Solomon, where they labor without respite to gather divine wisdom, so that (as they express it) they may at last become worthy to receive the hidden gifts from on high. I myself know such a man, who drew from the Kabbalah a lofty wisdom and who, notwithstanding his extreme poverty, never undertakes a kabbalistic operation for money. When I asked him one day why he had refused to write the amulet desired by a noble lord, who offered him a large sum for his services, he answered me with a saying from the celebrated Pirke Awoth (Extract or Fragment of the Fathers):

“Deitschtammasch Betaggo Chalof,” that is to say: “Whoever accepts the crown for wages shall perish suddenly. For all the money in the world, I would not do such a thing. But if I can thereby succor my neighbor in need, then I will do what I can, trusting in the almighty power of the Most Holy, without expecting any reward. As for my necessary sustenance, I have no concern for it, for the Almighty has means to support me, if I trust in him. Does he not take care of the sparrow itself?”

It is particularly remarkable that the greatest and most genuine kabbalists of the Jewish nation were almost all followers and disciples of the blessed Savior of the world, and that they still are, as I can prove satisfactorily by numerous passages from their writings and their prayers. Let this suffice for this kind of men. But that kabbalists have lived and still live who devoted themselves to experiments, who accomplished wondrous works and who will accomplish them still, is likewise an undeniable fact, unless we are prepared to condemn everything that men renowned for their wisdom have ever said upon this subject.

The celebrated and famous Prussian hussar, the Lord of Archenwood, declares in a description of London that he saw in that city a man by the name of Doctor Falcon, known to be a great kabbalist, and who is visited and consulted by the most honorable and most intelligent persons of London. He relates further that this same Doctor Falcon formerly lived in Brussels under the name of the Jew Chayim Schmul Falk, who, according to the testimony of the French Duke of Nancy, in his published memoirs on kabbalistic operations, performed the most astonishing wonders.

I confidently hope, and I can assure it without hesitation, that this little book can have no tendency to nourish superstition. Even supposing that one of my readers should choose to employ one of the methods described in these pages in order to attain a desired end: his eagerness to satisfy his curiosity will soon vanish when he takes into consideration the hard conditions and the strict morality which are required for its use, if he wishes to derive any benefit from it or to find success therein.

Before concluding my preface, it is necessary to give the reader some instructions concerning the arrangement of this volume. We find in it, for example, isolated words, names, sentences, and even entire experiments, printed in the Hebrew and Chaldean languages. This fact should deter no one from purchasing the book, for all the words printed in Hebrew and Chaldean which must be impressed upon the mind are also printed in English *[the language of the 1880 edition]*, in plain terms, and they have been carefully translated. As for the Hebrew passages, the meaning of each passage and of each experiment follows immediately in English, or else is placed beneath the Hebrew expression. I have adopted this arrangement in order to comply with the request of a number of eminent persons, so as to attest that the translation is genuine and correct. The chapter and verse of Holy Scripture where all the passages cited may be found are likewise recorded with exactness.

### Extract from the Preface of the Kabbalistic Editor

It is universally known and acknowledged that we are named after the most holy name of the Master of the world, and that we have received from him the holy Decalogue, or written law. It is furthermore well known that, besides the laws which he gave to Moses engraved upon stone, he also gave him certain verbal laws during his long sojourn upon Mount Sinai, where there were entrusted to him all the doctrines, the explanations of the mysteries, the holy names of God and of the angels, and particularly the manner of applying this knowledge to the best interest of man. All these doctrines, which God declared good, but which were not made public, and which in the course of time received the name of Kabbalah or Tradition, Moses communicated during his lifetime to Joshua; Joshua transmitted them to the elders; the elders gave them to the judges, and from the judges they descended to the prophets. The prophets entrusted them to the men of the great synagogue, and these gave them to the wise men; and thus the Kabbalah was handed down from one to another — from mouth to mouth — until the present day. Therefore we know that in the Torah are found many names of the Most High and of his angels, besides profound mysteries which may be applied to the welfare of man, but which, on account of the perversity of mankind and to guard them against all abuse, have been hidden from the great mass of human beings.

All that I have advanced here is as clear as the sun and needs no further proof; and it is equally clear and incontestable that the All-Merciful gave the Torah, from the beginning, to serve at all times the best interests of the soul and body of man. Therefore God has endowed it with talents, powers and virtues so exalted that, by a rational use which he makes of it, man can protect himself from danger when no other help is at hand, and save himself simply by pronouncing the words of the living God. It is for this reason that the expression “For it is thy life” recurs so frequently in the Torah. And Solomon says in his Proverbs, VI, 22: “When thou goest, it shall lead thee; and when thou sleepest, it shall keep thee.” That the Psalms and the Torah are equal in holiness and dignity, no one will call in question. Our wise men declare: “He who wishes to live each day nearer to God, who deserves to unite his soul with him, and who is disposed to live in the closest communion with him, must often pray the Psalms with fervor and devotion. Happy the man who does so every day and at every hour, for his reward shall be great.” The Psalms are formed and divided into five books, just like the Torah. We may therefore rely implicitly upon the doctrines of the enlightened kabbalists when they affirm that the Almighty has granted to the Psalms the same talents and the same powers as to the Torah, and that in them are hidden many names of the most high Majesty of God and of his angels, besides many mysteries.

Yes, dear reader, you must not doubt it: by a pious life and by a rational use of the Psalms, you may obtain the grace of God, the favor of princes and magistrates, and the love of your fellow men. You will be in a position to protect yourself from danger, to escape suffering and to labor for your own welfare.

That all this is true, the contents of the prayer with which we conclude each psalm, and which we are in duty bound to pray, will amply demonstrate. But the correctness of it is still better established by the teachings of the Talmud and of the ancient sages, who assure us that many of our illustrious ancestors availed themselves from time to time of apparently supernatural means to protect their best interests. I can establish the truth of this by the most trustworthy witnesses; I could even name some great men who, by a prayerful use of the Psalms, accomplished great works. Such examples are rare. Let it suffice for me to present to you a few passages drawn from authoritative works, by which you will be fully convinced that the Almighty has given to his revealed word true and unexampled talents and powers, and that, in a case of extreme necessity, we are permitted to make use of this gift of God for our own good and that of our neighbor: for example, to drive away evil spirits, to relieve a deep melancholy and to cure grave diseases; to deliver prisoners unjustly incarcerated; to stop and repel enemies, adversaries, murderers and highway robbers; to extinguish the most violent fires; to withstand floods, to defend innocence and to bring it to light, and to promote in a general way good fortune, welfare and peace.

Read the treatise which the excellent Rabbi Schimschon bar Abraham devoted to this subject, in his book entitled “Responsiones Raschaba”; examine the words of the enlightened Rabbi Jochanan ben Sackai in his treatise of the Talmud and of the Sanhedrin, chapter 2, where he treats of magical conjurations, and where he affirms and proves that it is permitted, in dangerous and incurable diseases, to make use of words and passages of Holy Scripture for their cure. You will find more or less similar references in the treatise of the Sabbath of the Talmud, as well as in the Responsonibus of Zemach, son of Simonis, where the ninety-second Psalm, accompanied by certain prescriptions, is highly recommended as a sure means of avoiding suffering and danger, even in case of war, fire and other similar circumstances, enabling us to escape from them unharmed, free, safe and unhindered.

In such happy circumstances, it is assuredly right and proper that so salutary a knowledge — which until this day was known only to few men, and even then only to the learned; free to all, however, but to be found only in the libraries and cabinets of the great, without being generally known — should at least, in some measure, be brought to light.

However, since I cannot attain my purpose otherwise than by delivering these pages to the world in printed form, and since they will inevitably fall into impure hands, I find myself compelled, in order to prevent an unworthy use of them, to extend this preface — which otherwise might very fittingly have ended here — in order to lay down some rules and some limits. Do not let yourself be discouraged by this, however, for I am really endeavoring to serve your best interests and to preserve you from all harm.

1. If you are disposed to avail yourself of the means indicated, I warn you to attempt it only in case of extreme necessity, and when no other help is at hand.

2. If such is the case, place, in operating, your trust in the goodness and power of the most high and forever blessed God, whom you have perhaps until now invoked under a holy name which remained unknown to you.

3. The psalm ordained for such or such an undertaking, besides the appropriate prayer, you must pray before God with a broken and contrite heart, and moreover keep in mind the holy name which is joined to it, with its letters, as the wise kabbalists give them. At the same time, you must have your undertaking continually before your eyes.

4. I must tell you, if you wish to console yourself with this help: you must live in such a manner that no crime nor willful sin troubles your conscience, for it is well known that the prayer of the ungodly is not acceptable to God. And herewith I commend you to the protection of the Most High.

## Of the Use and Efficacy of the Psalms, and of the Many Ends to Which They May Be Applied

### Psalm 1

When a woman is with child and fears a premature delivery or a dangerous confinement, she must write, or cause to be written, upon a piece of parchment prepared from the pure skin of a deer, the first three verses of the above psalm, with the holy hidden name and the appropriate prayer which are contained therein, place the whole in a small bag made expressly for this purpose, and hang it by a cord about her neck, so that the bag rests against her bare body.

The holy name is called Eel Chad, which signifies great, strong and only God; it is drawn from the following four words: Aschre, verse 1; Lo, verse 4; Jatzliach, verse 3; Vederech, verse 6.

The prayer is the following: May it please thee, O Eel Chad, to grant to this woman, N., daughter of R., that she may have, neither at this time nor at any other, a premature delivery; grant her rather a truly happy confinement, and preserve in good health both her and the fruit of her body. Amen! Selah!

**Translator’s admonition.** Before continuing the translation of the Psalms, it is necessary to insert here an admonition which the author, who wrote only for his own nation, deemed superfluous, but which must nevertheless be addressed to everyone.

“Every human being,” says the celebrated kabbalist Rabbi Isaac Loria, “except the ignorant idolater alone, may, by a pious and virtuous life, enter the consecrated temple of the true Kabbalah, and avail himself of its benefits without being able to speak or understand the Hebrew language. He may pray, read and write everything in his mother tongue; only, the holy names of God and of the angels which may occur in the experiment must, in every circumstance, be written and retained in the mind in the Hebrew language (for they must in no case be uttered); otherwise, a false direction might easily be given to the experiment, which would consequently lose all its holiness, its value and its efficacy.”

With this pronunciation we must all be well content; therefore I must write in Hebrew all the similar words and names whose letters compose the holy names. So that the reader may nevertheless read in his mind all the names and words which will thus present themselves, and retain them, I have written all the Hebrew words in Latin letters, with their meaning.
### Psalm 2

Should you be exposed to danger in a storm at sea, and your life be threatened, recite this Psalm without delay and with becoming reverence, and think respectfully upon the most holy name that it contains, namely Schaddei (which signifies mighty God); then pronounce at once the prayer belonging thereto, after which write the whole together upon a potsherd and, in full confidence in the Almighty who sets bounds to the sea and restrains its power, cast it into the foaming waves: you shall see marvellous wonders, for the billows will instantly cease their roaring and the storm will be stilled.

The words whose letters constitute this holy name are taken from Rageschu, verse 1; Nossedu, verse 2; and Jozes, verse 9.

The prayer is as follows: “Let it be, O Schaddei (almighty God!), thy holy will, that the raging of the storm and the roaring of the waves be stilled, and that the proud billows be calmed. Lead us, O all-merciful Father, to the place of our destination, safe and sound, for with thee alone are power and might. Thou alone canst help, and thou wilt surely help, for the honor and glory of thy name. Amen! Selah!”

This Psalm is also an effectual remedy against violent headaches. The prescription is as follows: write the first eight verses of this Psalm, together with the holy name and the appropriate prayer, upon pure parchment, and hang it about the neck of the patient; then pray over him the Psalm with the prayer appointed for it. Do this in humble devotion, and the patient will be relieved.

### Psalm 3

Whosoever is subject to violent pains of the head and of the back, let him pray this Psalm, with the holy directing names and the appropriate prayer that it contains, over a small quantity of olive oil, and let him anoint therewith the head or the back during the very act of prayer: he will receive immediate relief therefrom. The holy name is Adon (Lord), and it is found in the words Weatta, verse 3; Baadi, verse 3; Hekizoti, verse 5; and Hascheini, verse 7. The prayer is as follows: Adon (Lord) of the world, may it please thee to be my physician and my help. Heal me and deliver me from my violent pains of the head and of the back, for I can find help nowhere but with thee, and with thee alone are counsel and action. Amen! — Selah! — Selah!

### Psalm 4

Should you have been unlucky hitherto, in spite of all your efforts, pray this Psalm three times before the rising of the sun, with humility and devotion, impressing at the same time upon your mind its holy directing name, and saying each time the appropriate prayer, trusting in the help of the mighty Lord, without whose will not the least creature can perish. Then go in peace to carry out the undertaking you meditate, and all things will turn out to your entire satisfaction.

The holy name is called Jiheje (He is and he will be), and it is composed of the four final letters of the words Teppillati, verse 2; Selah, verse 5; Jehovah, verse 6; and Toschiweni, verse 9. The prayer is as follows: May it please thee, O Jiheje, to make my ways, my steps and my doings prosper. Grant that my desire may be amply fulfilled, and that my wishes may be satisfied this very day, for the sake of thy great, mighty and adorable name. Amen! — Selah!

Should you wish to accomplish your undertaking through the agency of another, proceed in all things as has been said above, with this exception only, that you must alter the prayer as follows: Let me find grace, favor and mercy in the eyes of N., son of R., so that he may grant my request, etc.

Should you have a cause to plead before high magistrates or princes, you must pray this Psalm, together with the closing prayer appointed for it, seven times in succession before the rising of the sun.

Let me be permitted to insert here another caution. When it is said: N., son or daughter of N., it is to be understood that we must first mention the name of the person by whom we wish to be served, and then the name of his mother; for example: Isaac, son of Sarah, or Dinah, daughter of Leah.

### Psalm 5

Should you have business to transact with your magistrates or with your princes, and desire to obtain their particular favor, pray this Psalm early in the morning, at the rising of the sun, and in the evening, at the setting of the sun. Do this three times over pure olive oil, thinking without ceasing upon the holy name of Chananjah (merciful God); anoint with this oil your face, your hands and your feet, and say: Be merciful unto me, for the sake of thy great, adorable and holy name, Chananjah; turn the heart of my prince toward me, and grant that he may look upon me with a favorable eye; cause me to find grace and courtesy with him. Amen! — Selah!
The holy name is found in the words Chapez, verse 5; Nechini, verse 9; Nechona, verse 10; Hadichemo, verse 12; and Kazlnna, verse 14.

Yet another peculiarity of this Psalm: should you find that, notwithstanding the greatest industry and the greatest care, your affairs do not prosper, and should you have reason to fear that an evil Masal — that is to say, an evil star, an evil spirit or an evil destiny — is opposed to you, then pray this Psalm daily, down to the last verse, with great devotion, and you will soon find yourself in more favorable circumstances.

### Psalm 6

By this Psalm all diseases of the eyes may be healed. Read the Psalm three days in succession, and pray the prescribed prayer seven times, slowly, in a low voice and with devotion, keeping continually in mind the holy name of Jeschajah (which signifies: help is with the Lord); believe, without doubting, that the Lord can and will help you. The prayer is as follows: Jehovah, my Father, may it please thee, for the sake of the great, mighty, holy and adorable name Jeschajah Baal Hatschna — that is to say: help is with the Lord (for he is the Lord of help, he can help) —, which name is contained in this Psalm, to heal me of my diseases, of my infirmities and of the pain of my eyes; for with thee are power and help, and thou alone art mighty enough to help; of this I am certain, and therefore I put my trust in thee. Amen! — Selah!
It is further said: should a traveller meet with danger by land or by sea, let him pray, when no other help is to be hoped for, this Psalm seven times, and each time with full confidence in the mighty and sure help of the Almighty, adding thereto: Jeschajah, Lord of help! let it be thy holy will and thy good pleasure to assist me in this extremity and to turn this danger away from me. Hear me for the sake of thy great and most holy name, for with thee are power and help. Amen! — Selah!
The five letters of this holy name contain, according to the prayer, the words: Jehovah al, verse 2; Schuba, verse 6; Oschescha, verse 8; Bewoschu and Vejibbahalu, verse 11.

### Psalm 7

When wicked men conspire to make you unhappy, if your enemies watch for an opportunity to overthrow you, if they pursue you in order to injure you, then take, on the very spot where you stand, a handful of earth or of dust, pray this Psalm and keep in mind the holy name of Eel Elijon — great, strong and most high God! — then cast the dust in the direction of your enemies while pronouncing the prayer prescribed for this case, and you will see that your enemies will cease their persecutions and leave you in peace. The letters of the holy name are found in the words: Aisher, verse 1; Ode, verse 18 (according to the order called Al Bam, and the letters must be transposed); Hoschieni, verse 2; Eli, verse 7; Jadin, verse 9; Jaschuf, verse 13; Elijon, verse 18.

The prayer is as follows: O Eel Elijon, great, strong and most high God! may it please thee to change the hearts of my enemies and of my adversaries, so that they may do me good instead of evil, as thou didst in the days of Abraham, when he called upon thee by this holy name (Gen. XIV, 22). Amen! — Selah!
Should you have incurred the ill will of an enemy whose cunning, power and vengeance you have reason to dread, you must fill a pot with fresh water drawn from the well, and pronounce over it the last twelve verses of this Psalm, namely the words: “Arise, Jehovah, in thine anger!” Pronounce them four times, thinking at the same time upon the holy name of Eel Elijon and upon your enemy, and pray each time: “Humble and overthrow, O Eel Elijon, my enemy, N., son of R., so that he may not have the power to provoke me nor to injure me.” Amen! After this prayer, pour the water upon a spot near the dwelling of your enemy, or in a place where he will have to pass over it, and thereby you will overcome him.

Should you have a cause to be tried before the court, and have reason to fear an unfavorable or partial verdict, then pray this Psalm slowly before appearing in the presence of the judge, thinking at the same time upon Eel Elijon and upon the justice of your cause; and as you approach the judge, pray thus: O Eel Elijon! turn the heart of the judge in favor of my best interests, and grant that I may be fully justified at my departure. Give my words force and vigor, and cause me to find favor. Amen! — Selah!
### Psalm 8

Should you wish to secure to yourself the love and good will of all men in your business dealings, you must pray this Psalm three days in succession after the setting of the sun, thinking continually upon the holy name of Rechmial, which signifies great and strong God of love, of grace and of mercy. Pronounce each time the appropriate prayer over a small quantity of olive oil, and anoint therewith the face, as well as the hands and the feet. The letters composing the holy name are found in the words: Addir, verse 2; Jareach, verse 4; Adam, verse 5; Melohim, verse 6; Tamschilehu, verse 7. The prayer reads as follows: May it please thee, O Rechmial Eel, to grant that I may obtain love, grace and favor in the eyes of men, according to thy holy will. Amen! — Selah!
### Psalm 9

The principal property of this Psalm, according to the precept, is to be an infallible remedy for the restoration of male children of delicate health, when no medicines nor help are within reach. This Psalm should also be prayed against the power and malignity of enemies. In the first case, write this Psalm, with its holy name, upon pure parchment, with a new pen, and hang it about the neck of the patient. Then repeat the prayer with reverence, thinking at the same time upon the holy name of Eheje Ascher Eheje — that is to say: I am he who will be —, and pronounce the following prayer: All-merciful Father! for the sake of thy mighty, adorable and holy name, Eheje Ascher Eheje, may it please thee to take from N., son of R., the disease [name the disease here] from which he suffers, and to deliver him from his pains. Make him whole in soul, in body and in mind, and preserve him, during his life, from all plagues, from all injury and from all danger, and be thou his help. Amen.

In the second case, repeat this Psalm and pray devoutly: May it be agreeable to thy will, for the sake of thy most holy name Eheje Ascher Eheje, to deliver me from the power of my enemies and of my adversaries, and to protect me from their persecutions, as thou didst once protect the Psalmist from the enemies who pursued him. Amen. — Selah!

The letters of this holy name are in the words: Ode, verse 2; Haojeff, verses 7 and 16; and, according to the alphabetical order, in the At Basch.

### Psalm 10

Should anyone be tormented by an unclean, restless and malignant spirit, let a new earthen pot be filled with spring water and let pure olive oil be poured into it, in the name of the patient; then let this Psalm be pronounced over it nine times, keeping constantly in mind the adorable name of Eel Mez, which signifies strong God of the oppressed, and saying at each ending of the Psalm: Let it be thy most holy will, O Eel Mez, to heal the body and the soul of N., son of R., and to deliver him from all his plagues and oppressions; be pleased to strengthen him in soul and in body, and to deliver him from evil. Amen! — Selah!

The holy name may be found in the words: Alah, verse 6; Lamma, Anawin, verse 16; and Haasez, verse 17.

### Psalm 11

Whosoever prays this Psalm daily with feelings of devotion, keeping constantly in mind the holy name of Pele, that is to say Wonderful, and who moreover addresses to God a suitable prayer, will be safe from all persecution and will have no great evil to fear.

The holy name is in the words: Ofel, verse 2; Paal, verse 3; and Adam. The closing prayer may be as follows: Adorable, mighty and holy God Pele! with thee are counsel, action and power, and thou alone canst do wonders. Turn away from me all that is evil, and protect me from the persecution of wicked men, for the sake of the great name Pele. Amen. — Selah.

### Psalm 12

This Psalm possesses a power, an action and a value similar to those of the preceding one. The holy name is Aineel, which signifies strong God! my Father! and it is found in the words of the sixth verse: Ewjonim, Akum, Lo. The prayer is as follows: Almighty Father, my God Aineel! grant that all the conspiracies plotted against me may be brought to naught; turn away from me all danger and all injury, for thine are the kingdom and the power. Amen. — Selah!

### Psalm 13

Whosoever prays this Psalm daily with devotion, together with the prayer belonging to it, thinking at the same time upon the mighty name of Essiel — that is to say: my help is the mighty God —, will be safe, for the following twenty-four hours, from an unnatural death and from all bodily sufferings and punishments. The prayer is as follows: Protect me, according to thy good will and thy good pleasure, from a violent, sudden and unnatural death, and from all other fatal accidents and grave bodily afflictions, for thou art my help and my God, and thine are the power and the glory. Amen. — Selah.

According to tradition, this Psalm is also a good remedy against dangerous and painful diseases of the eyes. The patient must procure a plant that is good for the eyes, and with it pray this Psalm, accompanied by a suitable prayer, trusting firmly in the certain help of the mighty Essiel; then let him bind the plant upon his eyes. The letters composing this holy name are contained in the words: Ezoth, verse 3; Mismor, verse 1; Jarum, verse 3; Aeni, verse 4; Ojewi, verse 5; and Jagel, verse 6.

### Psalm 14

Whosoever prays this Psalm with childlike faith, trusting in the most holy name of Eel Emmet — that is to say the true God, or God of truth —, and recites daily the prayer belonging to it, will find favor with all men and will be safe from slander and from distrust. The prayer is as follows: “May it please thee, O Eel Emmet, to grant me grace, love and favor with all men whose help I need. Grant that all may believe my words, and that no slander may avail anything against me to deprive me of the confidence of men. Thou canst do this, for thou turnest the hearts of men according to thy holy will, and liars and slanderers are an abomination unto thee. Hear me for the sake of thy name. Amen. — Selah!”

The letters composing this holy name are found in the words: Elohim, verse 1; Maskil, verse 2; […], verse 3; Ammi, verse 4; and […], verse 5.

### Psalm 15

Against the presence of an evil spirit, against madness and melancholy, pray this Psalm, together with the prayer belonging to it and the holy name Jali — which signifies: My Lord! or: the Lord, also, is mine —, over a new pot filled with well water drawn expressly for this purpose, and with this water bathe the body of the patient. The prayer, which must be repeated during the washing, is as follows: Let it be thy will, O God, to restore N., son of R., who has been deprived of his senses and who is grievously tormented by the devil, and to enlighten his mind, for the sake of thy holy name Jali. Amen. — Selah.

The three letters of this holy name are found in the words: Jagur, verse 1; Ragal, verse 3; and Jimmot, verse 5.

He who, moreover, prays this Psalm with reverence, will generally be received with great favor.

### Psalm 16

This Psalm is important and may be employed with profit in various undertakings. For example: 1. Should anyone have been robbed and wish to know the name of the thief, he must proceed as follows: take mud or slime and sand from a stream, and mix them together; then write the names of all the suspected persons upon small slips of paper, and apply the mixture to the back of the slips; then place them in a large clean basin, filled for this purpose with fresh water from the stream — place them therein one by one, and at the same time pray over them this Psalm ten times, with the prayer adapted thereto, keeping in mind the name of Caar, that is to say Living, which name is found in the words of the sixth verse, namely Chabalim and Alei; and if the name of the true thief is written upon the slips, the one upon which his name is inscribed will rise to the surface. The prayer is as follows: Let it be thy will, Eel Caar, living God, to make known the name of the thief who has robbed me (name here what has been stolen). Grant that the name of the thief, if it be among these names, may rise up before thine eyes, and thus be made manifest to mine and to those of all who are present, so that thy name may be glorified; grant this for the sake of thy holy name. Amen. — Selah!

2. Whosoever prays this Psalm daily with reverence, and with childlike confidence in the eternal love and goodness of God applied to his own circumstances, will see all his sorrows changed into joy.

Lastly, it is said that the daily prayer of this Psalm changes enemies into friends, and dispels all pain and all grief.

### Psalm 17

The traveller who prays this Psalm early in the morning, with fervor, accompanied by the suitable prayer, in the name of Jah, will be safe from all evil for twenty-four hours. The prayer is as follows: Let it be thy holy will, O Jah Jenofa, to make my journey prosperous, to lead me by pleasant ways, to protect me from all evil, and to bring me back safe and sound to my own people, for the sake of thy mighty and adorable name. Amen.

The two letters of the holy name Jah are taken from the words Shoddini, verse 9, and Mirmah, verse 1.

### Psalm 18

Should robbers be about to attack you, pray this Psalm promptly but with fervor, together with the prayer belonging to it, and with confidence in the most holy name of Eel Jah — that is to say mighty, all-merciful and compassionate God —: the robbers will suddenly leave you, without inflicting upon you the least injury. The letters necessary to form the holy name of God are contained in the words: Aisher, verse 1; Shoal, verse 1; Tamin, verse 33; and Haol, verse 47.

The prayer is as follows: “Mighty, all-merciful and compassionate God, Eel Jah! may it please thy most holy will to defend me against the approaching robbers, and to protect me against all enemies, adversaries and evil circumstances, for thine is the power, and thou canst help. Hear me for the sake of thy most holy name, Eel Jah. Amen. — Selah!”

Is there in your house a sick person with whom the ordinary bodily remedies have failed: fill a small vial with olive oil and water, pronounce over it, with reverence, the eighteenth Psalm, anoint all the limbs of the patient, and pray a suitable prayer in the name of Eel Jah, and he will soon recover his health.

### Psalm 19

In a prolonged and dangerous confinement, take earth from a crossroads, write upon it the first five verses of this Psalm, and lay it upon the belly of the woman in labor; leave it in place until the birth is accomplished, but no longer, and during this time pray this whole Psalm seven times in succession, together with the holy name of God belonging to it and the appropriate prayer. The holy name of this Psalm is composed of two letters of the most holy name Jehovah: He, which, according to the tradition of the Kabbalists, are of great power, and embrace the ten Sephiroth, or numerations, as well as other profound mysteries.

The prayer is as follows: Lord of heaven and of earth! May it please thee, in thy grace, to assist this woman in labor, N., daughter of R., who hovers between life and death; soften her sufferings, help her and the fruit of her body, so that she may soon be delivered. Preserve her and her child in perfect health, and grant her life, for the sake of the holy name He. Amen. — Selah!

Do you desire that your son should possess an open and broad heart, so that he may become a diligent pupil and promptly understand the lessons set before him: pronounce this Psalm over a cup filled with wine and honey, pronounce also over it the holy name and an appropriate prayer, give the child to drink of it, and your desires will be fulfilled.

Lastly, it is affirmed that this Psalm is effectual in driving out evil spirits. It is necessary, however, to pray this Psalm, together with the holy name and an appropriate prayer, seven times over the person possessed by the evil spirit. The letters of the name He are contained in the words Haschamajim, verse 2, and Begoali, verse 6.

### Psalm 20

Mix in a vessel oil of roses, water and salt; pray over this mixture, seven times, in the most holy name of Jeho, this Psalm and a suitable prayer, in a low voice and with reverence; then anoint with this oil your face and your hands, and sprinkle your garments with it, and you will remain, on that day, safe from all danger and all suffering.

Are you summoned to appear in person before the judge, in a judicial proceeding: have recourse shortly beforehand to the means indicated above, and thereby you will surely be justified and will withdraw unharmed. The prayer, in this last case, is as follows: Lord and judge of all the world! Thou holdest the hearts of all men in thy power and movest them according to thy holy will; grant that I may find grace and favor in the eyes of my judges and of those who are placed above me, and dispose their hearts in favor of my best interests. Grant that I may be favored with a reasonable and favorable verdict, that I may be justified thereby, and that I may go away free. Hear me, merciful and beloved Father, and fulfil my desire, for the sake of thy great and adorable name, Jeho. Amen. — Selah.

The letters of the holy name Jeho are contained in the words: Jaanah, verse 2; Sela, verse 4; and Korem, verse 10.

### Psalm 21

During a storm at sea, when danger presses, mix oil of roses, water, salt and resin; pronounce slowly over this mixture this Psalm and the holy name Jehach, then pour the consecrated balm into the foaming sea while pronouncing the following prayer: Lord of the world! Thou subduest the pride of the foaming and roaring sea, and thou stillest the terrible crashing of the waves. May it please thee, for the sake of thy most holy name Jehach, to calm the storm and to deliver us mercifully from this danger. Amen. Selah!

The letters of this holy name are contained in the words: Jehovah, verse 2; Duma, verse 14; and Ki, verse 13.

Should you have a request to present to the king or to some other person in high station, pronounce this Psalm over a mixture of olive oil and resin, thinking at the same time upon the holy name of Jehach; anoint your face, and pray with faith and confidence a prayer adapted to your circumstances: you may then console yourself with the assurance that you will be favorably received and that you will find grace.

### Psalm 22

Should a traveller pray this Psalm seven times each day, with the appropriate divine name, Aha, and a prayer composed according to circumstances, in full confidence in the mighty protection of our exalted and most merciful God, no misfortune will befall him. Should he travel by water, neither pirates nor storms will be able to harm him; and should he travel by land, he will be safe from all evil coming from beasts and from men.

The letters of this holy name are found in the words: Eli, verse 2; Assah, verse 33.

### Psalm 23

Should you desire to receive sure instructions concerning something, by a vision or in a dream, purify yourself by fasting and bathing, pronounce the Psalm seven times with the holy name Jah, and pray at the end of each repetition: Lord of the world! notwithstanding thine unspeakable and mighty majesty, thine exaltation and thy glory, thou nevertheless lendest an attentive ear to the prayer of thy humblest creature, and thou fulfillest its desires. Hear also my prayer, loving Father, and may it please thy most holy will to reveal to me in a dream whether (here state clearly the matter of which an exact knowledge is desired), as thou didst often reveal by dreams the lot of our fathers. Grant me my request for the sake of thine adorable name, Jah. Amen. Selah!

The letters of the holy name Jah are contained in the words: Jehovah, verse 1; Napschi, verse 3; and, according to the alphabetical order called Ajack Bechar, according to which the letters He and Nun are found transposed.

### Psalms 24 and 25

Although the contents of these two Psalms differ considerably, they are, as to their mystical uses, equal and alike in power and in action. Whosoever repeats these Psalms every morning with feelings of devotion will escape the greatest danger, and the devastating flood will not harm him.

The holy name is called Eli, and it is found in the words of the twenty-fifth Psalm: Elecha, verse 1; Lemaan, verse 11; and Mi, verse 12.

### Psalm 26

When imminent dangers threaten, whether by land or by water, or should anyone be called upon to undergo a hard captivity, let him pray this Psalm with the indicated holy name of Elohe and with an appropriate prayer, and he may then count with confidence upon a speedy deliverance from his prison.

You will find the letters of this holy name in the words: Atachef, verse 10; Lischmoa, verse 7; Lo, verse 4 (according to the order of the At Basch); and Chattaim, verse 9.

### Psalm 27

Should you wish to be well and kindly received in a strange city, and desire to be welcomed there with hospitality, repeat this Psalm during your journey, again and again, with reverence, and with full confidence that God will dispose the hearts of men to receive you and to shelter you with kindness.

**Translator's note.** Since the author gives for this Psalm neither holy name nor prayer, it may be presumed that the frequent repetition of the Psalm suffices for all the ends in view.

### Psalm 28

Do you wish your enemy to be reconciled with you: pronounce this Psalm, with the appropriate holy name He and a suitable prayer, trusting in the power and promptness of the great Master of hearts, and your wish will be fully realized.

The two letters of this holy name are contained in the words: Ledavid, verse 1, and Haolam, according to the order of the At Basch.

### Psalm 29

This Psalm is highly recommended for driving out an evil spirit. The manner of proceeding is as follows: take seven splinters of osier and seven leaves of a date palm that has never borne fruit; place them in a pot filled with water upon which the sun has never shone, and repeat over it, in the evening, this Psalm with the most holy name of Aha, ten times, with great reverence; then, in full confidence in the power of God, set the pot upon the earth, in the open air, and leave it there until the following evening. Then pour the whole at the door of the possessed person, and the Ruach Roah, that is to say the evil spirit, will surely depart.

The two letters of this holy name are contained in the words Jehovah, verse 11, and, according to the alphabetical order called Ajack Bechar, Habra, verse 2.

**Translator's notes.** For this Psalm, likewise, no prescribed prayer is given.

### Psalm 30

Whosoever prays this Psalm daily will be safe from every untoward event. The holy name is Eel, and it may be found in the words: Aromimcha, verse 2, and Lemaan, verse 12.

**From the translator.** This Psalm and the following one are likewise without a prescribed prayer.

### Psalm 31

Do you wish to escape slanders, and do you desire that evil tongues may be able neither to harm you nor to cause you vexation: repeat this Psalm in a low voice, with laudable devotion, over a small quantity of pure olive oil, and anoint therewith your face and your hands, in the name of Jah.

The letters constituting this holy name are found in the words: Palteni, verse 2, and Hammesachlim, verse 22.

**Note.** The translator deems it necessary to observe, once for all, that the prayers specially adapted to these Psalms, as to many of those that follow, are wanting, and that the author doubtless thought that the prayers already given would enable everyone to improvise a suitable prayer. This presumption is all the more probable in that we find further on, in the work, the author exhorting everyone to give himself to prayer unto God, without prescribing any particular form. Another circumstance, however, concerns the holy names: if they are wanting, it is because the ancient Kabbalists have so ordained it, and as such this must be particularly noted at all times.

### Psalm 32

Whoever prays this psalm daily receives grace, love and mercy. To this psalm are joined neither holy name nor prayer.

### Psalm 33

Have you had the misfortune of constantly seeing your children die at their birth: pronounce this psalm, with the most holy name Jehovah, over pure olive oil, and anoint your wife with it, and the children who are born to you henceforth will live.

In time of a general famine, the inhabitants of the afflicted district should pray this psalm with united heart and strength, and they will surely be heard.

You will find the letters of this holy name in Lajehovah, verse 2; Hodu, verse 3; Asath, verse 9; and Hejozer, verse 14.

### Psalm 34

Have you resolved to pay a visit to a prince or to some other person high in authority: pronounce this psalm and the holy name Pele, that is to say, Wonderful, shortly before appearing in their presence, and you will be received agreeably and will find favor.

The letters of this holy name are found in the words: Padeh, verse 23; Lifne, verse 1; and Karaf, verse 7.

This psalm is likewise highly recommended to every traveler: if he prays it diligently, he will surely complete his journey in safety.

### Psalm 35

Have you a lawsuit pending in which you are exposed to unjust, vindictive and quarrelsome people: then pray this psalm, with its holy name Jah, early in the morning, three days in succession, and you will surely win your cause.

The letters that compose this holy name are contained in the words: Lochmi, verse 1, and Wezinna, verse 2.

### Psalm 36

Against all wicked and slanderous libels, pray this psalm, and they will cause you no harm.

The holy name of this psalm is found in the words: Arwen, verse 6; Mischpatecha, verse 7; and Tehom, verse 7.

### Psalm 37

If someone has drunk so much wine that he has lost his reason, and in consequence there is fear for his safety, quickly pour water into a pitcher, pronounce this psalm over it, bathe his head and face with the consecrated water, and also give him some of it to drink.

### Psalms 38 and 39

If you have been slandered to such a degree that the king and the officers of the law have been turned against you and are taking measures to punish you, rise early, at daybreak, and go out into the fields. Pray these psalms and their holy name seven times with great devotion, and fast the whole day.

The holy name of the first psalm is Aha, and that of the second He; they are taken from the words Hascha, verse 14, and Amarti, verse 2.

### Psalm 40

The principal property of this psalm is that, by its use, we may deliver ourselves from evil spirits, if we pray it every day.

The holy name is Jah, and it is found in the words: Schawaiti, verse 2, and Chuscha, verse 14.

### Psalms 41 to 43

If your enemies have stripped you of your credit and have made you suspected, thereby reducing your income, or have perhaps deprived you of your office in order to install another in your place, you must pray these psalms three times a day, for three days in succession, with a prayer suited to your circumstances; in so doing, you will see incredible things: your enemies will be covered with shame, and you will remain unharmed.

The 42nd psalm possesses this particular property: if you wish to be settled on a certain matter and desire to obtain information through a dream, you must fast one day and, shortly before retiring to rest, pray this psalm, with the holy name Zawa (which signifies the Lord of hosts) belonging to the psalm, seven times, each time setting forth your desires in a suitable prayer, in which your wishes must be clearly named.

### Psalm 44

If you wish to be safe from your enemies, the frequent praying of this psalm will, it is said, answer your expectation.

### Psalms 45 and 46

These two psalms possess, it is said, the virtue of making peace between husband and wife and, in particular, of taming shrewish wives. The saying runs, indeed: Whoever has a quarrelsome wife, let him pronounce the 45th psalm over pure olive oil and anoint his body with it; his wife will, in the future, be more amiable and more friendly. But if a man has incurred, without fault, the enmity of his wife, and desires a just return of conjugal love and peace, let him pray the 46th psalm over olive oil and anoint his wife completely with it; and, it is said, conjugal love will return.

The holy name is Adojah (this name is composed of the first syllables of the two most holy names of God, Adonai and Jehovah).

The letters are in the words: Elohim, verse 2; Meod, verse 2; Jehovah, verse 8; and Sela, verse 12.

### Psalm 47

Do you wish to be loved, respected and well received by all your fellow men: pray this psalm seven times every day.

### Psalm 48

If you have many enemies without cause, who hate you out of pure envy, pray this psalm often, thinking at the same time of the holy name of Sach, which signifies Pure, Clear and Transparent, and your enemies will be seized with fear, terror and anguish, and in the future they will no longer attempt to harm you.

The letters of the holy name are found in the words: Achasatam, verse 7, and Ki, verse 14.

### Psalms 49 and 50

Is one of your own afflicted with a severe and perhaps incurable fever: then take a new pen and ink prepared for this purpose, and write the 49th psalm and the first six verses of the 50th psalm, with the appropriate holy name Schaddei, which signifies Almighty and which belongs to these psalms, upon pure parchment prepared for this particular case, and hang it about the neck of the sick person by a silken cord.

The letters that compose the divine name Schaddei may be found in the words of the 49th psalm: Schimma, verse 1; Adaw, verse 3; and Wikas, verse 8.

**Translator’s remarks.** (If anyone chooses to write and wear a talisman such as the one described above, we would kindly advise him to procure parchment, ink and pen from a Jewish scribe of the ten commandments.)

It is asserted that whoever carries upon his person the 50th psalm, written in the manner described above, will be safe from all danger and will escape all the machinations of robbers.

The holy name is Chai, which signifies Living, and the letters are taken from the words: Sewach, verse 5, and Anochi, verse 7.

### Psalm 51

If someone is tormented by an anxious and uneasy conscience for having committed a grave sin, let him pronounce this psalm, with the word Dam which is joined to it in spirit, three times a day — namely: in the morning, at noon and in the evening — over poppy oil, pronouncing at the same time a prayer suited to the circumstance, in which the evil deed must be mentioned with deep humility and a contrition that must be obtained from the just but merciful Judge of men through a broken heart; then let him anoint his body with the consecrated oil, and he will see in a few days that he has found grace, and that the heavy burden has been taken from him.

The letters of the word Dam are taken, by means of the transposition of the B and the M, from the words Parim, verse 20, and Bebo, verse 2, according to the order of the alphabet Al Bam, in which the B is taken for M.

### Psalm 52

He who has the misfortune to be troubled by frequent slanders is advised to recite this psalm every day, in the morning; no special prayer nor holy name is necessary to obtain the benefit of this psalm.

### Psalms 53 to 55

These three psalms are ordained to be recited by him who is persecuted without cause by open and secret enemies. If he desires only to appease his enemies, or to fill them with fear, he must repeat every day the prescribed 53rd psalm, with the holy name Ai. The letters of this name are the first letters of the two blessed names of God, Adonai and Jehovah, and they are found in the words Amar, verse 2, and Jiszmach, verse 6.

If, however, he wishes not only to be safe from their malice, but further desires to avenge himself upon them, he must then repeat the 54th psalm with the prescribed holy name, Jah. The letters of this are found in the last word of this psalm, Eeni, and in the word Immenu, verse 2, and this according to the kabbalistic rule of Gematria, inasmuch as the letter He, when written out in full, signifies six in number, and may in this manner very easily be taken for the letter Vav, which, in the reckoning, is likewise worth six.

If he desires to render to his enemies evil for evil, he will repeat the 55th psalm with the name Vah, which contains the two final letters of the name Jehovah. The letters of this name are found in the words: Weattah, verse 12, and Haasina, verse 2.

### Psalm 56

This psalm is recommended to him who desires to free himself from the bonds of passion and of the senses, and who ardently wishes to be delivered from the so-called Jezer Harra, that is to say, from evil lusts or the desire to commit sin.

### Psalm 57

Whoever wishes to be fortunate in all his undertakings must pray this psalm every day, after the morning prayer, at church, and with it the holy name Chai, which signifies living, which name he must constantly keep in mind.

The two letters of this name are contained in the words: Chonneni, verse 2, and Elohim, verse 6.

### Psalm 58

If you are attacked by a vicious dog, promptly pray this psalm, and the dog will do you no harm.

### Psalm 59

Do you wish to be entirely freed from the Jezer Harra, that is to say, from the inclination to evil which all men possess, and from the sensual appetites and passions which often carry them away: then pray this psalm, from the second verse to the end, for three days in succession, in the morning, at noon and in the evening, together with the holy name that belongs to it, namely Paltioel, which signifies Strong God, my deliverer and my savior; pray also the prescribed prayer, and you will perceive in yourself the most wonderful changes.

The prayer is as follows: Lord, my Father and the Father of mine, mighty God! May it please thee, for the sake of thy great, holy and adorable name, Paltioel, to deliver me from the Jezer Harra (from my evil desires and passions, and from all evil thoughts and deeds), as thou didst deliver the author of this psalm when he prayed to thee. Amen. — Selah!
The letters of the holy name of Paltioel may be found in the words: Pischti, verse 3; Elohim, verse 6; Chattaii, verse 8; Jehovah, verse 8; Aschir, verse 15; and Maschel, verse 14.

### Psalm 60

If you are a soldier in an army and are about to march into the field, repeat this psalm keeping in mind the holy name of Jah, and, at the conclusion of each repetition of the psalm, pronounce a suitable prayer, in full confidence in the infinite omnipotence of Him who can give victory to whom He will, and you will be able to return home without a wound.

The two letters of the holy name Jah are contained in the word Zarenu, verse 14, the last word of this psalm, and in Lelammed, verse 1.

### Psalm 61

When you are about to take possession of a new dwelling, repeat this psalm just before moving into it, with a suitable prayer, trusting in the name of Schaddei, and you will experience there blessing and good fortune.

The letters that compose this name are taken from the words Schimma, verse 2; Ken, verse 9; and Jom, the last word of this psalm. It must be remarked, however, that the last two letters are chosen according to the alphabetical order of Ajack Bechar.

### Psalm 62

Say this psalm with proper reverence on Sunday, immediately after the evening prayer, and on Monday after vespers, thinking at the same time of the holy name Ittami, which signifies “hidden, concealed or invisible” (which most probably refers to the invisible God, who covers the transgressions of penitent sinners), and pronounce the following prayer: Great, mighty and merciful God! may it be thy holy will to forgive me all my sins, transgressions and offenses; be pleased to cover them and blot them out, as thou didst blot out the sins and transgressions of him who pronounced this psalm in thy presence; be pleased to do this for the sake of the adorable name of Ittami. Amen. — Selah!

The letters of this name may be found in the words: Ach, verse 2; Jeschuati, verse 2; Jimmot, verse 3; Lelohim, verse 6; and Leisch, verse 13.

### Psalm 63

If you have reason to believe that your partner is preparing to take an unfair advantage of you and that you will suffer from it, and if for this reason you wish to withdraw from the partnership, repeat this psalm thinking of the holy name Jach, and not only will you be able to withdraw without loss, but you will further obtain fortune and blessings.

The letters of this holy name are contained in the words Jismach, verse 11, and Jechuda, verse 1.

### Psalm 64

Concerning this psalm, it is enough to say that seafarers who pray it every day with devotion will complete their voyage without accident and will reach in good health the place of their destination. For the rest, neither holy name nor particular prayer has been deemed necessary.

### Psalm 65

Whoever recites this psalm with its appropriate name, Jah, with perseverance, will be fortunate in all his undertakings, and everything he attempts will turn to his best advantage. It is particularly recommended to him who has a petition to present, for it is asserted that he will certainly obtain what he desires.

The two letters of this holy name are taken from the words Joachim, verse 14, and Dumijah, verse 2.

### Psalm 66

If a man is possessed by a Ruach Roah (evil spirit), write this psalm upon parchment and hang it upon him; then lay your hands upon him and say: Save me, O God; for the waters are come in unto my soul (Psalm LXIX, 2).

### Psalms 67 and 68

These two psalms both contain the divine name of Jah. The letters that compose it are found, in the first psalm, chosen from the words Jechonnenu, verse 2, and from the last word of the fifth verse, Sela; in the second, on the other hand, in Jakum, verse 2, and in Aora, verse 36. The first must be prayed in a case of prolonged fever, or in a hard captivity. The second, on the contrary, must be prayed over a vessel filled with water upon which the sun has never shone, in a low voice and in the name of the sick person; then his body is rubbed with this water, and the evil spirit will withdraw from him.

### Psalms 69 and 70

The first of these psalms must be recited every day over water by the libertine and the sensualist who is so confirmed in his evil habits that he has become their slave, and who, whatever desire he may have to escape them, does not succeed. After having prayed this psalm over the water, he must drink of it.

The second must be prayed by him who desires to overcome his enemies.

Neither of these two psalms has a prescribed holy name or prayer.

### Psalm 71

With this psalm, likewise, there is neither holy name nor prayer; but there is attributed to it the power of bringing out of prison whoever will pray it, for some time, with reverence, seven times a day.

### Psalm 72

Write this psalm, with the name Aha, in the usual manner, upon pure parchment, and hang it about your neck: you will become the favorite of all, and will find favor and grace with all men; you may then live without anxiety, for you can never fall into poverty.

The letters of the holy name are taken from the words: Elohim, verse 1, and Jeaschruhu, verse 17.

### Psalms 73 to 83

Since these eleven psalms have neither holy names nor particular closing prayers, I shall record, in order to save space, the particular virtues attributed to each of them for the good of mankind.

The 73rd psalm must be repeated with reverence seven times a day by those who are compelled to sojourn in a heathen, idolatrous or infidel country; in so doing, no one will have to fear being induced to deny his faith.

The frequent and fervent praying of the 74th psalm foils, it is said, the relentless persecution of enemies, and will bring to nought the oppression of those powerful people, greedy for riches and hard of heart, while leading them to a terrible end.

The devout praying of the 75th psalm will obtain the forgiveness of sins.

The 76th psalm is reputed the promptest and most effective defense against the danger of fire and water.

Whoever prays the 77th psalm every day will not be touched by want or by scarcity.

Whoever prays the 78th psalm fervently and often will be loved and respected by kings and princes, and will receive favors from them.

The frequent praying of the 79th psalm is, it is said, fatal to enemies and adversaries.

The constant and assiduous praying of the 80th and 81st psalms is, it is said, a happy means of preserving men from falling into unbelief, and it also keeps them from other errors.

The praying of the 82nd psalm will help an envoy to conduct his affairs to the satisfaction of his principals, and his undertakings will succeed and prosper.

You must write the 83rd psalm, in due form, upon pure parchment, and hang it about your neck; in so doing, you will remain safe in war, escaping defeat and captivity. If, however, you should be vanquished, those who have captured you will do you no harm, for even in captivity no injury can reach you.

### Psalm 84

When a man, in consequence of a severe and prolonged illness, has contracted a repulsive, nauseating and evil odor, he must pronounce this psalm, with the prescribed holy name of Af, which signifies Father, over a pot of water upon which the sun has never shone, then pour all this water over his body, and the evil odor will leave him.

The letters of the holy name Af are found in the words: Zebaoth, verse 2, and Bach, verse 6.

### Psalm 85

Do you wish that your former friend, who now lives in enmity with you, should be reconciled with you again, if you can discover in him no disposition to make up: then go out into the open field, turn your face toward the South, and pronounce this psalm, with its prescribed holy name Yah, seven times in succession, and he will come to you and receive you in great friendship.

### Psalms 86 to 88

These three psalms, again, are left without a holy name, and nothing further is said of them, except that a person should accustom himself to praying them often, because in this way much good may be done and much evil avoided. The frequent praying of the 86th psalm, in particular, is reputed to promote the welfare of the community and of the congregation.

### Psalm 89

If one of the members of your family, or one of your dear friends, is wasting away so rapidly, in consequence of a severe illness, that he is already almost without strength and without resource, say this psalm over olive oil, pour the oil upon wool shorn from a sheep or a ram, and anoint with it the body and limbs of the sick person, and he will promptly recover.

If your friend is under arrest and you desire his release, go into the open field, lift your eyes toward heaven and repeat this psalm, with a prayer suited to the circumstances, which must be pronounced in full confidence in God.

### Psalm 90

If you should chance to meet a lion in the forest, or if you are deceived, duped or tormented by an evil spirit or a ghost, then take hold in your mind of the name of God (Schaddei) and repeat this psalm, and they will withdraw. But you will be still safer, when such a danger arises, if you pray the 91st psalm which follows at the same time as the 90th, both together.

### Psalm 91

The holy name of this psalm is **El**, which signifies *Strong God*. After having pronounced this psalm, together with the preceding one, over a person tormented by an evil spirit, or afflicted with an incurable disease, in the name of Eel Schaddei, then pray humbly: “May it be according to thy holy pleasure, O my God! to take from N., son of R., the evil spirit that torments him, for the sake of thy great, mighty and holy name El Schaddei. Be pleased to send him health promptly and may he be perfectly restored. Hear his prayer as thou didst once hear that of thy servant Moses when he prayed this psalm. May his prayer reach unto thee as of old the holy incense rose up to thee on high. Amen. Selah!”

The two letters of the name Eel are contained in the words *Jeschuti*, verse 16, and *Orech*, verse 16.

Write this psalm again, joined to the last verse of the preceding psalm, upon pure parchment, and hide it behind the door of your house: you will be safe from all fatal accidents.

The kabbalists attribute to this psalm, taken with the aforesaid verse, the most wonderful virtue, when it is employed according to the nature of the circumstances, and when it is combined with other passages of Scripture, holy names of angels, characters and prayers. It is said, for example:

**Prayer by which all distress, danger and suffering may be averted.** If someone is in danger of death or falls into distress, whatever it may be — stricken with an incurable disease, with the plague, threatened by fire or by water, overwhelmed by enemies or murderers, in battles, sieges, robberies, close captivity, etc. —, let him first confess his sins, then let him pronounce the prayer **Vihi Noam** (the name by which the 91st psalm joined to the aforesaid verse is commonly known) ninety-nine times, according to the number of the two most holy names of God, Jehovah Adonei. Each time he comes to the fourteenth verse — “Because he hath set his love upon me,” etc. — let him keep in mind the holy name, then let him pray each time with devotion:

“Thou art the most holy, king of all that is revealed and hidden, exalted above all that is high; sanctify and glorify thine adorable name in this world which is thine, so that all the nations of the earth may know that thine are the glory and the power, and that thou hast kept me from all distress, and particularly from the painful extremity (here the object of the prayer must be distinctly stated) which has befallen me, N., son of R. And I promise and vow, from now on and for ever, as long as I shall live upon the earth and until I return to the dust from which I was taken…” (Here the vow must be stated aloud — declaring what we will do, perform or give in the service of our Creator. The vow may consist in fasting, in giving alms, or in reading every day several chapters of the Holy Scriptures, of the Psalms, of the Sohar or of the Talmud, in ransoming captives, in tending the sick and in burying the dead.) “Praised be Jehovah, my Rock and my Salvation. Thou wilt be my representative and my intercessor, and thou wilt help me, for thou helpest thy poor, weak and humble creature; in time of distress thou deliverest from fear and danger, and thou showest mercy to thy people; merciful and gracious, thou hearest the prayer of every one. Praised be thou, Jehovah, thou who hearest prayer.” (The last words must be repeated seven times at each ending of the prayer.)

And now, whoever will punctually observe the foregoing instructions three days in succession, in full confidence in the mighty help of God, may be assured of the assistance he desires.

The kabbalists, and particularly the celebrated Rabbi Isaac Loria, have assured us that in time of plague or general calamity, the prayer Vihi Noam must be prayed seven times a day, associating with it in spirit the figure of the golden candlestick, when it is composed of the forty-one holy and important words and names of this psalm, among which we must particularly consider the holy names in their order. Here are the names:

Yean, Aim, Bich, Iba, Wich, Ika, Aan, Beni, Mil, Tmol, Veal, Ktaz, Ilu, Mehoh, Imi, Becha, Im, Retak, Betu, Lir, Uma, Ima, Mix, Mehi, Aki, Lakad, Mili, Ibak, Rul, Leta, Akham, Pesch, Aab, Schin, Aki, Acchu, Kuck, Yetat, Raasch, Jaub, Ana.

*(see fig. A)*

After this must be pronounced verses 21 to 28 of chapter XII of Exodus, keeping in mind the names contained in the 23rd and 28th verses, in the following order:

Awal, Jahel, Ito, Huj, Husch, Aha, Imo, Vil. — Likewise Yohu, Uha, Bam, Bili, Zel, Holo, Yesop, and finally the holy name: **Nischaszlas**.
