And now, he who observes all these things to the letter, and who can keep in memory all the letters, points and vowels, will be safe from all danger and will be strong as steel, so that no firearm can reach him. The kabbalists show the certainty of this by the following: the letter *Sajin* is nowhere found in the whole psalm; and since the word *Sajin* (or *Kle Sajin*) embraces in its meaning all deadly weapons, this conclusion is entirely well founded.

**Translator’s remarks [of 1880].** — The extraordinary powers attributed to the 91st psalm may well be founded and legitimate; but it is to be regretted that the reader can hardly avail himself of them, especially in the last experiment: for all the holy names reported are composed of the first letter of all the words of the 91st psalm, as well as of the 23rd and 28th verses of chapter XII of Exodus, a passage already cited. It is therefore impossible to pronounce this name correctly, and it can be translated neither into English *[the language of the 1880 edition]* nor into any other language. And how is one to memorize the first letters of each word of the psalm, with the points and vowels that belong to them? If anyone, despite the difficulties that accompany the use of this psalm, nevertheless desires to avail himself of its virtues, he will have to undertake the laborious task of learning the Hebrew language — or else write it, and wear it upon his heart as an amulet.

### Psalm 92

He who desires to attain to high honors, let him take, for this purpose, a new pot filled with water. Let him place in it leaves of myrtle and of vine, and let him pronounce over it, with perfect confidence, the following Psalms, namely the 92nd, 94th, 23rd, 20th, 24th and 100th, three times in succession; and let him each time wash himself with the water of the pot, then anoint his face and his whole body with this water; let him then turn his face toward the north, pray to God for the fulfillment of his desires, and he will see wonderful things. He will be astonished at his ever-increasing fortune. He will also rise, in a wonderful manner, from one post of honor to another.

### Psalm 93

Nothing particular is reported of this psalm, except that it is highly recommended to whoever has a lawsuit against a hard and unjust adversary. The proper use of this psalm, it is said, will surely make him win his cause.

### Psalm 94

If you have a hard, inflexible and relentless enemy, who oppresses you harshly and causes you great anguish, go on a Monday into an open field, take incense into your mouth, turn your face toward the East and the West, and repeat first the 94th psalm, then the 92nd, seven times, keeping in mind at the same time the holy name **Eel Kanno Taf**, which signifies great, strong, zealous and good God, and pray each time, at the end of these Psalms: “May it please thee, O great, strong, zealous and good God, to humble my enemy N., son of R., as thou didst once humble the enemies of our great master Moses, who rests in peace, and who completed this psalm to thy glorification. May my prayer rise up to thee as rose the sweet odor of the incense from the altar of perfumes, and let me behold thy wonderful power. Amen! — Selah!”

### Psalm 95

The holy name of God appropriate and proper to this psalm is **Eel**, that is to say, great and strong God, and the letters of it are found in the words: Eel, verse 3, and Lezur, verse 1.

The pious believer must pray this psalm for his erring and unbelieving brethren.

### Psalms 96 and 97

The holy name of these two Psalms is **Jah**, and the letters of the first are found in the words Jeschuato, verse 2, and Hawn, verse 7, and those of the other in the words Jismechu, verse 1, and Atta, verse 9. Whoever will pray these two Psalms three times a day will procure for his family great joy and great contentment.

### Psalm 98

The holy name of this psalm is likewise **Jah**, and it must be pronounced in order to establish peace and union between families. The letters of the holy name are taken from the words: Israel, verse 3, and Haschiah, verse 1.

### Psalm 99

No holy name is recorded with this psalm, and all those who wish to become truly pious are invited to pray it often with fitting devotion.

### Psalm 100

The holy name **Jah**, already so often mentioned, is likewise suited to this psalm, and whoever prays it several days in succession, seven times each day, will triumph over all his enemies. The two letters of this holy name are recorded in verse 3 and in Aetodah, verse 4.

### Psalm 101

Whoever carries this Psalm upon his person, together with the 68th, written on parchment, is safe from the persecution of evil spirits and of vindictive persons.

### Psalms 102 and 103

These two Psalms are both held to be very good for barren women, who, through their use, may receive grace and favor from God. The holy name of the one is called **Jah**, and it is taken from Anneni, verse 3; and the name of the other is **Aha**, and it is taken from the word Adonai, verse 12, and from Sela, verse 20.

### Psalm 104

The frequent and fervent prayer of this Psalm is said to be attended with so great a power that by it the Masick may be destroyed.

**Translator’s remarks.**

The word Masick signifies, according to its root, nothing more than something harmful, something that will cause injury, whether by spirits, beings or animals. Generally, however, the Jews understood by this term the Devil, and, in the context in which it appears here, the word must signify original sin and the propensity to commit sin.

### Psalms 105 to 107

To these three Psalms is ascribed the holy name of **Jah**, and, according to the original writing, it is said that the 105th Psalm will cure the tertian fever; that the prayer of the 106th Psalm will cure the quartan fever, and finally that the prayer of the 107th will cure the daily fever.

The letters of the holy names are taken from Lejaakof, verse 7, and from Hodu, verse 1; then from Sochreni, verse 4, and from Tehillato, verse 2, of the 106th Psalm; and finally from Jischlach, verse 19, and from Verinna, verse 21.

### Psalm 108

Write this Psalm, with the holy name proper to it, **Vi** (two letters of the most holy name of Jehovah, in which the Kabbalists seek, through its many divisions, great secrets), upon a pure parchment, and hide it in a safe place behind the door of your house: then your goings out and comings in will be blessed, and you will succeed in all your affairs.

The two letters of the holy name of Jehovah, by a transposition of the Vav and the Jod, are contained in the words: Zarenu, verse 14, and Nachon, verse 2.

### Psalm 109

Have you a powerful enemy who torments and oppresses you? Fill a new jug with new, sparkling wine, add mustard to it, then repeat this Psalm three days in succession, keeping at the same time in mind the holy name of **Eel** (great and strong God), and afterwards pour the mixture before the door of your enemy’s dwelling. Take care, however, not to spill a single drop upon yourself at the moment of pouring it.

The letters of the name Eel are found in the words Elohim, verse 3, and in Ki Jamood, verse 5.

### Psalms 110 and 111

The first of these Psalms is marked with the holy name **Jah**, and by its frequent use in the form of prayer, a man may compel all his enemies and adversaries to bow before him and to beg for grace and peace.

By the prayer of the 111th Psalm, a man may acquire many friends, without its being necessary to keep any particular holy name constantly in mind.

### Psalms 112 and 113

By praying the first of these Psalms, a man will grow from time to time in might and power, and by praying the second with devotion, it is possible to check growing heresy and unbelief. Neither of these two Psalms possesses any particular holy name.

### Psalm 114

The holy name of this Psalm consists of two letters, taken together from the names Adonai (Lord) and Jehovah, namely **Aha**, which may be found in this Psalm in the words Jiszraoel, verse 1, and Jehuda, verse 2. If you desire success in your trade or business, write this Psalm with its appropriate holy name upon a pure parchment, and carry it constantly about you in a small bag prepared especially for this purpose.

### Psalm 115

If you are resolved to dispute with unbelievers, heretics and scoffers at religion, pray this Psalm beforehand with devotion.

### Psalm 116

Whoever prays this Psalm daily with devotion, trusting fully in God, will be preserved from a violent death, nor will he be surprised by a sudden death.

### Psalm 117

Have you made a vow to fulfil a certain commandment or good work, and have you failed to fulfil it through forgetfulness or negligence? As soon as you recall your failure, pray this Psalm with a broken and contrite heart.

### Psalm 118

If you pray this Psalm often and with devotion, you will be able to silence all free-thinkers, scoffers at religion and heretics who strive to lead you astray.

### Psalm 119

This is, as is well known, the longest of all the Psalms, and it consists, in the Hebrew, of eight alphabets, but in such a manner that each letter appears in it with constant regularity; from which result twenty-two particular divisions, each comprised in eight verses. Since a particular power is ascribed to each division, I cannot present it to the reader in a clearer manner than by placing before him each letter that forms the division in question.

**Aleph.** — The eight verses of this letter, which all begin with Aleph, must be pronounced over a man whose limbs tremble and shiver; and if this is done in a low and even voice, he will be relieved. If anyone has made a vow whose fulfilment has become burdensome to him, it will be easy for him to keep his promise.

**Beth.** — It is said that by the second division, from the ninth to the sixteenth verse, a man may obtain a good memory, an open heart eager to learn, as well as an extensive understanding. Whoever desires to attain this must begin thus: remove skilfully and cleanly the shell of a hard-boiled egg, so that the inside remains intact, and write upon it the eight verses above, as well as the fourth verse of Deuteronomy, chapter xxxiii, and the eighth verse of Joshua, chapter i, and also the holy names of the angels **Chosniel, Schrewniel and Mupiel**. It is not necessary to know the translation of these three angels’ names, for they are not to be pronounced; but as it will be interesting to the reader to know their meaning, it will not be superfluous to give it here. Chosniel signifies: Cover me, or overshadow me, mighty God! (namely, with the spirit of wisdom and knowledge). Schrewniel: Turn me again, mighty God! that is to say, change me, convert me into a better man or woman, as David once said: “Create in me, O God,” etc. Mupiel signifies: From the mouth of the mighty God (namely: cause me to observe the decrees of thy laws, as if I heard and received them from the mouth of God himself). Finally, the following must also be written upon the egg: Open and enlarge my heart and my understanding, that I may hear and understand all that I read, and that I may never forget it. All this must be done on a Thursday evening, after having fasted the whole day; then the egg must be put whole into the mouth, and while it is being eaten, the first four verses of this division must be repeated three times in succession.

**Gimel.** — The division of the third letter, verses 17 to 24, must be prayed seven times in succession, in a low and solemn tone and with full confidence in the omnipotence of God, over the seriously injured eye of a friend, that the pain may cease and the eye be restored.

**Daleth.** — By the fervent prayer of this division, verses 25 to 32, a painful injury of the left eye may be healed, in the first place, in the same manner as that described above; and in the second place, if a man is engaged in a lawsuit, or troubled by a change of occupation or of residence, or if he desires to make an advantageous choice or to take a resolution, he must repeat these verses eight times in succession. If, on the other hand, a man must have recourse to the counsel and assistance of many persons in order to carry an undertaking through, he must repeat this division ten times.

**He.** — The division of the letter He, verses 33 to 40, is said to have the power of turning men away from committing sins. A sinful being, so accustomed to committing sin and vice that he cannot abstain from them in spite of his best resolutions, must write these eight verses upon a parchment prepared from a pure deerskin (or have them so written), place it in a bag prepared for this purpose and hang it about his neck, so as to carry it continually upon his breast.

**Vau.** — Pronounce these eight verses, 41 to 48, properly over water, and give it to your servant or subordinate to drink: then your authority and power over him will become easy and pleasant, and he will serve you willingly.

**Zain.** — To the seventh division, verses 49 to 56, two different effects are ascribed. It is said, for example: if one of your friends or acquaintances is afflicted with melancholy, or becomes splenetic, or suffers violent stitches in the side, write this division, with the holy name **Raphael**, which signifies “heal, mighty God,” properly upon a small piece of pure parchment, and bind it upon the sick person at the place where the spleen is.

If you have been drawn, through the deceptions of evil counsellors, into an undertaking which promises bad results, repeat this division eighteen times, and you will find the means of withdrawing from the undertaking without harm to yourself.

**Cheth.** — Pronounce the division of this letter, verses 57 to 64, seven times over wine, and give it to drink to a sick person who suffers sharp pains in the upper part of the body, and he will soon find relief.

**Teth.** — The division of the letter Teth, verses 65 to 72, is an easy, prompt and proven remedy for curing the most serious cases of diseases of the kidneys or of the liver, or for removing pains of the hips. Pronounce these eight verses properly, distinctly and with reverence over the sick person, and he will enter upon convalescence.

**Jod.** — Would you find grace and favor before God and men, pray, at the end of each morning prayer, the division of this letter, verses 73 to 80, trusting fully in the mercy and grace of God, and your prayer will be heard.

**Caph.** — If one of your household has a dangerous sore, or a burning swelling on the right side of the nose, pray the eight verses of this division, verses 81 to 88, ten times, in a low and conjuring voice, over the sore, and you will find, to your astonishment and joy, that the sore, otherwise incurable, will be healed.

**Lamed.** — If you are summoned to appear in person before the judge in a lawsuit, pray on the evening before, immediately after the evening prayer, the division of the letter Lamed, verses 89 to 96, and you will obtain a favorable hearing, and you will be permitted to leave the court justified.

**Mem.** — For pains in the limbs, and especially for paralysis of the right arm or hand, this division, verses 97 to 104, must be prayed seven times during three consecutive days, in a low and conjuring voice, over the affected arm; the pain will cease and the arm will be healed.

**Nun.** — Do you intend to travel, pray this division, verses 105 to 112, which begins with the words: “For thy word is a lamp unto my feet,” some days before undertaking your journey, each time after the morning and the evening prayer, and you will accomplish your journey in safety and prosper in your undertaking.

**Samech.** — If you have a favor to ask of a superior, pray, before presenting your petition, or before attempting to ask the favor by word of mouth, the eight verses of the letter Samech, verses 113 to 120, and you will not go away without having been heard.

**Ain.** — In the same way and manner in which the prayer of the division of the letter Mem heals the pain of the right arm, so the prayer of the eight verses of the letter Ain, verses 121 to 128, will heal the pain of the left arm and hand.

**Pe.** — The prayer of this division, verses 129 to 136, will produce the same effect in the case of a boil or a swelling on the left side of the nose, and the procedure, in both cases, must be the same in order to effect the cure.

**Tsaddi.** — Since it frequently happens that persons invested with an official charge are led, through deceptions and other circumstances, to render a false and unjust decision, even against their own conviction and their own desire, they are kindly advised to pray the eight verses of this letter, verses 137 to 144, three times with devotion before rendering their decision, asking at the same time the help of the Judge of all judges, that he may enlighten their minds.

**Koph.** — The mysterious operation of this division, verses 145 to 152, relates to the healing of a dangerous and painful wound in the left leg. These eight verses must be pronounced in a low and conjuring voice over a quantity of rose oil, and the wound must be anointed with this oil.

**Resh.** — Are you afflicted with a painful and constantly suppurating abscess in the right ear, pronounce the eight verses of the division of the letter Resh, verses 153 to 160, in a low and conjuring voice, over water or onion juice, and let a drop of it run into the ear: you will experience immediate relief.

**Schin.** — Against violent and burning headaches, pronounce the division of this letter, verses 161 to 168, in a low and conjuring voice, three times over pure olive oil, and anoint the place where the pain is most severe.

**Tau.** — The last division of this Psalm, verses 169 to 176, must be employed in the same manner as the division of the letter Resh, that is to say, it must be pronounced over onion water, and by its use an abscess of the left ear may be healed.

Finally, it is declared at the end of this Psalm that whoever is afflicted with tearing pains in both arms, in the sides and in the legs at the same time, must repeat this entire Psalm in the following order: 1. the eight verses of the letter Aleph, those of Tau and of Beth; 2. those of the letter Schin; 3. the division of the letter Gimel; 4. the eight verses of the letter Resh; 5. the division of the letter Daleth; 6. that of the letter Koph; 7. the eight verses of the letter He; 8. those of the letter Zain; 9. the division of the letter Vau; 10. the eight verses of the letter Pe; 11. the division of the letter Tsaddi; 12. the division of the letter Ain; 13. the eight verses of the letter Cheth; 14. those of the letter Samech; 15. those of the letter Teth; 16. of Nun; 17. the eight verses of the letter Jod; and finally, 18. the division of the letters Mem, Caph and Lamed. This remedy has been tried and has proved infallible. If anyone should be afflicted with tearing pains in the kidneys, make for him, at the conclusion of this Psalm, knots, combinations or magic knots in the water, with or under the names of: Adam, Seth, Enoch, Canaan, Mahalleel, Jared, Methusaleh, Lamech, Noah, Shem.

[The translator is compelled to confess honestly that he does not understand this last clause, and that he is still less able to give any indication whatever of the method employed for making magic knots; nor does he presume that anyone takes an interest in this matter.]

### Psalm 120

If you must appear before the judge, repeat this Psalm beforehand, and you will receive grace and favor.

If a traveller finds himself in a forest infested with many venomous serpents, scorpions and other venomous reptiles, as may easily happen, and is thus exposed to danger, let him pray this Psalm seven times as soon as he catches sight of the forest, and he will be able to continue his journey without any harm.

### Psalm 121

Are you compelled to travel alone by night, pray this Psalm with reverence seven times, and you will be safe from all accidents and from every untoward event.

### Psalm 122

If you are about to address a man of high station, repeat this Psalm beforehand thirteen times, and you will be received graciously and find favor. Pray this Psalm also every time you are in church, and you will obtain a blessing.

### Psalm 123

If your servant or your fellow-workman has run away from you, write this Psalm, with his name, upon a plate of lead or of tin, and he will return to you.

### Psalm 124

If you are about to cross a swollen torrent, or to undertake a journey by water, pray this Psalm before entering the ship, and you may then begin your journey without fear.

### Psalm 125

If you are compelled to travel in a country where you have declared enemies whom you have reason to fear on account of the harm with which they threaten you, then take, before entering the country, your two hands full of salt, pronounce this Psalm seven times over them, then scatter the salt into the air toward the four corners of the world; by so doing, none of your enemies will be able to do you any harm.

### Psalm 126

Are you so unfortunate that your children are taken from you in their earliest infancy, and that you cannot raise any of them? Then, when your wife is again with child, write this Psalm upon four amulets made of pure parchment, and add to the last line of each amulet the names of the following angels: **Sinui, Sinsuni and Semanglaf**, then hide the amulets in the four walls of your house, and your child will live.

### Psalm 127

Write this Psalm upon a pure parchment, place this amulet in a clean bag and hang it about the neck of a new-born son immediately after his birth, and no harm will ever befall him thereafter.

### Psalm 128

Write this Psalm upon a pure parchment and hang it upon a woman with child: she and the fruit of her body will always be safe from unfortunate accidents, and she will have a happy delivery.

### Psalm 129

Whoever accustoms himself to repeat this Psalm daily after the morning prayer will finally dispose himself to live in piety and virtue, and will be able to accomplish many good and profitable works.

### Psalm 130

If you live in a besieged city, into which and out of which no one can go without danger, and if you have urgent business, so that you feel compelled to venture upon a journey, then, at the very moment of leaving the city, pray this Psalm in a low and adjuring voice, toward the four corners of the earth, and you will then be able to pass before all the sentinels without being seen or molested. A heavy sleep will fall upon them, so that they will not be aware of your presence.

### Psalm 131

He who is so strongly possessed by the evil spirit of pride that he looks upon all other men with contempt, but who, after mature reflection, desires to follow a middle course, if only his intolerable pride would permit him, is invited to pray this Psalm with reverence three times a day, after the morning and the evening prayer. His pride will receive from it a certain check.

### Psalm 132

If you have sworn to perform something punctually and, in spite of your oath, you neglect to fulfil your obligation, thus rendering yourself perjured, you must, in order to avoid a similar crime in the future, pray this Psalm daily with deep reverence.

### Psalm 133

Whoever prays this Psalm daily will not only retain the love and friendship of his friends, but will also gain many more.

### Psalm 134

This very short Psalm, which counts only three verses, must be repeated by every learned man, and especially by every student before entering college.

### Psalm 135

Whoever desires to repent sincerely of sin and to consecrate his life to the service of God must pray this Psalm daily after the morning and evening prayers; then his heart and his mind will be renewed from day to day, and he will unite himself each day more closely to God.

### Psalm 136

Whoever desires, on account of wilful sins and transgressions, to make a penitent confession of his misdeeds, must pray this Psalm beforehand, then make his confession with a humble and broken heart, and with great reverence.

### Psalm 137

The prayer of this Psalm, it is said, will root out of the heart the most inveterate hatred, envy and malice.

### Psalm 138

Praying this Psalm, it is declared, will produce love and friendship.

### Psalm 139

This Psalm must be prayed in order to increase and preserve love between married people.

### Psalm 140

Praying this Psalm is held to be a powerful means of removing growing hatred between husband and wife.

### Psalm 141

Whoever is often oppressed with inward fears must pray this Psalm frequently.

### Psalms 142 and 143

The prayer of the first of these two Psalms will heal pains of the thighs, and the prayer of the other will remove tearing pains of the arms.

### Psalm 144

When someone breaks an arm, this Psalm must be prayed, and the perfect healing of the arm can be neither delayed nor interrupted by adverse circumstances.

### Psalm 145

He who fears ghosts and evil spirits must pray this Psalm, in connection with the 144th, with reverence, for the prayer of these Psalms will instantly drive away all ghosts and all apparitions.

### Psalm 146

Whoever has been dangerously wounded by a sword or another deadly weapon must, during the time in which he receives surgical care, pray this Psalm daily with reverence, and especially when the wound is dressed and the bandages renewed, and he will soon have cause to rejoice in a perfect recovery from his wounds.

### Psalm 147

For the healing of dangerous and mortal wounds, of bites and stings of the salamander, lizard, serpent, scorpion or other venomous reptiles, the fervent prayer of this Psalm is said to possess the same healing power as the preceding Psalm, already described.

### Psalms 148 and 149

These two Psalms are said to possess the desirable virtue of stopping fire, when they are prayed with a childlike confidence in the unfailing help of the Almighty.

### Psalm 150

This happy Psalm of praise must be pronounced by every God-fearing and grateful being, after having escaped a great danger, or received a particular grace in answer to a prayer addressed to the Lord of hosts; and it must be repeated with a grateful heart, to His praise and to His glory.

END OF THE PSALMS.

## Supplement

Finally, the author adds the following by way of supplement:

### I.

Whoever prays daily and at every hour the five verses below addressed to God will receive grace from God and from men, and will obtain prosperity and blessing in all his undertakings.

The verses must be pronounced in the following order:

Psalm 121, verse 2; Psalm 66, verse 23 *[sic — Psalm 66 has only 20 verses]*; Psalm 37, verse 37; Psalm 37, verse 3; Psalm 118, verse 13.

### II.

A truly mysterious and miraculous formula is furnished by the celebrated Kabbalist Raf Amram; it is said to possess the special virtue of protecting and defending him who, after the morning prayer, prays it with the proper reverence for a whole day of twenty-four hours, whether he is at home or on a journey, against every evil power or accident, against theft, murder, and wounds by firearms or other weapons. No man will be able to attack or wound him, and no firearm will be able to harm him. This wonderful saying is the following:

See and know that I am He! I am He, and beside me there is no other God. I am He who can kill and make alive; I wound, and I am He who can heal, and none can escape from my hand or from my power. For I lift up my hand to heaven (that is to say: I swear by heaven), and I say: I am He who lives for ever.

**Animon, Animon, Alimon, Rirvtip, Taftian!** The Lord can watch and keep, and he will do so. Amen. After a man has pronounced this three times in succession, and has kept in mind at the same time the three angels’ names that are added to it, he must say the following: Happy art thou, people of God! Who is like unto thee, people whose help is Jehovah? He is the shield of thy help and the strong sword of thy pride. Thine enemies shall deny themselves before thee (that is to say, shall hide themselves from thee or withdraw before thee), but thou shalt stand upon their high places. Lord of the earth, may it please thy holy will to command thine angels to protect and defend me in all my ways. Amen!

**Translator’s remarks.**

The Hebrew words above are Kabbalistic names of angels; they are very difficult to understand, and still more difficult to translate. Thus, for example, the letters of the word Alimon signify Eel Leolam, Jehovah, Melech Waaed, Netzach, that is to say: Jehovah is an eternal God! he is a victorious king for all eternity. This prince or king, according to the belief of the Kabbalists, is charged with the superintendence of all the weapons and all the guns of the whole world, for the letters of the word Alimon, according to the signification of numbers, amount to 137, and the two little words Klo Sajin, by which are understood all kinds of guns and weapons, likewise contain, according to the signification of numbers, 137, namely:

| Alimon | | Klo Sajin | |
|---|---|---|---|
| Aleph | 1 | Beth | 20 |
| Lamed | 30 | Lamed | 30 |
| Jod | 10 | Jod | 10 |
| Mem | 40 | Zain | 7 |
| Vav | 6 | Jod | 10 |
| Nun | 50 | Jod | 10 |
| | | Nun | 50 |
| **Total** | **137** | **Total** | **137** |

According to this, there are placed under the power of this general-in-chief all the cannons, howitzers, mortars, guns, carbines, pistols, all the lances, pikes, sabres, swords and daggers of the whole world, and when he sees fit, none of these weapons can do the slightest harm. Whoever, consequently, knows how to obtain the favor of the mighty Alimon, it will be very easy for him to render himself invulnerable to wounds from firearms and from cutting instruments of every kind. The words Rirvtip and Taftian are likewise full of a mysterious signification: they are the names of two angels who are the servants of Alimon.

The letters of the holy name Animon are contained in the words Ani, Nelech, Raaed, Ne-Zachim, that is to say: I am King from eternity to eternity! In general, this angel claims a far higher rank than the angel Alimon, for he is named first and invoked twice in succession, whereby he amounts, according to the signification of numbers, to 314, that is to say, as much as the holy name of Schadai, the name of the Almighty.

### III.

I present to you here (writes the celebrated Rabbi and great Kabbalist Moses, son of Nochman, known also by the name of Ramban) a great and useful remedy, **Leraue, Weacuo, Nirch**, which signifies that a man (especially on a journey) may see everything while himself remaining invisible. It is said that, by this mysterious means, a man may see and know all the enemies who lie in wait for him or hide themselves, the persecutors, thieves, robbers and murderers, without being seen by them, and that he may remain invisible to them. This means has been tried and proven, says the author: “and I have tried it myself on occasions and in places where my life and limbs were in danger, and at every trial, with the help of God, I have escaped from it unharmed. It is in truth a blessed remedy! You must know, however, that the power of this means lasts only from the morning until after vespers (the evening prayer).”

THE REMEDY IS:

After having offered to God your fervent travel prayer, early in the morning, before beginning your journey, leave your lodging or your dwelling, and when you come to the highway by which you intend to travel, stop and cover your eyes with the fingers of the right hand in the following manner: lay the forefinger upon the right eye, the middle finger upon the nose and the ring finger upon the left eye, then say three times: By the word of the almighty God. Then say also the following passages of the holy Scriptures — Genesis, chap. i, verse 1: “In the beginning God created the heaven and the earth.” Deuteronomy, chap. xxxiv, last verse: “And in all that mighty hand, and in all the great terror which Moses shewed in the sight of all Israel.” Genesis, chap. xxxii, 1, 2: “And Jacob went on his way, and the angels of God met him. And when Jacob saw them, he said: This is God’s host; and he called the name of that place Mahanaim,” that is to say, two camps or hosts, namely that of God and his own. Deuteronomy, chap. xxxii, 1, 2: “Give ear, O ye heavens, and I will speak; and hear, O earth, the words of my mouth. My doctrine shall drop as the rain, my speech shall distil as the dew, as the small rain upon the tender herb, and as the showers upon the grass.” Numbers, chap. x, verse 35: “And it came to pass, when the ark set forward, that Moses said: Rise up, Lord, and let thine enemies be scattered, and let them that hate thee flee before thee. And when it rested, he said: Return, O Lord, unto the many thousands of Israel.” Numbers, chap. ii, verse 6: “And his host, and those that were numbered thereof, were fifty and four thousand and four hundred. All that were numbered of the camp of Ephraim were an hundred thousand and eight thousand and an hundred, throughout their armies, and they shall go forward in the third rank.” Here follow several isolated verses of the Psalms, namely — Psalm 46, 8: “The God of hosts is with us; the God of Jacob is our refuge.” “Lord of hosts! blessed is the man that trusteth in thee.” “Save, Lord! the King will hear us when we call upon him.” “Jehovah will give strength unto his people, the Lord will bless his people with peace.” Psalm 19, 14: “Let the words of my mouth and the meditation of my heart be acceptable in thy sight, O Lord, my strength and my redeemer.” And now, remove your fingers from your eyes, and repeat with devotion the following Psalms — the 121st Psalm, the 67th Psalm and the 91st Psalm — joined to the last verse of the 90th Psalm, and repeat twice the last verse of the 91st Psalm. If you have strictly conformed to all these instructions, you are under the mighty protection of God, and you may go forward in safety and without fear, for no harm will befall you, and neither robbers nor murderers will attack you.
### IV.

Here is yet another mysterious means of protection for travellers, furnished by the same author, but in a different manner. If you see, says Ramban, in a dangerous place (for example in a forest or a desert), heathens, robbers or hostile troops approaching you, stop suddenly, draw a circle around yourself, and repeat, from Exodus, chap. xi, verse 8: “And all these thy servants shall come down unto me, and bow down themselves unto me, saying, Get thee out, and all the people that follow thee: and after that I will go out.”

## Astrological Influence upon Man and Magical Cures of the Ancient Hebrews

*[After the work of Dr. Gideon Brecher: “Transcendental Magic and the Magical Art of Healing in the Talmud.” Vienna, 1850.]*

### Astrological Influences

That the Cosmos constitutes an organic whole, whose separate parts act upon one another and in opposition to one another, was well known to the ancients. Man, the highest individuality of the earth — which served the ancients as the centre of the universe —, reflects in his being, as microcosm, the macrocosm. The Book of Jezira (a division of the Talmud) accordingly sets in parallel three factors of the Cosmos, namely the earth, time and man, corresponding to the cardinal numbers and to the elements of words (the letters). It is there said: Three principal letters, Aleph, Mem, Schin, correspond, in the earth, to air, water and fire; in man, to the breast, the belly and the head; in the year, to mild temperature, cold and heat. The seven compound letters, Beth, Gimel, Daleth, Khaf, Pe, Resch and Tau, correspond, in the earth, to Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, the Sun, Venus, Mercury and the Moon; in man, to wisdom, wealth, dominion, life, amativeness, the blessing of children and peace; in the year, to Saturday, Thursday, Tuesday, Sunday, Friday, Wednesday and Monday. The twelve remaining simple letters correspond, in the world, to Aries, Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius, Pisces; in man, to the organs of sight, hearing, smell, speech, taste, copulation, to the faculty of setting things in motion (the hands), of feeling anger, of laughing and of sleeping; and in the year, to the twelve months. The Tali, with the boundary of the earth, continues the Book of Jezira, is like a king upon his throne. The sphere of the years is like a king in the country; the heart of man is like a king in battle.

The Tali, the ecliptic and the heart are the points of concentration and of digression of the *primum movens* of the macrocosm and of the microcosm. The Tali, already mentioned, is declared by all the ancients, and among others by Rabbi Jehuda Halewi, to be the Dragon known in astronomy, which the Arabs call Bashar. (It is supposed that this refers to the eccentric orbit of the moon in relation to the orbit of the sun.) The Gymnosophists called the distance of the moon from the point of its intersection with the ecliptic Patona Chandera: “the offended dragon,” which nevertheless seems impossible, for the Book of Jezira gives the Tali as the *primum movens* of the whole universe. It would be more reasonable to understand by it a supposed zone outside the zodiac, by reason of which it appears as a king upon a throne, who holds in his hands all the threads of the entire kingdom. Consequently, the Tali can be nothing other than the world-serpent Beschat (Adischen Wasughl) of the ancient Hindus, which served them as a symbol of divine protection and of eternity.

We have much less to do with the question of how man, as microcosm, by his spiritual powers, which he can set in action by his will and by the mighty word, operates upon the entire universe, than with the physical, and above all magical, influence of the entire universe upon man as a part of nature.

Rabbi Jehuda Halewi already draws attention to this wonderful phenomenon: that even the elements and the powers of nature which contribute to the maintenance of the human organism — which man receives into himself, transforms and assimilates in order to reproduce the worn-out particles of his body, and by which he preserves his bodily integrity —, that these same elements, after a certain cycle of years, will in their turn exert a hostile and destructive influence upon the human organization, until man dissolves by an individual death and returns to the elements and to the chemistry of the general matter of nature. Admitting this constant struggle of man with surrounding nature, the Book of Jezira compares, as has already been said, the heart, the source of life, to a king in battle, who at the beginning dominates and subdues his enemies, but who nevertheless succumbs at last in the struggle.

That the entire universe, the sun, the moon and the rest of the planets and heavenly bodies exert a certain influence upon the earth, and consequently upon man who forms part of it, and thus produce numerous and diverse effects, was no rare conclusion among the ancients, for they were led to it by visions and evident appearances, and they were compelled to discover the sidereal influences by attentive observation. The moon, especially, with its periods of twenty-seven and twenty-eight days, made its influence upon man and upon his condition felt in an unmistakable manner. Among these effects may be ranked menstruation in women, the different phases of worm diseases according to the different phases of the moon, the increase and diminution of certain cutaneous diseases, etc., according to these phases; the influence of the moon upon certain bodily diseases. Beil mentions that the influence of the moon as a cause of dysentery has been recognized by a number of eminent physicians, who, it is said, have observed that attacks of dysentery are more frequent at the new moon and at the full moon than at any other period. Others declare that the plague is much more severe at the time of the new moon. The Brahmins also warn against the new moon. Kant says: “It is a powerful argument in favour of the hypothesis that the moon influences diseases, that the deaths by fever in Bengal multiplied greatly during an eclipse of the sun, etc., because the attraction of the moon unites at that time with that of the sun, whose power of acting upon terrestrial bodies has been established beyond all dispute.” Finally, to this chapter belong the critical days, and the power of attraction of the moon upon night travellers.

The influence of the light of the sun and of the moon upon mankind must have been evident from the remotest period, and it was soon admitted that it powerfully affected the well-being of man as well as his disposition of mind, together with the manifestations of the various temperaments. This knowledge was extended by the observation of local diseases during the different seasons of the year and the changes of weather, by the beneficial effect of the rays of the sun upon painful and diseased parts of the body, and by the influence of the day upon births and deaths. Recognizing the harmful effects of the sun and of the moon, the Psalmist says: “The sun shall not smite thee by day, nor the moon by night.” The Talmud attributes curative powers to the sun. Abaii says: the prophet speaks of the sun that heals, for the rays of the sun possess curative virtues. The Talmud says: “God hung a precious stone about the neck of Abraham; all who were sick and beheld it were healed.” But after the death of Abraham, God took the precious stone and hung it in the sphere of the sun.

But the ancients also recognized a physical influence of the rest of the planets and of the whole host of stars upon man. The belief that the heavenly bodies were endowed with life and that, invested with superior powers, they could exert an influence upon man by means of mysterious magical influences, regulate his temperament, his dispositions and the duration of his life, was widespread from the Ganges to the Tiber and as far as the Pillars of Hercules. The Talmud likewise teaches this astrological faith, with this difference, that the Israelite is placed under the immediate guidance of God, and that, notwithstanding the iron astrological necessity and predestination, it concedes the possibility that, through the omnipotence of God, good may be accomplished in spite of the constellations. This is expressed in the Talmud by the words: “The Israelite has no constellation.” Rabbi Jochanan victoriously establishes this fact against Rabbi Chanina, who affirms that “wisdom and wealth are the gifts of the constellations”; for, says Rabbi Jochanan, it is written in Jeremiah 10, 2: “Be not dismayed at the signs of heaven; for the heathen are dismayed at them.” Rab says: Abraham spoke before God: Lord of the earth! shall my servant be my heir? God said: No, thy son shall be thy heir. Abraham answered: My horoscope has shown me that I shall be childless. Then God spoke and said: Turn away from thy astrology, the Israelite is not subject to the constellations.

On the writing-table of Rabbi Jehoschua, says the Talmud, there was found written: “Whoever is born on a Sunday shall be distinguished above others.” Whereupon Rabbi Asche said: Dime bar Kakustha and I were both born on a Sunday; I became the head of a school, and he became a captain of robbers. Light and darkness were created on the day of the Sabbath. He who is born on a Monday will always be a passionate man, for on that day the waters were divided. Whoever is born on a Tuesday will become rich and given to pleasures; on that day all the herbs were created. Whoever is born on a Wednesday will be wise and happy: on that day the stars of the firmament were created. He who is born on a Thursday will promptly devote himself to works of love. Whoever is born on a Friday will become active in good works. Whoever is born on the day of the Sabbath will also die on the day of the Sabbath, because the Sabbath will have been profaned on his account. Raba, the Rabbi Schila, adds: He shall be called holy. Chanina said to his pupils: Go to the son of Levi, and tell him: It is not the constellation of the day that has an influence, but the stars of the hour of birth. He who is born under the influence of the sun is endowed with beauty, eats and drinks of his own possessions, but his secrets will become known; if he becomes a thief, he will be unlucky. If anyone is born under the influence of Venus, he will become rich and voluptuous, because the (fire) of love is born in him. He who is born under the influence of Mercury will become enlightened and prudent, because Mercury is the secretary of the sun. He who is born under the influence of the moon will learn to bear trials. He builds and moves in, moves in and builds, eats and drinks of the possessions of the stranger; his secrets remain hidden, and as a thief he will be fortunate. He who is born under Mercury *[sic — Mercury appears twice; the Talmud (Shabbat 156a) attributes this to Saturn]* will see all his plans perverted and thwarted; but the plans of others against him will likewise be thwarted. Whoever is born under the influence of Jupiter will become a just man. He who is born under the influence of Mars will become a shedder of blood. Rabbi Asche affirms it: he will become either a robber, or a butcher. Abam adds: or a judge.

We observe that the Talmud regards the sidereal nature of man as a hypostatic being in itself. This siderealism, pre-existing in the constellation of man, it names Masol. The most distinguished persons were their own star, and thus, according to the Talmud, the star of the commander-in-chief Sisra was called Meroz (Judges 5, 23). The siderealism of the proselytes was present at Sinai.

Upon the constellation, says Raba, depend the blessing of children, longevity, success in life. Job cursed his constellations. Since the destiny of man is fixed beforehand by the constellation, no harm can reach him, and no accidental death can fall upon him. If therefore a man meets serpents or scorpions that attack him, and if he destroys them, let him be assured that they were predestined to be destroyed by him, though he may regard the act as a miracle.

Beings who are born under the same constellation stand in close relation and in magical sympathy with one another. The Talmud even raises, from these premises, the problem of whether such persons have the same marks, such as moles, for example.

The conjunction of the planets, the eclipse of the sun and of the moon exert a great influence upon plants and upon man. Samuel teaches that, when the spring equinox falls in the hour of Jupiter, the strength of the fruit tree will be broken. If the winter solstice falls in the hour of Jupiter, then the grain will wither; but for this it is necessary that the new moon also fall in the hour of Jupiter or of the moon. The first hour of Saturday evening (between six and seven o’clock) is that of Mercury. The second is that of the Moon. The third, that of Saturn. The fourth, that of Jupiter. The fifth, that of Mars. The sixth, that of the Sun. The seventh, that of Venus. The eighth hour, again, is that of Mercury, etc.

The Rabbis teach that an eclipse of the sun is an evil sign for all peoples. It is as if a king prepared a feast for his subjects and set before them a brilliant light; if he becomes angry with his subjects, however, he commands his servants to remove the light, so that they may be in darkness. Rabbi Meir taught that the eclipses of the heavenly bodies were an evil sign, for the Israelites especially, because they were accustomed to misfortune. If the master enters the school with a rod in his hand, who is afraid? only he who receives punishment every day. The Rabbis teach that an eclipse of the sun is an evil sign also for the rest of the nations; an eclipse of the moon, for the Israelites, for they reckon their time by the moon, and the other nations by the sun. If the eclipse is in the East, it is an evil omen for the inhabitants of the East; if it is in the western sky, it is an evil omen for the inhabitants of the West; if it is in the middle of the firmament, it is an evil sign for all the inhabitants of the earth. When the sun appears blood-red, it presages war. If the sun has a greyish appearance, it is a sign of famine. When an eclipse occurs at sunset, it is a sign that the Judgment is still far off; if it is at sunrise, it is the omen of the near coming of the Judgment. According to others, these omens signify the reverse of what has just been said. A general calamity never befalls a people without its heavenly prince (protecting spirit) suffering defeat with it. If, however, Israel walks according to the will of God, it will have nothing of this kind to fear.

The Rabbis further teach: on account of sin, the sun will be darkened when the presiding judge dies and is not properly mourned; when a betrothed young woman, in a city, cries in vain for help when violence is done to her; and on account of the simultaneous murder of two brothers. The other heavenly luminaries will be darkened on account of the making of counterfeit money; on account of false witness; when the property of a stranger is seized by force, or when fruit trees are cut down without necessity.

To this belong the *dies nefasti*, concerning which we give the following passages from the Talmud:

Samuel teaches that the time propitious for blessing is Sunday, Wednesday and Friday. No man should let himself be bled on Monday or Thursday, for Mars says: Only he who can enjoy the savings of his parents should have himself bled on Monday and Thursday, because on these two days the earthly and heavenly tribunals sit. On Tuesday one should not have oneself bled, because on that day Mars governs the hour. Raschi explains it in the following manner: Mars has the oversight of wars, of plague and of all kinds of accidents. The even (exact) number is likewise an ill omen, for with it the demons have power (Pessach 110), and accidents are imminent. The order of the planets is: Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon. The order of the hours during which they govern begins with the creation of the heavenly luminaries, namely at the first hour of Wednesday (with us, between six and seven o’clock). In this hour Saturn governs, in the second Jupiter, in the third Mars, etc. — thus seven planets in seven hours. This cycle continues for ever. According to this order, we find that the planets govern, at the first hour of each night, in the following order: Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, Saturn, Sun, Moon, Mars — namely: at the first hour of the close of Sunday, Mercury; at the first hour of the night of Monday, Jupiter, etc. According to this order, the first hours of the days of the week are then governed by the Sun, the Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, Saturn. The first hour of Sunday morning is governed by the sun; the first hour of Monday by the moon, and Tuesday morning by Mars. Mars will govern again on the same day at the eighth hour, a direct hour. On the other days, its reign will not fall upon a direct hour, but only during the night, when it is not customary to have oneself bled. On the remark of the Talmud that Mars governs at a direct hour on Friday, Raschi says: at the first hour of Friday, Venus governs. This planet is followed, in regular order, by Mercury, the Moon, Saturn, Jupiter and Mars. The latter also governs at the sixth hour; whereupon comes the answer that on Saturday many people are in the habit of having themselves bled, and that “God preserves the simple.”

Samuel further teaches that he who is the fourth, the fourteenth or the twenty-fourth cannot be bled without danger, that is to say, a Wednesday falling on the fourth, the fourteenth or the twenty-fourth of the month. Likewise a fourth after which there is no further four, that is to say, a Wednesday after which four days will not elapse before the new moon. At the new moon, and three days after, bleeding is debilitating; on the third day after the new moon, it is notoriously dangerous.

### Of Magical Remedies in General

Since, in the ancient world, diseases were regarded as the work of angry gods and malevolent demons, there were then, as in our own day among nations in the state of nature, few remedies that were not magical, employed to secure the assistance of the demons or to render them harmless.

Sanchuniaton declares that the children of the Kabiri could heal the venomous bite by their songs. The Brahmins regarded diseases as a punishment for sins committed in the present life or in a former life. The cures consisted in expiation by prayer, alms, exorcisms, but also in rational means. The Vedas contain hymns and prayers to which magical powers are attributed, and it is in this capacity that they are employed, without being understood, as formulas of exorcism. All diseases, says the Brahmin, are the work of evil demons and can be cured only by purification, by means of sorcery. Ormuzd awakened the great prophet Hom, from whom all magicians derive their origin. He healed all diseases by the word of Ormuzd and by a plant which bears his name. Many cures, it is said in the ten books, are effected by means of trees and herbs, others by the office of the knife, others by the word, but perfect cures result only from the divine Word. Even though the ancient Greeks healed by natural means, the common people nevertheless believed that a prompt convalescence was obtained by magical formulas, songs and incantations. The medical heroes of the primitive Greek world were at the same time poets, legislators, generals and astronomers.

Of the cures effected by spiritual men and prophets we have already spoken. The prophets were unanimous in applying, together with natural means, prayer and blessing. Rabbi Jitzchak said: Four things destroy the evil passions of man, namely: alms, prayer, change of name and repentance. According to other authors, also change of place. Josephus writes, concerning King Chiskia: God gave him the power, by fervent adjurations, to reconcile the Divinity and to drive out the evil spirits which are the cause of evil diseases; and this method of healing, continues Josephus, is that which prevails among us at the present time. Josephus adds: I was an eye-witness of a cure which the prophet Eleazar effected, in the presence of the Emperor Vespasian, upon a man possessed by demons. He made use of a root recommended by King Solomon as efficacious against demoniac disease: the root was introduced into the nose of the patient, while he pronounced at the same time the name of Solomon and the magical formula which that king had taught. The Gnostics followed in this the example of the prophets. The theosophico-medical sect of the Escucans sought the power of roots, herbs and stones for purposes of healing, and strove at the same time to cure diseases by leading a pious life and by a total surrender of themselves in the idea of God, and by this means they also sought to perform wonders. The substantial Word of God, which, as a mark of preference, is called the Angel, the Monad, the World of Light, the Bread of Life, the First-Born of all creatures, the Physician of all diseases, dwells in the Epoptae and makes them partakers of the divine nature, so that they can heal diseases and perform wonders of every kind. The magical Logos, which draws its analogy or its origin from the exalted and holy word Oum of the Brahmins, we find again in the Book of Jezira, where we read (1, 8): “One, the Spirit of the living God, voice and Spirit and Word.” “And this is the Holy Spirit.” The divine number, the divine Word, the divine Scriptures, says Rabbi Jehuda Halewi in his commentary on the Book of Jezira, are one and the same thing with reality. What God thinks and wills has at the same time reality. Man alone makes a distinction between will and act; in God, these two things are identical. Rabbi Jehuda Halewi continues: “We may form some conception of this when, in thinking of a name and pronouncing it, we form at the same time in our mind an image of the object named. Among languages and scriptures this peculiarity may be remarked, that some specify the existence of things in particular, while others do so in a general manner. The holy language, however, by which God called the universe into being, demonstrates the primary existence of all things with the greatest precision. Whoever, consequently, knows the original meaning of the Scriptures and grasps with a pure thought the existence of the thing intended, that man can, in conformity with his thoughts and his will, will things into existence.” A thorough knowledge of the holy word and of the transposition of its letters can, as the Talmud says, render man capable of performing wonders and even of creating things. Rabbi Jonathan says: “The time will come when the pious shall raise the dead.” Raba says: “If the pious desire it, they can create a world.” It is related of this Rabbi that he created a man, and of the Rabbis Chanina and Oschaja, that they occupied themselves every Friday with the Book of Jezira, and that they created a fatted calf which they consumed. Gechasie made the golden image of the bull speak by impressing a “name” upon its lips. Solomon gave Benajah ben Jehojada a chain and a seal, upon each of which he had engraved a “name,” to bind the king of the demons Asmedi. Of Bezalel, the builder of the tabernacle of the temple in the wilderness, it is said: “He knew the transposition of the letters by which heaven and earth were created.” Abischai pronounced a holy name, and thereby suspended David between heaven and earth.

The word which expresses the being of the prototypes and the idea of the intelligence can operate, through them, along the whole chain of beings down to the most insignificant, causing changes, overthrows and new creations, and it can also overcome the demons.

“Rabbi Chanina bar Papi was requested by a matron, who had become enamoured of him, to satisfy her amorous desires. He pronounced something, and his body became covered with scabs and abscesses. She did something in her turn, and he became sound again; then he fled and hid himself,” etc.

But since there exist holy words, whose literal ideas and transpositions are innumerable modifications of existence, there exist also unholy words, which give the prototypes of the dark side of the world of spirits. And if the profane use of the holy name is forbidden on pain of losing future felicity, the use of the unholy names, which Abraham transmitted to the children of his concubines, is the most terrible, because they lead to the sins of idolatry, by which man acquires evil principles and becomes possessed by demons.

The Talmud, in general, forbids sorcery, heathen superstition and heathen usages. To these belong: the conjuration of wounds and injuries, for which, in spite of the exorcism, use was made of a verse of the Bible; the conjuration of serpents and scorpions. The following usages, in particular, were declared heathen: 1. To hang the egg of a grasshopper on the ear, to cure earache. 2. To wear the tooth of a fox to promote sleep: the tooth of a dead fox was employed against somnolence, and the tooth of a living fox induced sleep. 3. The wearing of a splinter from a gallows, to cure a wound caused by iron. 4. The proverb: “Good fortune! and grow not weary day or night,” probably a formula for obtaining luck. “Gad” signifies luck, and it is the name of the idol Belus (Isaiah 65, 11). 5. To obtain luck, the man takes the name of the woman, and the woman the name of the man. 6. The formula: “Be strong, O my casks!” to increase the blessing of the wine — Dan is the name of an idol (Amos 8, 14). 7. To have luck, one cries to the male raven: “Cry!” and to the female raven: “Croak! and turn thy tail toward me for luck.” 8. If anyone says: “Kill this cock, for it crows late in the evening; or kill this hen, for it crows like a cock.” 9. “I drink and I leave some; I drink and I leave some” (so that the wine may increase). 10. Whoever throws the egg-shells against the wall (those from which the young chicks have already come out, so that they may not die). 11. He who stirs in a pot before the young chicks, for the same purpose. 12. Whoever, for the same purpose, dances before the chicks and counts seventy-one chicks. 13. To dance during the preparation of a meal. To command silence during the cooking of lentils. To utter cries during the cooking of grain. To urinate before the pot to facilitate the cooking.

Rabbi Acha says: Before bleeding, we must repeat the following prayer: May it be thy will, O Lord! that this curative operation hasten my convalescence, etc.; for no one should seek the assistance of the physician, but should expect the return of health from God alone. To which Abaii objects, because Rabbi Jischmael teaches, and because he deduces it from the Thora (Exodus 21, 19): The physician shall have an unconditional right to heal. The Talmud not only adopts this view of Abaii, but it forbids dwelling in a place where no physician is to be found. If, on the other hand, we read in the Talmud that King Chiskia had the Book of Cures destroyed, and that for this act he was praised by the Rabbis, then this book, as Maimonides supposes, contained merely superstitious, astrological and idolatrous remedies, which the pious king sought to consign to oblivion, but a great part of which found its way into the hands of the people, and also became, in time, a common possession for the more enlightened.

### Of Magical Cures in Particular

### 1. — Healing by Amulets

The amulet Kamea was employed as a remedy against diseases and especially against witchcraft. This amulet was either a written parchment, properly called Pitka, or a small bundle of plants. The words written upon the amulet were of various character; among their contents, however, were found verses of the Bible. That amulet is called a proven amulet by which cures have already been wrought three times, whether it was written or consisted of roots. With regard to the Sabbath (the question of whether it might be worn on that day or not), it must always be regarded as safe, provided it was made and completed by one skilled in such matters. An amulet may prove effective in healing a human being while remaining without effect in the case of an animal, because in man the constellation operates in conjunction with the amulet. In order to be effective, it must relate to the name and to the number of the demon. Thus, for example, the demons of the caper-bush are called spirits; those of the service-tree, Schedim; in gardens, Rispe. At the caper-bush no fewer than sixty Schedim gather, and the amulet must consequently relate to that number.

A cord made of the seeds of the madder-plant was hung upon children as a protection against certain diseases. The nurse of Abbaii taught him: Three seeds will arrest the disease, five will cure it, and seven will be effective against witchcraft. Rabbi Acha bar Jacob remarks: This last thing is true only when the bewitched person is not exposed to the light of the sun or of the moon, nor to rain, and when he hears neither the clink of iron, nor the crowing of the cock, nor the sound of footsteps. Rabbi Nachman bar Jitzchak says that this remedy was already forgotten in his time.

### 2. — Healing by Charms

The nurse of Abbaii taught him: “Every numbering (naming?) must be done in the name of the mother (N., son of R.), and all external passes must be made on the left side of the body.” Abbaii further says: If the number is fixed, it must be strictly adhered to, without departing from it; if the number is not fixed, it is forty-one.

As customs that are not heathen, the four following things are permitted: 1. To put into the pot a splinter of mulberry-wood or fragments of glass, so that the dish may boil more quickly. (The Rabbis, however, forbade the fragments of glass, on account of the danger of swallowing them with the food.) 2. The toast: “Wine and life rejoice the Rabbis!” Rabbi Akiba repeated this toast at every cup of wine served at the wedding feast of his son. Against drunkenness: anoint the palms of the hands and the soles of the feet of the drunken person with a mixture of oil and salt, and say: As this ointment becomes pure, so may the senses become clear; or else, place the bung of a cask in water and say: As the bung becomes clean, etc.

Charm against suppuration: say, for example: Bas, Basia, Mas, Masia, Kas, Kasia, Scharial and Amarlia, the angels who came forth from the land of Sodom to heal painful boils. Let the color grow no redder; let it spread no further; let its seed be absorbed into the bowels. And as the mule does not propagate its kind, so may this evil not propagate itself in the body of N., son of R.

Charm against the smallpox: for example: Drawn sword and bent sling. His name is not Jacob, the pain rages.

Against possession, as follows: Cursed, crushed and banished be the demon, by the name of Bar Tet, Bor Tama, etc.

Charm against injuries inflicted by the demons of privies: On the head of the lion and in the nose of the lioness, I found the demon Bar Schirka Panda. I threw him into a fish-pit and killed him with the jawbone of an ass.

To turn bad dreams to good, three men would recite certain consoling verses of the Bible. “Whoever has had a bad dream must hasten in the morning, as soon as he wakes, to repeat a verse of the Bible whose content is favorable to him, and this must be done quickly, before a verse of unfavorable meaning for him comes to his mind.”

He who is about to enter a city and fears the evil look, let him put his right thumb into his left hand and his left thumb into his right hand, and let him say: I, N. N., am of the lineage of Joseph, over whom the evil eye can have no power. He renders the look of the evil eye harmless. The tail of a fox was hung upon the horse.

### 3. — Remedies and Charms against Witchcraft

Ben Asai says: Do not irritate your anus while seated in order to obtain an evacuation of the bowels, for he who does this will be struck by witchcraft, even were it practiced in Spain. If a man, however, has done so inadvertently, let him pronounce after the evacuation the following charm:

“You cannot approach, neither Tachim nor Tachtum, nor all of you, nor any one of you, neither the enchantment of the sorcerer nor the charm of the witch.”

Rabbi Chanina says: To drink an eighth of an ounce of the milk of a she-ass forty days old is effective against the sting of a wasp; a quarter against the sting of a scorpion; half a measure against witchcraft and against the drinking of liquids left open and exposed.

Rabbi Jochanan said: A mistress among the witches told me: If you meet witches, you must pronounce the following charm: “Hot mud, in pierced baskets, into your mouths, enchantress women! May your heads become bald; may the wind carry away your breadcrumbs; may it scatter your spices; may the fresh saffron you hold in your hands fly away. Witches! so long as men were favorable to me and I was prudent, I did not come into your midst; now I have done so, and you are not favorable to me.”

The Rabbi taught: There are three things between which we must not pass, and which, likewise, two persons walking side by side must not let pass between them. These three things are: a dog, a palm-tree, and a woman. Some also add a pig, and others, a serpent. If it nevertheless happens, what must a man do to escape unharmed? Rabbi Papi says: “Repeat a verse of the Bible which begins and ends with the same letter.”

If women are sitting facing each other on either side of the street, there is no doubt that they are engaged in witchcraft. How can a man escape them without harm? He must avoid them and take another road. If that is not possible, then let him take someone by the hand and pass in his company. If there is no one at hand, he must say: “The demons of witchcraft have already been slain by arrows. Arat, Assia, Belussia are the names of the demons. (Raschi.)”

The Rabbis teach: A man must not drink water during the night, neither from a stream nor from standing water. Whoever does so, let his blood fall upon his own head, for in doing so he exposes himself to becoming blind. How can drinking at night be made safe? If there is someone present, he must be awakened and told: “I am drinking water!” If you are alone, make a noise with the lid of the cup and say to yourself: “N., son of R., your mother said to you: Beware of Schabriri, briri, riri, iri, ri, in a white pitcher (Raschi) — this is a magical charm.” As the syllables of the word Schabriri diminish, the demon withdraws. The Targum renders blindness (Genesis 19, 11) by Schabriri.

Concerning even numbers, of which we have already spoken, Rabbi Papa says: “Joseph, the Demon, told me that by two we kill; by four we inflict only wounds. In the number two, evil will follow, whether we did the thing in that number intentionally or accidentally; in the number four, only when the thing is done intentionally. If we have accidentally taken a meal or drunk from a plate or a pitcher in an even number, how shall we avoid the fatal consequences? We must take the thumb of the right hand in the left, and the thumb of the left in the right hand, and say: You and I make three. If the answer is: You and I make five, — and if we again hear the answer: You and I are six, we must answer again: You and I are seven. It happened that someone continued in this way up to the 101st time, until at last the demon burst with vexation.”

### 4. — Application of Charms and Remedies

We insert here the passages of the Talmud which treat of this subject, on account of their coherence, in their whole extent, although this may perhaps not properly belong in this place, because in several cases, in the remedies that follow, natural cures as well as magical charms are prescribed, and because these passages of the Talmud otherwise offer much interesting matter. They treat of the procedures against serpents and of other remarkable occurrences.

He who has swallowed a serpent, let him eat ivy and then walk three miles. Rabbi Sime bar Asche saw a man who had swallowed a serpent. Rabbi Sime fell upon him like a warrior, gave him ivy and salt to eat, then drove him before him for three miles, and the serpent came out of him in pieces. Others report that it was Rabbi Sime himself who swallowed the serpent, and that the prophet Elijah came in the form of a fierce warrior, gave him ivy and salt to eat, etc., etc.

If a man is bitten by a serpent, let him take seventy-nine hairs from the tail of a white she-ass, let him bind them with a silken thread, and let the bitten man sit upon them. The she-ass, however, must have been free from Trefa (an organic disease). An official of Pumbeditha was bitten by a serpent. Now there were at Pumbeditha thirteen white she-asses with foal, which were opened one after the other, but all were found to be afflicted with Trefa. It was also known that in a distant quarter of the city there was another she-ass, and messengers were sent to bring her. But in the meantime a lion had devoured the she-ass. Then Abaii said: Perhaps the serpent of the Rabbis has bitten him, against whose bite there exists no remedy. The pupils answered: Yes, it is so, master; for when Rab died, Rabbi Jitzchak bar Bina forbade the use of myrtle and palm leaves for a whole year. Now, this official had paid no heed to this prohibition.

If a serpent coils itself around a person, he must go into the water and take a basket which he shall hold above the head of the serpent, then lower the basket in such a manner that its rim comes to rest between him and the serpent (to seize the serpent with the hands would be dangerous, because it might become enraged and injure the person). When the serpent is in the basket, throw it quickly into the water and hasten away.

If someone is pursued by a furious serpent (when the serpent follows the person by scenting his tracks), he must have himself carried a short distance by another person, if one is present (by the stranger's steps, the serpent will lose its trail); if there is no one with him, he must jump over a body of water or sit above a well. The following night, he must place himself upon four casks, so that the serpent cannot reach him easily, or rather sleep in the open air, so that it cannot come to him by the roof; then let him tie four cats to the casks and make a noise with straw or brushwood, so that the cats, when the serpent comes, may throw themselves upon it and devour it.

If someone is pursued by a serpent, let him seek refuge between two beehives.

Rabbi Jochanan says: The drink of roots described in the Mischna consists of Alexandrian gum, powdered alum and fine saffron, each of the weight of a Sus (a small coin). To cure jaundice, two of these are taken in beer.

The following remedies are likewise recommended in cases of female weakness: Take the heads of three Persian onions, boil them in wine and give the woman to drink of it, saying to her at the same time: Rise up from your sickness!

Another remedy: Take a handful of cumin, a handful of saffron and an equal quantity of fenugreek, boil them together in wine, and give the woman to drink of it, saying: Rise up from your sickness!

Another remedy: Take sixty bungs of wine-casks, soak them in water, and give this water to the woman to drink, saying to her at the same time: Rise up from your sickness!

Or else, take a quantity of young vine-shoots, boil them in wine, give the woman to drink of it, and speak to her as above.

Or else, take the plant that grows beside the thorn, and burn it to ashes. The woman must carry these ashes upon her, in a linen cloth in summer, or in a woolen cloth in winter.

Or else, dig seven holes in the earth, and burn in each of them young vine-shoots of less than three years. Let the woman take a cup in her hands and sit over the first hole and drink; then let her rise and sit over the second hole, etc., etc. Each time, say to her: Rise up, etc.

Or else, anoint the lower part of her body with rose-water.

Or else, take a crown of feathers, burn them to ashes. The sick woman must carry these ashes in a linen bag in summer, and in a woolen bag in winter.

Or else, open a cask of wine expressly for her. She must drink much wine.

Or else, take a grain of barley found in the stable of a white she-ass. If she holds it one day in her hand (the right hand), the malady will cease for two days. If she holds it two days, it will cease for three days. If she holds it three days, it will cease forever. According to others: if she takes one grain in her hand, etc.; if she takes two grains in her hand, etc.

### 5. — Magical Remedies against Various Diseases and Afflictions

**Abortion (Abortus).** — The aetites (eagle-stone), the stone of the rattlesnake — the stone of retention, of preservation — was worn by women as a preservative against abortion.

**Bloodletting.** — If you have eaten fish, you must not be bled the next day. If you have been bled, you must not eat fish the next day. On the third day, both would be dangerous.

The Rabbis teach: After bloodletting, a man must not, on the same day, drink milk, eat cheese, onions or vegetables. If a man, however, has eaten of these things, he must mix, says Abaii, a quarter of a measure of vinegar and a quarter of a measure of wine, and drink it. If a movement of the bowels follows, the evacuation must take place outside the city and on its eastern side, for the exhalation from it is harmful.

**Diseases of the Eyes.** — The saliva of a first-born on the father's side (not on the mother's side) is effective for the healing of diseases of the eyes.

Rab says: A degenerate eye is dangerous to life, for the visual power of the eye is closely bound to the pericardium.

Rapid journeys weaken the sight; but it may be restored by pronouncing the blessing of the consecration of the Sabbath, on Friday evening.

**Blindness.** — Against night-blindness, take a cord made of the hair of an animal; tie one end of it to the foot of the sick person, and the other end to the foot of a dog, then let the children make a noise behind the dog, and say: “Hold the dog, deceive the rock.” Then take seven pieces of meat from seven different houses, and let the inhabitants of each house hang a piece of the meat on the door-post of the sick person. The dog must eat this meat in an open place. Then let the sick person untie the cord of hair, and let someone say: Night-blindness of N., son of R., leave N., son of R., and pierce the pupil of the dog.

**Against Day-Blindness.** — Take seven pieces of spleen from seven heifers, and lay them upon the vessel which the physician uses to collect the blood when he bleeds a patient. The patient must be seated in a chamber. A man must stand posted outside, who must say: “Blind man! give me, that I may eat.” The patient answers: “How can I open? Come in and eat!” After eating, he must break the dish, otherwise he will himself become blind.
